** Purpose

This file records concrete environment and packaging findings relevant to the
transition from:

- Python 2 to Python 3
- old `dolfin` packaging to a reproducible Python 3 runtime for the same
  DOLFIN generation

The emphasis here is on exact versions, package sources, and constraints that
were observed directly during probing.

** Strategic Direction

The transition is now treated as four separate milestones, not one continuous
upgrade:

1. Python 3 usability on the current legacy =dolfin 2017.1.0= transition
   stack;
2. a user-facing installation path outside the specialist container workflow,
   preferably via =pixi=;
3. migration to legacy FEniCS/=dolfin 2019.1.0=;
4. a later, reduced-scope =dolfinx= port.

This matters because legacy FEniCS and =dolfinx= are different targets with
different risks. The last legacy FEniCS release is =2019.1.0=; =dolfinx= is a
separate actively developed stack and should be handled as a redesign/porting
track rather than a routine package upgrade.

Guiding rule:

- do not spend time reviving old features unless that work directly helps one
  of the four milestones above.

In practice this means:

- keeping historical xfails and niche comparison paths deferred when they do
  not block Python 3, =pixi=, =dolfin 2019.1.0=, or =dolfinx=;
- preferring a small stable workflow matrix over broad historical parity;
- using the current Python 3 core gate as the main M1 regression baseline.

** M2 Dependency Inventory For `pixi`

This section records the first packaging-oriented dependency inventory for the
`pixi` track.

*** Important structural finding

The repository does not currently contain a modern top-level Python packaging
file such as:

- `pyproject.toml`
- `setup.py`
- `setup.cfg`
- `pixi.toml`

The only `setup.py` found in the tree is:

- `dev/setup.py`

and that file is only a historical helper for installing an already-prepared
tree from `/tmp/finmag/`. So M2 is not only a dependency-translation task; it
also needs an explicit packaging decision for Finmag itself.

*** Current Python 3 transition image inputs

The working Python 3 transition image currently installs, from the Debian
snapshot and a small amount of pip/GitHub glue:

- Python runtime and build tools:
  - `python3`
  - `python3-dev`
  - `python3-pip`
  - `python3-setuptools`
  - `cython3`
  - `g++`
  - `make`
- scientific Python stack:
  - `numpy`
  - `scipy`
  - `matplotlib`
  - `pytest`
- legacy FEniCS stack:
  - `python3-dolfin = 2017.1.0-4`
  - `python3-ffc = 2017.1.0-2`
  - `python3-ufl = 2017.1.0-2`
  - `python3-dijitso = 2017.1.0-2`
  - `python3-fiat = 2017.1.0-2`
  - `python3-instant = 2017.1.0-2`
- PETSc / SLEPc layer:
  - `python3-petsc4py = 3.7.0-3+b2`
  - `python3-slepc4py = 3.7.0-3+b1`
  - matching PETSc / SLEPc / DOLFIN development libraries
- native build / runtime libraries:
  - `libsundials-dev = 2.7.0+dfsg-2`
  - `libboost-python-dev`
  - `libboost-test-dev`
  - `libhdf5-openmpi-dev`
  - `libhdf5-mpi-dev`
  - `libnetcdf-dev`
  - `libvtk6-dev`
  - `libgdal-dev`
  - `default-libmysqlclient-dev`
  - image-format and compression headers such as `libpng-dev`, `libtiff-dev`,
    `zlib1g-dev`
- mesh and geometry tools:
  - `gmsh`
  - `netgen`
- visualization / headless runtime support:
  - `xvfb`
  - `xauth`
  - `tix`
  - `tcl-dev`
  - `tk-dev`
- extra external components:
  - `dolfinh5tools` from GitHub via `pip`
  - `oommf` from `https://github.com/fangohr/oommf`

*** Likely `pixi` / conda-manageable today

Based on the current codebase imports, the transition image, and current
conda-forge package availability, the following parts look feasible for a
`pixi` environment:

- core Python toolchain:
  - `python`
  - `pip`
  - `setuptools`
  - `cython`
  - `pytest`
- scientific Python stack:
  - `numpy`
  - `scipy`
  - `matplotlib`
- legacy FEniCS track:
  - `fenics = 2019.1.0` is available on conda-forge
- FEniCSx track:
  - `fenics-dolfinx` is available on conda-forge
- PETSc / SLEPc layer:
  - `petsc`
  - `petsc4py`
  - `slepc`
  - `slepc4py`
- meshing / geometry:
  - `gmsh`
  - `python-gmsh`
  - `netgen`
- visualization:
  - `paraview`
- external solver:
  - `oommf`

This is encouraging for M2 and also for later M3/M4 work, because it means the
broader numerical and meshing ecosystem is not obviously blocked on package
availability alone.

*** Likely external or project-specific work, even with `pixi`

The following items look like they will still need custom handling:

- Finmag packaging itself
  - there is no modern top-level package metadata yet
  - M2 needs a real install layout, not just dependency installation
- `dolfinh5tools`
  - this is an external companion package, not part of Finmag
  - the current Python 3 image installs it directly from GitHub and patches it
  - for `pixi`, this likely needs either:
    - a pip dependency from GitHub, or
    - a separate package recipe / packaging story
- Finmag native extensions
  - `native/Makefile` still drives the build
  - M2 needs a decision on whether `pixi` invokes that build explicitly or
    whether packaging is taught to build/install these extensions
- OOMMF integration details
  - package availability now looks good, but Finmag depends on the
    Southampton/Fangohr fork and on executable discovery conventions
  - M2 should verify that the conda-forge `oommf` package is functionally
    equivalent for Finmag's comparison helpers
- Netgen runtime behavior
  - package availability is not the same as runtime reliability
  - the current Python 3 transition image still needs compatibility handling
    for the historical `netgen 4.9.13` shutdown bug and Tcl/Tix paths
  - a `pixi` environment will need its own probe and validation path

*** Likely blockers for a full M2 environment on the current 2017 stack

These look like the main risks for a full `pixi` environment that reproduces
the current M1 stack exactly:

- exact legacy `dolfin 2017.1.0`
  - the current working environment depends on a Debian snapshot package set
  - the conda-forge route is much more naturally aligned with legacy
    `fenics 2019.1.0` than with this exact `2017.1.0` stack
- direct parity with the current container's native-library mix
  - the container uses historical Debian package versions and local native
    compatibility fixes
  - `pixi` may be an easier fit once the project moves to M3
- historical optional tool paths
  - `nsim`
  - Mercurial / `hg`
  - GUI-heavy Paraview workflows
  - these should not block M2 unless we decide they are part of the supported
    user-facing environment

*** Current recommendation for M2

Do not aim first for:

- "reproduce the whole M1 Debian-snapshot stack in pixi"

Aim first for:

- a `pixi`-managed environment for the Python-facing Finmag package and its
  practical workflows,
- most likely using conda-forge `fenics 2019.1.0` as the more natural legacy
  FEniCS packaging target,
- while treating exact `2017.1.0` parity as a container-only reference.

That suggests the packaging track and the M3 legacy-FEniCS-2019 track may be
partly coupled in practice.

*** Proposed first `pixi` target

The first `pixi` attempt should be intentionally narrow.

Recommended target environment contents:

- `python`
- `pip`
- `setuptools`
- `cython`
- `numpy`
- `scipy`
- `matplotlib`
- `pytest`
- `fenics = 2019.1.0`
- `petsc4py`
- `slepc4py`
- `gmsh`
- `netgen`

Recommended initial omissions:

- `oommf`
- `paraview`
- GUI/rendering support
- `nsim`
- Mercurial / `hg`
- any attempt to preserve exact `dolfin 2017.1.0` package parity

Recommended first acceptance checks for M2:

1. `pixi run import-finmag`
2. `pixi run barmini-construct`
3. `pixi run barmini-smoke`

Recommended interpretation of the outcomes:

- if step 1 fails, the blocker is packaging/import-level and M2 should stay
  focused there;
- if steps 1 and 2 pass but step 3 fails, M2 has still made real progress,
  but the native-build/runtime path needs a second pass;
- if all three pass, M2 has a credible first user-facing environment even
  before optional tools are layered in.

*** Suggested next M2 deliverables

In order:

1. add a first `pixi.toml` to the repo, even if incomplete;
2. add a minimal packaging/install story for Finmag itself;
3. document the exact `pixi` commands for environment creation and the first
   acceptance checks above;
4. only then broaden to optional tools such as OOMMF or visualization.

*** First `pixi.toml` draft

The repository now contains:

- `pixi.toml`

This first draft is intentionally a packaging probe, not a claim of immediate
success. It currently targets:

- conda-forge
- `linux-64`
- `python 3.11`
- `fenics 2019.1.0`
- `petsc4py`
- `slepc4py`
- `gmsh`
- `netgen`
- the standard scientific Python stack needed by the current Finmag code

The initial tasks in that file mirror the proposed first M2 acceptance checks:

- `pixi run import-finmag`
- `pixi run barmini-construct`
- `pixi run barmini-smoke`

The tasks currently rely on:

- `PYTHONPATH=src`

rather than a real package installation, because Finmag still lacks modern
top-level packaging metadata. That is acceptable for the first probe, but it
is not the final M2 install story.

*** Verified first-pass `pixi` status

The current verified position is:

- `pixi run import-finmag` works against conda-forge `fenics 2019.1.0`
- `pixi run barmini-construct` works with the full default
  `finmag.example.barmini()` setup
- `pixi run barmini-smoke` works through `run_until(1e-12)` on the default
  Sundials backend
- `pixi run restart-smoke` works through save/restart/reload on the default
  Sundials backend
- the verified smoke path uses the full default `barmini`, including FK
  demag, FEniCS 2019.1.0, and the native Sundials 7.2.1 wrapper

The `pixi.toml` tasks `barmini-construct` and `barmini-smoke` have been
aligned to this stronger verified workflow, and `restart-smoke` extends that
matrix to state persistence.

*** Aggregate M2 witness and CI

The pixi side now has a single aggregate witness, analogous to the Docker-side
=dev/bin/verify-python3-m1= script:

- `dev/bin/verify-python3-m2`
  - runs:
    - `pixi run import-finmag`
    - `pixi run barmini-construct`
    - `pixi run barmini-smoke`
    - `pixi run restart-smoke`
  - exports `XDG_CACHE_HOME=/tmp/pixi-cache` and
    `PIXI_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/pixi-cache` by default so the pixi witness does not
    recreate repo-local state that would later interfere with the
    Docker-copying M1 helpers. [Codex GPT-5.4]

There is now a matching GitHub Actions workflow:

- `.github/workflows/python3-m2.yml`
  - uses `prefix-dev/setup-pixi@v0.9.3`
  - pins `pixi-version: v0.62.1`
  - enables action-managed cache reuse
  - runs `dev/bin/verify-python3-m2`

This makes the pixi / FEniCS-2019 path visible in CI as a workflow-level M2
usability witness, rather than only as manually remembered `pixi run ...`
commands. [Codex GPT-5.4]

*** Native Sundials port status

The previous main runtime gap for M2 was the native Sundials backend. That gap
is now closed for the first practical workflow.

Verified result:

- `from finmag.native import sundials` works in the `pixi` environment
- `sundials.get_sundials_version()` reports `7.2.1`
- `finmag.example.barmini().run_until(1e-12)` completes on the default
  Sundials backend in `pixi`

Key porting points:

- `native/src/sundials/sundials_cvode_impl.h`
  - conditional SUNDIALS 7 include path
  - `SUNContext` lifecycle handling
  - modern `CVodeSetLinearSolver` / `CVodeSetJacTimes` /
    `CVodeSetPreconditioner` wiring
  - compatibility aliases for pre-SUNDIALS-6 type and constant names
  - cloned `N_Vector` template retained from `init()` so iterative solvers do
    not depend on `CVodeGetCurrentState()` being populated during setup
  - synthetic scratch vectors supplied to Python preconditioner callbacks on
    the SUNDIALS 7 path to preserve the historical Python callback signatures
- `native/Makefile`
  - `sundials.so` is back in the `pixi` native module set
  - the module now links explicitly against the split SUNDIALS 7 CVODE,
    SUNLINSOL, SUNMATRIX, and core libraries

The FK demag path itself is no longer the main blocker:

- a new array-based native BEM entry point now bypasses the old
  `BoundaryMesh -> std::shared_ptr` Boost.Python conversion assumption
- this is enough for the default `barmini` construction and short SciPy-backed
  run under `fenics 2019.1.0`

** Current Reference Environment

Reference container:

- `finmag/finmag:latest`

Verified contents:

- `python` = Python 2.7.12
- `python3` = Python 3.5.x
- `python` can import `dolfin`
- `python3` cannot import `dolfin`

Verified DOLFIN version in the reference container:

- `dolfin.__version__ == 2017.1.0`

Installed package metadata observed in the container:

- `python-dolfin` = `2017.1.0-1~ppa3~xenial1`
- `libdolfin2017.1` = `2017.1.0-1~ppa3~xenial1`
- `libdolfin-dev` = `2017.1.0-1~ppa3~xenial1`
- `python-ffc` = `2017.1.0-1~ppa1~xenial1`
- `python-fiat` = `2017.1.0-1~ppa1~xenial1`
- `python-ufl` = `2017.1.0-1~ppa1~xenial1`

Observed package source configuration in the container:

- binary packages come from `ppa:fenics-packages/fenics`
- the `deb-src` entry for that PPA was commented out by default

** Important Constraint

Ubuntu/xenial packaging in the current Finmag image provides:

- `python-dolfin`

but not:

- `python3-dolfin`

for the installed `2017.1.0` stack.

This is why the Python 3 port can proceed with syntax and import cleanup, but
cannot yet run Finmag end to end under Python 3 in the current image.

** Upstream / Packaging Findings

*** DOLFIN 2017.1.0

During probing we established that upstream `dolfin 2017.1.0` switched the
default build to Python 3, with Python 2 requiring:

- `-DDOLFIN_USE_PYTHON3=off`

This is important because it means a Python 3 build of the 2017 generation is
not conceptually alien to DOLFIN itself; the limitation is mainly packaging and
dependency availability in the current reference image.

*** PPA Source Mismatch

After enabling the PPA `deb-src` entry in a disposable container, `apt-get
source dolfin` did not fetch `2017.1.0`. It fetched:

- `dolfin 2019.1.0-1~ppa0~xenial2`

This means the current PPA source state no longer matches the installed binary
`2017.1.0` packages in `finmag/finmag:latest`.

So:

- the PPA is useful to inspect newer packaging patterns,
- but not sufficient to reconstruct the exact old source package used by the
  reference image.

*** Debian Snapshot With Matching Python 3 Packages

The decisive finding came from Debian snapshot probing.

Snapshot tested:

- `http://snapshot.debian.org/archive/debian/20170920T000000Z sid`

Observed package availability there:

- `python3-dolfin = 2017.1.0-4`
- `python3-ffc = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-ufl = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-dijitso = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-fiat = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-instant = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-petsc4py = 3.7.0-3+b2`
- `python3-slepc4py = 3.7.0-3+b1`

This is the first verified source of a historically matching Python 3 DOLFIN
stack for the 2017 generation.

The same snapshot also contains the corresponding source package:

- `dolfin = 2017.1.0-4`

The package metadata and build logs for this generation show Debian/Ubuntu
building with both Python 2 and Python 3 via:

- `dh ... --with python2,python3`

and using:

- `-D DOLFIN_USE_PYTHON3:BOOL=OFF` for the Python 2 build directories

which confirms that the `2017.1.0-4` packaging is a real dual-runtime baseline.

** Snapshot Package Resolution Notes

Initial install probing against the `20170920T000000Z sid` snapshot showed:

- installing `python3-dolfin=2017.1.0-4` alone is insufficient
- it requires `libdolfin-dev=2017.1.0-4`
- `libdolfin-dev=2017.1.0-4` in turn requires additional dev/runtime packages,
  including:
  - `libhdf5-mpi-dev`
  - `libscotch-dev`
  - `libvtk6-dev`
  - `libpetsc3.7.6-dev`
  - `libslepc3.7.4-dev`

This means the snapshot image needs either:

1. a broader install of the full matching dependency closure from the same
   snapshot, or
2. a staged `dist-upgrade` onto that historical snapshot before package
   installation.

The experimental Dockerfile under:

- `install/docker/python3-dolfin2017-snapshot/Dockerfile`

follows the second approach.

*** Exact package versions currently targeted

The current experimental Python 3 image is pinned to the following package
versions from the same snapshot:

- `libdolfin-dev = 2017.1.0-4`
- `python3-dolfin = 2017.1.0-4`
- `python3-ffc = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-ufl = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-dijitso = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-fiat = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-instant = 2017.1.0-2`
- `python3-petsc4py = 3.7.0-3+b2`
- `python3-slepc4py = 3.7.0-3+b1`
- `libpetsc3.7.6-dev`
- `libslepc3.7.4-dev`
- `zlib1g = 1:1.2.8.dfsg-5`

In addition to the DOLFIN-specific packages, the current install attempt also
pulls in development headers required by the historical dependency closure,
including:

- `libhdf5-openmpi-dev`
- `libhdf5-mpi-dev`
- `libptscotch-dev`
- `libscotch-dev`
- `libvtk6-dev`

The `zlib1g` pin was added because the resolver otherwise tried to combine:

- `zlib1g-dev = 1:1.2.8.dfsg-5`

with:

- `zlib1g = 1:1.2.8.dfsg-5+deb9u1`

which is not accepted by `apt`.

** Comparison With Other Archive Routes

Other archive probes were less suitable:

- Ubuntu 18.04 (`bionic`) offers `dolfin 2017.2.0.post0-2`, not `2017.1.0`
- Debian `stretch` snapshot route provided `dolfin 2016.2.0-2`, not `2017.1.0`

Therefore the currently best historically matching route for a Python 3 DOLFIN
runtime is:

- Debian snapshot `20170920T000000Z sid`

** Working Assumption Going Forward

For the purpose of getting Finmag running under Python 3 with an old DOLFIN
stack, the most promising path is:

1. build or install a Python 3 environment from the Debian snapshot carrying
   `python3-dolfin=2017.1.0-4`,
2. copy the Finmag checkout into that environment,
3. continue validating:
   - `python3 -c "import dolfin"`
   - `python3 -c "import finmag"`
   - then progressively larger test subsets.

** Current Build Result

The experimental image defined in:

- `install/docker/python3-dolfin2017-snapshot/Dockerfile`

now builds successfully as:

- `finmag-py3-dolfin2017`

Verified build-time import result:

- `python3 = 3.5.4`
- `dolfin.__version__ = 2017.1.0`
- `dolfin.__file__ = /usr/lib/python3/dist-packages/dolfin/__init__.py`

Verified runtime import result in the built image:

- `docker run --rm -e OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated finmag-py3-dolfin2017 ...`

and again:

- `python3 = 3.5.4`
- `dolfin.__version__ = 2017.1.0`

** Open MPI Runtime Note

Importing `dolfin` in this snapshot-based image triggers MPI initialization.
In both Docker build and container runtime contexts, this required:

- `OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated`

Without that override, `import dolfin` failed with Open MPI errors about:

- `plm_rsh_agent`
- `Unable to start a daemon on the local node`
- `MPI_Init_thread`

So the current working assumption is that any Python 3 Finmag test runs in this
image should start with at least:

- `OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated`

** OOMMF Runtime Notes

The Python 3 transition image now installs the Southampton/Fangohr OOMMF fork
from:

- =https://github.com/fangohr/oommf=

This fork is needed rather than a plain system OOMMF because Finmag's OOMMF
comparison helpers reference local extensions such as:

- =Southampton_CubicAnisotropy8=
- =Southampton_UniaxialAnisotropy4=
- DMI and PBC extension objects compiled under =app/oxs/local=

Build/runtime constraint:

- OOMMF refuses to run as root.

The Dockerfile therefore:

- creates an =oommfbuild= user,
- clones and builds OOMMF as that user with =make build=,
- installs a small =/usr/local/bin/oommf= wrapper around
  =/usr/local/oommf/oommf/oommf.tcl=,
- verifies =oommf +version= as =oommfbuild=,
- switches the final image runtime user to =oommfbuild=.

Verified OOMMF version in the image:

- =OOMMF 2.1a0=

Python 3 fixes required after OOMMF became available:

- =src/finmag/util/oommf/ovf.py= now opens OVF files in binary mode and
  decodes only line-oriented ASCII headers, because OOMMF writes binary OVF
  payloads that cannot be read through Python 3 text decoding.
- =src/finmag/util/oommf/lattice.py= now uses identity checks for optional
  NumPy-array data, avoiding ambiguous array truth-value errors.

- =src/finmag/tests/oommf/test_anisotropy.py=
- =src/finmag/tests/oommf/test_exchange.py=

Result:

** Netgen / Mesh-Test Findings

The transition image now also installs:

- =netgen=
- =tix=

plus compatibility symlinks for the Tcl/Tix lookup paths expected by

But the historical binary still is not fully stable in this container:

- it can abort with =corrupted double-linked list= after successful mesh

- tests should not treat =shutil.which("netgen")= as sufficient

  =.grid= file was written successfully
- mesh conversion now falls back to the Python module
  =dolfin_utils.meshconvert.meshconvert.convert2xml= when the
  =dolfin-convert= script is absent from =PATH=

** External Package Brought Across: dolfinh5tools

From the repo structure and install scripts, =dolfinh5tools= is an external
package, not part of Finmag itself.

Evidence in-tree:

- install scripts and Docker recipes fetch it from
  =git+https://github.com/fangohr/dolfinh5tools.git=
- =src/finmag/field.py= imports it optionally and uses it for
  =Field.save_hdf5(...)=
- =src/finmag/field_test.py= currently uses =pytest.importorskip("dolfinh5tools")=
  for the HDF5 save test

Current Python 3 transition status:

- the transition image now installs =dolfinh5tools= directly from its GitHub
  repository during the Docker build
- the image applies a small Python 3 compatibility patch to the installed
  package:
  - explicit relative import in =dolfinh5tools/__init__.py=
  - Python 3 print syntax in =dolfinh5tools/lib.py=
- direct import now works under
  =docker run --rm -e OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated finmag-py3-dolfin2017 ...=
- =src/finmag/field_test.py::TestField::test_save_hdf5= now passes under
  Python 3
- because =src/finmag/field_test.py= was already part of
  =dev/bin/verify-python3-core-suite= and
  =.github/workflows/python3-core-suite.yml=, this progress is already
  reflected in CI without adding a new test file

One real runtime bug was fixed while bringing this suite across:

- =src/finmag/scheduler/derivedevents.py=

Specifically, =RepeatingTimeEvent= claimed to support callable intervals, but
its constructor still compared the interval directly against zero. Under
Python 3 this failed for =functools.partial= during =sim.relax()=. The fix was
to validate negativity only for non-callable intervals.

With the additional snapshot package:

- `libsundials-dev = 2.7.0+dfsg-2`

the Finmag native build in the Python 3 image now gets past missing-header
failures such as:

- `cvode/cvode.h`
- `nvector/nvector_serial.h`

The build now fails later, inside the Finmag Sundials wrapper itself.

Current failing file:

- `native/src/sundials/sundials_cvode_impl.h`

Observed issue:

- the wrapper only recognises Sundials `2.4.0` and `2.5.0`
- the snapshot image provides Sundials `2.7.0`
- the internal version-to-type mapping therefore resolves to:
  - `sundials_traits<-1>`

This causes the compile-time API mismatches now seen in the Python 3 image:

- callback signatures for `CVDlsSetDenseJacFn` and `CVDlsSetBandJacFn`
- `int*` vs `long int*` mismatches for:
  - `CVDlsGetLastFlag`
  - `CVDiagGetLastFlag`
  - `CVSpilsGetLastFlag`

Working assumption:

- Sundials `2.7.0` should be mapped like `2.5.0` here, i.e. use `long`
  parameter types in the CVODE wrapper
- the next patch should therefore start by extending the wrapper's version
  trait handling to `2.7.0`

** Native Build Result

The native blocker above has now been resolved sufficiently for the current
milestone.

Changes that proved necessary:

- extend the Sundials wrapper in:
  - `native/src/sundials/sundials_cvode_impl.h`
  to recognize Sundials `2.7.0`
- make the native build Python-version-aware in:
  - `native/Makefile`
  - `src/finmag/util/native_compiler.py`

** Python 3 Import Milestone

The following command now succeeds:

#+begin_src bash
docker run --rm \
  -e OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated \
  -v /tank/fangohr/git/finmag:/repo:ro \
  finmag-py3-dolfin2017 \
  bash -lc 'rm -rf /tmp/finmag && cp -a /repo /tmp/finmag && cd /tmp/finmag && \
            PYTHONPATH=/tmp/finmag/src python3 -c "import finmag; print(finmag)"'
#+end_src

Observed output at the end of startup:

- module import succeeds
- `print(finmag)` reports:
  - `<module 'finmag' from '/tmp/finmag/src/finmag/__init__.py'>`

Additional observations from the successful import path:

- startup logging now reports:
  - `Python = 3.5.4`
  - `Dolfin = 2017.1.0`
  - `Sundials = 2.7.0`
- missing optional packages such as SciPy no longer abort the import
- `paraview` is absent in this image, which only triggers warnings

** Manual Verification Workflow

For manual verification of the current milestone, use the following steps from
the repository root.

*** 1. Build the Python 3 DOLFIN image

#+begin_src bash
docker build \
  -f install/docker/python3-dolfin2017-snapshot/Dockerfile \
  -t finmag-py3-dolfin2017 .
#+end_src

*** 2. Start an interactive container

#+begin_src bash
docker run --rm -it \
  -e OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated \
  -v "$PWD":/repo:ro \
  finmag-py3-dolfin2017 \
  bash
#+end_src

*** 3. Copy the checkout to a writable location and test the import

Inside the container:

#+begin_src bash
rm -rf /tmp/finmag
cp -a /repo /tmp/finmag
cd /tmp/finmag
PYTHONPATH=/tmp/finmag/src python3
#+end_src

Then at the Python prompt:

#+begin_src python
import finmag
print(finmag)
#+end_src

*** 4. One-shot non-interactive verification command

#+begin_src bash
docker run --rm \
  -e OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated \
  -v "$PWD":/repo:ro \
  finmag-py3-dolfin2017 \
  bash -lc 'rm -rf /tmp/finmag && cp -a /repo /tmp/finmag && cd /tmp/finmag && \
            PYTHONPATH=/tmp/finmag/src python3 -c "import finmag; print(finmag)"'
#+end_src

*** 5. Helper script

The repository now contains:

- `dev/bin/verify-python3-m1`

- `dev/bin/verify-python3-restart-smoke`

This helper:

and:

#+end_src

and:

#+end_src

and:

** Scope-Limiting Import Decisions

To reach the import milestone without pulling in the entire unfinished Python 3
port, a few non-core features are now optional at import time:

- `SLLG` support is deferred unless the `sllg` kernel is explicitly selected
- the SciPy integrator backend is deferred unless explicitly selected
- normal-mode support is deferred unless explicitly selected

These are intentional scope boundaries for the current phase, not final
feature-complete Python 3 support. `SLLG` is no longer blocked, but keeping it
lazy at import time still avoids widening the base import surface unnecessarily.

** GCR Demag Remains Unported

The historical `GCR` demag solver is intentionally not counted as ported on
the Python 3 path.

What changed:

- `src/finmag/energies/demag/gcr_demag.py`
  - now serves only as a historical placeholder
  - instantiating `GCRDemag` raises `NotImplementedError`
- `src/finmag/energies/demag/__init__.py`
  - `"GCR"` was removed from `KNOWN_SOLVERS`

Why:

- the repo still contains the native `compute_bem_gcr(...)` symbol
- but the surviving Python-side demag code does not retain a complete and
  clearly correct GCR formulation
- mapping `Demag("GCR")` to FK made the tests pass but was scientifically
  misleading, since it did not restore a distinct GCR solve

Current status:

- `test_compute_scalar_potential_gcr` is an explicit skip again
- the Python 3 transition focuses on genuinely ported functionality only

** Barmini Smoke Milestone

The runtime milestone above has now been reached.

This remains the primary practical smoke path for M1 because it exercises the
time-integration workflow directly.

The following command succeeds:

#+begin_src bash
docker run --rm \
  -e OMPI_MCA_plm=isolated \
  -v /tank/fangohr/git/finmag:/repo:ro \
  finmag-py3-dolfin2017 \
  bash -lc 'rm -rf /tmp/finmag && cp -a /repo /tmp/finmag && cd /tmp/finmag && \
            PYTHONPATH=/tmp/finmag/src python3 - <<\"PY\"
import finmag
sim = finmag.example.barmini(name=\"barmini_py3_smoke\")
print(\"constructed\", sim.__class__.__name__)
sim.run_until(1e-12)
print(\"advanced\", sim.t)
PY'
#+end_src

Observed result:

- `constructed Simulation`
- `advanced 1e-12`

** Aggregate M1 Verification Command

The practical M1 witness can also be run as one command:

#+begin_src bash
dev/bin/verify-python3-m1
#+end_src

This wrapper runs the four manual M1 workflow witnesses in sequence:

- import
- `barmini` time integration
- `barmini` restart/save-load
- supplementary normal-modes smoke coverage

This is intended as the simplest reproducible answer to "is M1 usable right
now?" without requiring a full pytest run.

The helper now executes the full M1 witness in a single container session and
single writable checkout copy, which avoids repeating the native rebuild four
times. The targeted sub-helpers still exist for manual debugging, and GitHub
Actions has a matching lightweight workflow in:

- `.github/workflows/python3-m1.yml`

** Normal-Modes Smoke Milestone

The M1 workflow matrix also has an explicit normal-modes smoke command, but it
is supplementary rather than primary. For practical usability on the legacy
stack, the =barmini= time-integration path is the more important smoke witness.

#+begin_src bash
dev/bin/verify-python3-normal-modes-smoke
#+end_src

Observed result:

- `constructed NormalModeSimulation`
- `modes 2`
- a finite printed `first_freq`
- a small printed `first_rel_error`

This smoke path is intentionally lightweight. It reuses the checked-in nanodisk
mesh and relaxed restart state from `src/finmag/example/normal_modes/`, so it
verifies the Python 3 normal-modes runtime without paying the cost of fresh
mesh generation or relaxation.

** What Fixed The Runtime Regression

After `import finmag` was working, the next blocker was a CVODE runtime failure
even for trivial ODEs under Python 3:

- `CV_CONV_FAILURE` at `t = 0`

Important narrowing observations:

- the same `barmini().run_until(1e-12)` step succeeds in the Python 2 reference image
- the initial Finmag RHS for `barmini` is identical in Python 2 and Python 3
- even a one-dimensional test problem `y' = 0.5 y` failed in the Python 3 image

This showed that the regression was in the native Sundials/NVector runtime
path, not in the Python-level field setup.

The fix that restored runtime integration was:

- stop linking the Python 3 Sundials module against Finmag's vendored custom
  `libnvector_serial_custom_malloc`
- link it against the system:
  - `libsundials_nvecserial`
- construct callback-local NumPy array views directly from raw NVector memory in:
  - `native/src/sundials/numpy_malloc.cc`

This keeps the callback bridge working under Python 3 while avoiding the
runtime incompatibility between the old vendored NVector implementation and
SUNDIALS `2.7.0`.

** Comparison Test Milestone

The remaining legacy comparison test files under
=src/finmag/tests/comparison/= now collect under Python 3 and are represented
in the active core gate.

Useful details:

- =finmag.util.magpar.read_inp= now reads gzipped reference data in text mode,
  which is required for Python 3.
- =finmag.util.oommf.comparison= now materialises =zip(...)= iterators before
  building NumPy coordinate arrays.
- =finmag.util.oommf.oommf_calculator= and =finmag.util.oommf.ovf= were patched
  far enough that OOMMF comparison tests run under Python 3.
- =finmag.util.oommf.lattice= imports =reduce= from =functools=.

  image

mesh helpers, comparison data, normal modes, drivers, Sundials, demag/BEM,
SLLG, Zhang-Li, simulation helpers and the main =Simulation= subsystem.

  now allows a small regression margin instead of asserting strict

The Zeeman promotion keeps the historical sphere-vs-dipole absolute
stray-field guard at =140.0= A/m. Investigation showed that the
DOLFIN-2019/Netgen mesh with =maxh_sphere= = =2.5= produced a slightly larger
pointwise tail (about =150.9= A/m) despite passing the relative field checks;
refining only the magnetic sphere to =maxh_sphere= = =2.0= brings the tail
back below the original guard and avoids hiding the drift by loosening the
assertion. [Codex GPT-5.4]

The same increment promotes the non-PBC demag/BEM regression files. The
Magpar boundary-element reference helper now falls back to a pure-NumPy
implementation when =instant= is absent, and the BEM tests use the production
DOLFIN-2019 array fallback for native FK BEM construction. The legacy GCR
solver path remains intentionally skipped on Python 3. [Codex GPT-5.4]

Compatibility changes that enabled this:

- =finmag.util.helpers.vector_valued_function()= and
  =scalar_valued_function()= now accept =df.UserExpression= and initialize
  callable helper expressions in the DOLFIN 2019-compatible order, which
  repairs callable magnetisation/current-profile setup on the pixi path
- =finmag.physics.llg_stt.LLG_STT= now uses =get_local()= instead of the
  removed PETSc vector =.array()= accessor in the exercised Sundials/STT path
- =test_zhangli_sllg= now skips explicitly when =finmag.native.llb= is absent,
  which keeps the deterministic Zhang-Li regression in M3 without forcing the
  stochastic =sllg= native module into the first pixi build
- =finmag.util.solid_angle_magpar.return_csa_magpar()= now falls back to a
  NumPy implementation of the Magpar solid-angle reference formula when the
  old =instant= package is unavailable on the pixi path
- =finmag.physics.neb= now imports without =finmag.native.neb= for helper-only
  use, and defers the native-module requirement to the actual tangent
  computation path used by full NEB runs
- the pixi native build now includes =llb.so= again, and
  =finmag.physics.llb.sllg.SLLG= now uses =get_local()= on the DOLFIN 2019
  PETSc-vector paths exercised by the stochastic =sllg= runtime
- =finmag.util.meshes.build_mesh()= now opens =MeshEditor= with the DOLFIN
  2019 cell-type string API, while the Gmsh helper forces =-format msh2= so
  the legacy =dolfin-convert= path can still import the generated mesh on the
  pixi stack
- =finmag.sim.sim_savers= now saves PETSc-backed field data through
  =vector().get_local()=, and the skyrmion-density helper in
  =finmag.sim.sim_helpers= uses the same DOLFIN 2019-compatible vector access
- =finmag.normal_modes.eigenmodes.helpers= now passes =rcond=None= into
  =numpy.linalg.lstsq()= and uses a raw docstring for the linear-combination
  helper, which removes NumPy API drift warnings from the promoted M3 slice
- the normal-mode eigenproblem/eigensolver tests now work on the pixi path
  after:
  - switching old pytest =setup()= hooks to =setup_method()=
  - replacing positional Matplotlib =legend(..., 'lower center')= calls with
    the modern keyword form
  - adding a local DOLFIN 2019 timing fallback in
    =finmag.normal_modes.eigenmodes.eigensolvers=
- the field setter/field regression files now work on the pixi path after:
  - switching the large =TestField= fixture class to =setup_method()=
  - replacing removed PETSc vector =.array()= and =.abs()= assumptions in the
    tests with =get_local()= and NumPy-based comparisons
  - treating the removed DOLFIN Python =Function.label()= accessor as optional
- =finmag.sim.Simulation.schedule()= now uses a Python 3.11-safe
  =inspect.getfullargspec= fallback rather than the removed
  =inspect.getargspec=
- =finmag.util.meshes= now falls back to the Netgen Python API when the
  conda-forge package does not provide the historical =netgen= CLI, and
  launched-and-hung Netgen probes raise rather than silently turning into
  skips
- =finmag.util.meshes= now uses Matplotlib's supported
  =add_subplot(..., projection='3d')= path for 3D plotting on this stack
- =finmag.util.helpers= and its exercised callers now use =get_local()= on
  PETSc-backed vectors, accept =UserExpression=-compatible callable helpers,
  and avoid the removed =df.parameters.reorder_dofs_serial= attribute on the
  DOLFIN 2019 path
- =src/finmag/util/mesh_templates_test.py= now uses a looser combined-mesh
  volume tolerance on this path to account for the small numerical
  differences in regenerated Netgen geometry
- =src/finmag/util/length_scales_test.py= now uses a pytest-9-compatible
  =setup_method()= hook instead of the old bare =setup()=
- =src/finmag/util/vtk_saver_test.py= now initializes PETSc-backed vectors
  with =set_local()= instead of the removed =.array()= accessor
- =src/finmag/drivers.sundials_integrator.SundialsIntegrator.advance_steps()=
  now treats the expected "stop after mxstep internal steps" condition as
  success based on actual step-count deltas, which makes the wrapper robust to
  newer SUNDIALS bindings that surface the stop as a generic
  =RuntimeError: CVode has returned error -1=
- the Robertson stiff-ODE file now uses =scipy.integrate.solve_ivp= with
  =method="BDF"= as the SciPy-side success reference; direct investigation
  showed that old =ode(..., "vode")= /= BDF= completed correctly on
  SciPy =0.19.1= but fails badly on SciPy =1.17.1= for the same equations and
  Jacobian, while =solve_ivp(BDF)=, =solve_ivp(Radau)=, =solve_ivp(LSODA)=,
  and =zvode= still converge to the correct Robertson asymptotic state
- the two transposed-Robertson cases are now explicit =xfail(strict=True)=
  tests of known pathological failure modes on the current stack, rather than
  passing negative tests that only asserted work-budget exhaustion
- =src/finmag/tests/jacobean/test_native_llg.py= now passes on the DOLFIN
  2019 path by using =get_local()= instead of the removed PETSc
  =.array()= accessor when passing alpha values into the native LLG helper
- OOMMF-backed comparisons now run on the pixi path because =oommf= is a
  conda-forge dependency. The promoted DMDT and exchange comparison tests were
  adjusted to use =get_local()= for DOLFIN-2019 PETSc vectors instead of the
  removed legacy =.array()= accessor. [Codex GPT-5.4]
- the remaining Magpar comparison table labels now escape the literal
  backslash in =:math:`\,`=, avoiding Python invalid-escape warnings while
  preserving the generated reST text. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- =compute_normal_modes_generalised()= in the deprecated normal-mode module
  now uses a deterministic alternating ARPACK start vector when =v0= is not
  supplied, stabilising the Kittel-mode regression on SciPy =1.17.1=. [Codex

The next small M4 step adds a DOLFINx-only prototype module:

- =dev/dolfinx/prototype.py= creates vector-valued magnetisation function
  spaces, interpolates constant vector fields, assembles exchange energy
  =A * integral(grad(m) : grad(m))=, assembles the constant-field Zeeman energy
  =-mu0 * Ms * integral(m . H)=, and assembles constant-axis uniaxial
  anisotropy energy =K * integral(1 - (m . axis)^2)=
- =dev/dolfinx/test_prototype.py= compares that assembled Zeeman energy with
  the analytical unit-square value, checks =unit_length= scaling, and covers
  analytical uniaxial anisotropy cases including axis normalisation and
  zero-axis validation. It also checks exchange energy for constant and linear
  fields, including the explicit gradient scaling convention
  =unit_length ** (dim - 2)=. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- the prototype includes a deliberately small explicit normalized LLG step for
  a constant effective field. This checks a first DOLFINx-backed
  time-integration workflow without claiming to be a production solver.
  [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- =dev/dolfinx/relaxation_example.py= ties the prototype into a small
  end-to-end example: mesh creation, magnetisation setup, exchange/Zeeman/
  anisotropy energy evaluation, explicit LLG stepping, and JSON summary output.
  The wrapper task =dolfinx-example= writes
  =/tmp/finmag-dolfinx-relaxation-summary.json= by default. [Codex gpt-5.5
  high]
- the JSON summary has =schema_version = 1= and a hand-written validator,
  keeping the first M4 output contract explicit without adding a JSON-schema
  dependency. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- =dev/dolfinx/README.md= records the supported prototype scope and explicit
  non-scope. This is important because M4 is a reduced prototype, not a
  promise of legacy =finmag.Simulation= compatibility. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- the relaxation example uses small dataclasses for parameters and
  JSON-compatible results. Treat these as an API sketch for review, not as a
  stable public API. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- the reduced parameter dataclass now validates constants and vector sizes
  before DOLFINx form assembly. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- the relaxation example CLI path is covered by pytest, including the JSON
  printed to stdout and written to disk. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- invalid relaxation example CLI controls are covered by pytest, keeping the
  wrapper-facing path aligned with function-level validation. [Codex
  gpt-5.5 high]
- summary validation checks the nested =parameters= block, including missing
  keys and invalid vector values. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]
- the relaxation example is tested for deterministic JSON summaries across

** DOLFINx Porting Strategy

The current =dev/dolfinx= work is an exploration lane. It should answer
practical DOLFINx questions and provide executable witnesses, but it should not
become a clean-room mini-Finmag or the default architecture for the final port.
[Codex gpt-5.5 high]

For the actual port from FEniCS 2019.1 to DOLFINx, preserve the existing Finmag
software design as far as practical: public API, =Simulation= orchestration,
energy modules, drivers, field/data abstractions, restart/output conventions,
and the existing test suite should remain the baseline. Any departures should
be justified by DOLFINx semantics, Python/MPI/runtime changes, packaging
constraints, or other concrete software-stack changes. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]

Use the green FEniCS-2019/Python-3 tests as the behavioural contract. If the
DOLFINx port needs different behaviour, change the tests explicitly and record
why. Do not let the exploratory =PrototypeSimulation= API become the production
API unless that choice is made deliberately after mapping it back to the
existing Finmag design. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]

=dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md= records the main legacy surfaces to preserve, the
current DOLFINx exploration evidence, and the readiness criteria for editing the
matching =src/finmag= module directly. Prototype implementations remain in the
exploration lane rather than being moved into production.

The FK demag baseline in the M3/pixi path still uses compiled native BEM code:
=finmag.native.llg.compute_bem_fk= or the DOLFIN-2019-compatible
=compute_bem_fk_from_arrays= entry point. The Python/NumPy Magpar code is a
small-reference/comparison path, not the production FK BEM implementation.
Keep PBC/treecode demag separate because it depends on the missing
=finmag.native.treecode_bem= extension. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]

=pixi run native-fk-bem-smoke= now gives that compiled FK BEM baseline its own
small witness by building a tiny boundary mesh and calling
=compute_bem_fk_from_arrays=. The M3 verifier runs this before the larger test
suite so a missing or broken native FK BEM backend is reported directly.
[Codex gpt-5.5 high]

=dev/bin/verify-python3-native-fk-bem-smoke= exposes the same check as a
single-purpose diagnostic command for CI triage. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]

** Revised Direct-Source DOLFINx Migration Decision

The production DOLFINx port will not be developed as a second =finmag= package
under =dev/dolfinx= and copied into place later. The existing modules directly
under =dev/dolfinx= remain bounded mechanics probes. Once a capability's
DOLFINx behavior and legacy contract are understood, the corresponding
=src/finmag= module is edited directly and its existing tests are ported in
place.

This avoids duplicate implementations, API drift, and a final delete/move diff.
After the first DOLFINx-only source edit, commit
=ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687= is the immutable
Python-3/FEniCS-2019 oracle and is run from a separate process or checkout when
new numerical reference data is required. Production source will not acquire a
general legacy-DOLFIN/DOLFINx compatibility facade merely to keep both stacks
live in one checkout.

Two focused probes explain why the current prototype must remain evidence only:

- a restart state with cubic-anisotropy values =(7, 3, 2)= restores them as
  =(0, 0, 0)= because the reader does not reconstruct the newer fields;
- a nonuniform nodal field averaged on two MPI ranks gives a different result
  from the serial calculation because ghost entries are included in the global
  sum and count.

The direct-port design and task sequence are recorded in
=docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-06-dolfinx-core-port-design.md= and
=docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-06-dolfinx-core-port.md=.

The migration control plane now distinguishes the frozen prototype witness
from future direct source gates:

- the DOLFINx environment pins =fenics-dolfinx = "0.10.*"= and includes
  SciPy;
- =dolfinx-prototype-pytest= is the canonical name for the unchanged M4 test
  scope, while =dolfinx-pytest= remains a compatibility alias;
- future production slices use =dolfinx-src-*= task names and are not added to
  the prototype verifier;
- =dolfinx-versions= emits a sorted JSON report for DOLFINx, Python, NumPy,
  SciPy, PETSc, and MPI;
- the M2, M3, and frozen DOLFINx verifiers snapshot the tracked diff and fail
  if validation changes it;
- M2/M3 read-only validation exports =WRITE_FINMAG_VERSION=0= so a native
  rebuild cannot rewrite =src/finmag/__version__.py=. Normal native builds
  retain the historical version-generation default. [Codex GPT-5]

** Immutable FEniCS-2019 Oracle Contract

The immutable Python-3/FEniCS-2019 oracle is commit
=ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687=. Its exact aggregate M2 command is:

#+begin_src bash
dev/bin/verify-python3-m2
#+end_src

At that commit the wrapper expands, in order, to:

#+begin_src bash
pixi run import-finmag
pixi run barmini-construct
pixi run barmini-smoke
pixi run restart-smoke
#+end_src

The contemporaneous record says all four succeeded against FEniCS =2019.1.0=:
plain import, full default =barmini= construction, advancement to =1e-12= on
native Sundials =7.2.1= with FK demag, and save/restart/reload. No aggregate M2
test count or runtime was recorded, so none should be inferred. A current
pre-production-source rerun on 2026-07-20 also passed all four after a native
rebuild; that current control-plane result is evidence about the runner, not a
replacement numeric result for the historical oracle.

The exact aggregate M3 command at the oracle is:

#+begin_src bash
dev/bin/verify-python3-m3
#+end_src

It expands to:

#+begin_src bash
pixi run native-fk-bem-smoke
pixi run barmini-suite
#+end_src

The recorded oracle result is =493 passed, 21 skipped, 5 xfailed= in
approximately =730s=. This was not rerun during the control-plane work because
the existing result is tied to the immutable commit and lock, and the full gate
takes about twelve minutes.

=dev/bin/run-legacy-oracle= now runs focused argv commands in a disposable
detached worktree at that exact commit. A locked import probe completed and
reported Finmag =ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687=, DOLFIN =2019.1.0=,
Python =3.11.15=, NumPy =1.26.4=, SciPy =1.17.1=, and Sundials =7.2.1=. The
helper removed the worktree after both successful and deliberately failing
command probes without changing the active tracked state. Fixture shape and
analytic-versus-oracle rules are recorded in
=docs/superpowers/specs/legacy-oracle-fixtures.md=. [Codex GPT-5]

Native-code caveat: =finmag.native.llg.compute_bem_fk_from_arrays= accepts the

a whole still links =libdolfin=, compiles =swig_dolfin= converters, and
registers those converters at module import. The array-only LLG/BEM binding must
therefore be separated from the legacy DOLFIN mesh surface before rebuilding it

** Lazy Source Import Boundary

The first direct source slice replaces wildcard package initialization with
explicit PEP 562 lazy exports in =src/finmag/__init__.py= and
=src/finmag/energies/__init__.py=. The reviewed public-name inventories are
recorded in the direct-port design. Plain package discovery now imports neither
=dolfin=, =dolfinx=, nor =finmag.native=; requesting an unported legacy object
in the DOLFINx environment fails at the real missing =dolfin= dependency.

Importing DOLFINx =0.10.0= separately showed that it does not load legacy
=dolfin=, does not load Finmag, and exposes no =dolfinx.parameters= equivalent
to the old =reorder_dofs_serial= initializer setting. No DOLFINx initializer
side effect is therefore required. Legacy public-object resolution still sets
=dolfin.parameters["reorder_dofs_serial"] = False= before loading the object.
Because direct =from finmag.field import Field= bypasses the root resolver,
=field.py= makes the same small compatibility call after importing =dolfin=;
this is relocation of the old initializer contract, not a Field port.

** Field Ownership And Ordering Probe

Task 4's pre-source-edit probe established that DOLFINx =Function.x.array=
contains owned blocks followed by ghost blocks, unlike the owned-only legacy
=GenericVector.get_local()= result used by =Field.as_array()=. On a
=UnitSquareMesh(3, 3)= with 16 global CG1 vertices, the DOLFINx ranks own 7/9
blocks and store 5/3 additional ghosts; block size three produces owned raw
lengths 21/27 and full local lengths 36/36. The equivalent legacy run owns
6/10 scalar dofs (18/30 vector scalar entries) while each rank's mesh exposes
10 vertices including ghosts.

The accepted compatibility rules before editing production =Field= are:

- raw arrays are flat rank-local owned backend-order values; setting them is
  followed by =scatter_forward()=;
- =xyz= is flat coordinate-ordered per-node data and =xxx= is its explicit
  component-blocked transpose, not a native DOLFINx layout;
- rank-local coordinate/value and ordered-array exports exclude ghosts;
- =Field.average()= remains a global volume-weighted FEM integral;
- the prototype-only nodal summary averages owned nodal blocks once;
- normalization modifies owned nodes, detects zeros collectively, and then
  refreshes ghosts.

The old ghost-inclusive nodal reduction returned
=[1.38888889, 2.37037037, 4]= for the nonuniform two-rank witness instead of
=[1.38888889, 2.38888889, 4]=. The bounded helper is corrected and the new
=dev/dolfinx/field_ownership_probe.py= checks the fixed result, raw ownership,
normalization, flat =xyz=/=xxx= round trips, and a gathered union of 16 unique
analytic coordinate/value rows.

Verified on 2026-07-20:

- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-field-ownership-probe= passed with 16 owned
  blocks and no ghosts on one rank;
- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-field-ownership-probe-mpi= passed with 7/9
  owned and 5/3 ghost blocks;
- the focused DOLFINx adapter tests reported =31 passed in 1.51s=;
- the frozen prototype gate remained =105 passed in 2.23s=;
- the unchanged legacy =field_test.py= and =field_setters_test.py= reported
  =48 passed, 1 skipped in 126.95s=. [Codex GPT-5.6]

** Direct DOLFINx Field Port

Task 4 now edits =src/finmag/field.py= directly; no second package was built in
=dev/= and no prototype module was copied into place. The active =Field=
stores DOLFINx =fem.Function= data and makes the migration contract explicit:

- =as_array()= and =from_array()= use flat rank-local owned backend-order
  values, and all mutations call =scatter_forward()=;
- coordinate/value rows are owned rank-local exports; scalar values have shape
  =(n_owned,)= and vector values =(n_owned, components)=, while =xyz= and
  =xxx= are flat arrays with =xxx= implemented as the deliberate
  component-blocked compatibility transpose;
- constants, DOLFINx =Constant=/=Function= objects, Fields, naturally
  vectorized callables, and legacy point-at-a-time Python callables are
  accepted; cross-space Field assignment interpolates;
- =average(dx=...)= assembles a global FEM volume average using the passed
  measure, including restricted/subdomain measures;
- normalization detects a zero on any rank collectively, updates owned nodal
  blocks, and refreshes ghosts;
- VTK/PVD and XDMF output use DOLFINx-native writers.

All reductions and ghost-refreshing mutations are documented as collective.
Scalar detection uses the UFL reference value shape, preserving the distinction
between a scalar element and a blocked one-component vector. Zero-volume
rejection is exact rather than tolerance-based so physically valid nanometre-
scale measures are not rejected.

The independent pre-commit review found and resolved four gaps that the first
unit-square gate did not expose:

- a tolerance-based zero-volume check rejected valid domains smaller than
  =1e-8=; an exact-zero check and a =1e-9=-length regression now preserve the
  scale-independent average;
- a blocked one-component vector was mistaken for a scalar when inspecting the
  collapsed DOLFINx storage shape; UFL's reference value shape now distinguishes
  the two, with normalization and vector averaging tested;
- =allclose()= could compare unrelated same-shaped local buffers; it is now a
  documented collective requiring congruent communicators and the same mesh
  and function-space objects, with both equal and unequal two-rank cases;
- the production parity matrix now covers mesh dimensions 1/2/3, scalar and
  1/2/3/4-component fields, underlying mesh/dofmap/vector/PETSc accessors, and
  normalized ghost values on two ranks. No medium-or-higher finding remained
  after re-review.

The old =field_test.py= and =field_setters_test.py= remain unchanged as oracle
history. Focused production tests live in
=src/finmag/tests/test_field_dolfinx.py=, and the two-rank executable gate is
=src/finmag/tests/field_dolfinx_mpi_probe.py=. Expression strings,
=Expression=/=UserExpression=, point probing and point-measure arithmetic,
legacy plotting, spherical conversion, and =dolfinh5tools= HDF5 now raise
precise =NotImplementedError=s rather than failing incidentally.

The root =finmag.Field= export now resolves without loading legacy =dolfin=.
Other public objects such as =Simulation= still cross legacy modules and are
intentionally deferred; the lazy import test checks their real missing
dependency in the DOLFINx-only environment. In the legacy-only pixi lane, the
old downstream public-name resolution check is skipped after this DOLFINx-only
module cutover, while the package's safe lazy import boundary remains tested.

** Task 5 Energy Foundation Probe

The Task 5 source audit found that the energy package was syntactically lazy
but not backend-sliceable: =finmag.energies.__getattr__= called the legacy
=dolfin= initializer for every exported interaction. The direct port now lets
=EnergyBase=, =Exchange=, static =Zeeman=, and =UniaxialAnisotropy= bypass that
bridge, while untouched interactions retain the real missing-=dolfin= failure.
The ported modules also removed imports of =finmag.util.meshes=,
=finmag.util.helpers=, and =finmag.native= so those dependencies cannot reopen
the legacy backend transitively.

The legacy default =box-matrix-petsc= method could not remain the default when
the first supported DOLFINx algorithm is =box-assemble=. Task 5 changes the
default to the usable box-assembly path and rejects historical matrix, project,
and direct requests explicitly. Mapping those names to assembly would conceal
an algorithm change. Existing legacy Exchange, Zeeman, and anisotropy test
files remain oracle history because they import =dolfin= at collection time;
their analytical cases were translated into a compact DOLFINx production
matrix rather than edited into a second backend package.

=dev/dolfinx/energy_box_probe.py= now freezes the DOLFINx 0.10 ownership
sequence before production edits. Linear-form vectors first
=scatter_reverse(InsertMode.add)= to accumulate contributions into the owning
rank and then =scatter_forward()= to refresh completed ghost copies. Only after
that sequence may owned coefficients be divided by lumped nodal volumes. The
probe checks:

- scalar and three-component nodal volumes summing globally to the unit-square
  area without double-counting ghosts;
- Zeeman =H_eff == H=, including the negative variational sign, =mu0=, and
  cancellation of =Ms=;
- the constant-axis =K1= and =K2= anisotropy energy and field formulas;
- analytical linear-field exchange energy and the =unit_length**-2= effective
  field scaling;
- complete owner/ghost values in serial and on two ranks.

Verified on 2026-07-20:

- the serial probe passed with 16 owned blocks and no ghosts;
- the two-rank probe passed with 7/9 owned blocks and 5/3 ghosts, and both runs
  recovered global volume =1.0=. [Codex GPT-5.6]

** Direct DOLFINx Common-Energy Port

Task 5 edits the existing modules under =src/finmag/energies= directly. No
energy package was recreated in =dev/= and the validated probe was not copied
into production. The accepted production contract is:

- setup and every FEM assembly/reduction operation are collective over the
  magnetisation mesh communicator;
- the supported magnetisation space is explicitly a blocked three-component
  CG1 space; higher-order/mixed layouts are rejected before lumped weights can
  fail inconsistently across ranks;
- assembled linear forms reverse-add into owners and forward-refresh ghosts,
  while public =compute_field()= arrays contain flat owned DOLFINx backend
  order only;
- total energy performs one scalar MPI sum and applies
  =unit_length**topological_dimension=; exchange alone puts the additional
  =unit_length**-2= factor in its gradient density;
- =EnergyBase= provides box-assembled field, total energy, lumped nodal energy
  density, refreshed density Function, and owned-only global nodal average;
- static =Zeeman= accepts constants/callables, keeps its =H= Function live
  across =set_value()=, rebuilds it when =setup()= moves to a new mesh,
  preserves restricted =dx= energy assembly and legacy pointwise nodal energy
  density, and returns =H_eff == H=;
- =Exchange= accepts a finite scalar =A= and retains the public DG0 =A= Field;
- uniaxial anisotropy preserves the repository's exact
  =K1*(1-s**2)-K2*s**4= law and dynamically reassembles the nonlinear =K2=
  field after =m= changes;
- Exchange and anisotropy retain their validated constructor inputs so a
  deliberate second =setup()= can rebuild coefficient Fields/forms on a new
  mesh instead of failing through deleted state; cached density Functions are
  invalidated so they cannot remain attached to the old mesh.

The anisotropy source had lost axis normalization during the 2014 Field
migration even though the API describes the axis as a unit vector and commit
=eb504a0a= introduced normalization so =axis dot m= is a cosine. The DOLFINx
port restores that intended behavior: non-zero constant axes are normalized
and zero axes are rejected. This is a deliberate correction of a historical
regression, not an accidental numerical change.

The bounded slice requires positive constant =Ms= and finite positive
=unit_length=. Spatially varying =A=, =K1=, =K2=, anisotropy axes, and =Ms=
raise precise =NotImplementedError=s. =DipolarField= and the time,
discrete-time, Python-time, and oscillating Zeeman variants are explicit
deferred stubs. Matrix, project, native/direct anisotropy, PBC/region
interaction behavior beyond static Zeeman's passed measure, and downstream
=EffectiveField=/=Simulation=/hysteresis/LLB/normal-mode integration remain
future tasks.

Independent review found and resolved five gaps beyond the first analytical
matrix:

- the first lazy-map draft left the new time-Zeeman stubs behind the legacy
  bridge, producing a false missing-=dolfin= error; they now resolve without
  =dolfin= and fail on construction with the intended deferral;
- arbitrary higher-order spaces could reach non-positive row-sum lumped
  weights; the supported blocked three-component CG1 boundary and collective
  invalid-volume check are now explicit;
- repeated static-Zeeman setup reused an =H= Function from the old mesh and
  its first density implementation used a lumped projection rather than the
  legacy pointwise nodal product; both lifecycle and density contracts now
  have nonuniform regressions;
- callable/string spatial coefficients failed incidentally, and Exchange/
  anisotropy deleted the values needed to rebind to a new mesh; every deferred
  input now raises the documented error and repeated setup is tested;
- =EnergyBase= kept a cached density Function across re-setup; setup now
  invalidates it, and the mesh-rebind regression checks the replacement.

** Direct DOLFINx EffectiveField Port

Task 6 ports =src/finmag/physics/effective_field.py= directly. The registry
API is unchanged: =add=/=get=/=exists=/=all=/=remove=, the unique-name
=ValueError= on duplicate =add=, =UnknownInteraction= on missing =get=/
=remove=, =compute=/=compute_jacobian_only=/=total_energy=, and the
=with_time_update= callback contract including the "some interactions
require a time update, but no time step was given" error when =t= is omitted.

Three legacy surfaces were replaced:

- =self.m_field.f.vector().local_size()= (legacy dolfin) is now
  =self.m_field.as_array().size=, i.e. the DOLFINx =Field='s flat rank-local
  owned-dof count -- the same owned-array contract every ported interaction's
  =compute_field()= already returns, so =H_eff= accumulation stays
  shape-consistent.
- =get_dolfin_function= no longer imports
  =finmag.util.helpers.vector_valued_function= (which pulls in legacy
  =dolfin= at module import time). It is reimplemented directly using the
  ported =Field=: =Field(self.m_field.functionspace,
  interaction.compute_field()).f= builds a fresh DOLFINx =Function= on m's
  space from the interaction's owned field array, matching the legacy
  contract of returning a bare Function (mirroring how
  =Simulation.llg._m_field.f= already returns one for ~m~ itself). The
  historical =region= parameter was already unused by the old
  implementation's call to =vector_valued_function=; rather than preserve
  that silent no-op, the DOLFINx port raises an explicit
  =NotImplementedError= naming region-restriction as deferred instead of
  quietly ignoring the argument.
- =from finmag.energies import TimeZeeman= is preserved unchanged. Task 5
  already made =TimeZeeman= resolve to an explicit deferred stub
  (=_DeferredZeeman= subclass) without requiring legacy =dolfin=, so this
  import and the =isinstance(interaction, TimeZeeman)= auto-connection check
  both work exactly as before; only actually constructing a =TimeZeeman=
  still raises its documented deferral. The new test suite exercises the
  auto-connection with a plain-Python subclass double that overrides
  =__init__= (so =isinstance= still succeeds without triggering the raise),
  confirming =with_time_update= is wired to =interaction.update= exactly as
  the legacy behavior intended.

=src/finmag/physics/__init__.py= is empty, so importing
=finmag.physics.effective_field= directly never pulled in unrelated legacy
subsystems; no import-boundary change was needed there.

New focused tests (=src/finmag/tests/test_effective_field_dolfinx.py=) cover:
registry behavior against plain-Python interaction doubles independent of
FEM assembly (add/get/exists/all/remove, duplicate-name rejection,
=UnknownInteraction=); total field and total energy summed across real
=Exchange=, =Zeeman=, and =UniaxialAnisotropy= instances on a small
DOLFINx mesh; the legacy compute()-returns-a-copy regression (mutating a
returned array must not corrupt the next =compute()= result); the
time-update callback contract including the "no t given" =ValueError=; the
=TimeZeeman= auto-connection double described above; and that removing an
interaction changes the accumulated field array itself (not only the
reported energy), verified against the removed interaction's own
=compute_energy()= delta. A two-rank probe
(=src/finmag/tests/effective_field_dolfinx_mpi_probe.py=) checks the same
total-field/total-energy consistency, the copy contract, and the
remove-changes-field behavior collectively, with total energy independently
confirmed equal across both ranks.

=Simulation=, hysteresis, LLB, and normal-mode consumers of
=EffectiveField= remain unported.
