The new =dolfinx-src-effectivefield-pytest=/=dolfinx-src-effectivefield-mpi=
pixi tasks were deliberately *not* added to =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m4=: that
script is the frozen M4 prototype gate, and the existing convention (recorded
above under "Lazy Source Import Boundary" and followed by every
=dolfinx-src-*= task since) is that direct production-port gates stay
separate, focused tasks rather than being folded into the frozen aggregate
verifier. [Claude Sonnet 5]

** Direct DOLFINx LLG Port

Task 7 ports =src/finmag/physics/llg.py= directly to DOLFINx. The deterministic
core (=LLG=, scalar =Ms=, scalar =alpha=, =gamma=, =set_m=, =solve=,
=solve_for=, =pins=) is now pure NumPy on top of the ported =Field= and
=EffectiveField=; no compiled =finmag.native.llg= extension, =dolfin=,
=finmag.util.helpers=, or =finmag.util.meshes= import remains. Importing
=finmag.physics.llg= in the DOLFINx env pulls in only =numpy=,
=dolfinx.fem= (for the DG0 =Ms= space), =finmag.util.consts= (pure
constants), =finmag.field=, and =finmag.physics.effective_field=.

The exact node-local right-hand side was transcribed from the legacy native
kernel =calc_llg_dmdt= in =native/src/llg/llg.cc= (=damping_i= line 35,
=relaxation_i= line 105, =precession_i= line 74, assembled in the per-node loop
at lines 194-200). With =gamma_LL = gamma / (1 + alpha^2)= the update is

  dm/dt = -gamma_LL (m x H)
          - alpha gamma_LL (m x (m x H))
          + c (1 - |m|^2) m

The final term is the legacy numerical norm-relaxation correction: the native
code passes =0.1/char_time= as =relaxation_i='s coefficient, and =solve= sets
=char_time = 0.1/self.c=, so the coefficient is exactly =self.c = 1e11 1/s=. It
vanishes for unit-length m and holds |m| = 1 during integration. =precession_i=
is gated by =do_precession= exactly as in the native loop; the damping and
relaxation terms are always applied. Pinning zeroes dm/dt at the listed node
indices, matching the native =pin(...)= call.

Ordering is made unambiguous, resolving the raw/=xxx= ambiguity flagged in the
porting map: =solve=/=solve_for= and the =m= setters use the component-blocked,
coordinate-ordered =xxx= state vector, and =H_eff= (accumulated by the registry
in raw owned-dof order) is routed through a throwaway =Field.from_array= then
=get_ordered_numpy_array_xxx()= so m and H share one node ordering before the
node-local cross/dot products. Every RHS evaluation goes through
=self.effective_field.update(t)= and the accumulated =H_eff=; there is no bypass
path.

Constructor shape is preserved: =LLG(S1, S3, do_precession=True, average=False,
unit_length=1)=, where =S1=/=S3= are now DOLFINx scalar/3-component CG1
=fem.functionspace= objects instead of legacy =df.FunctionSpace=/
=df.VectorFunctionSpace=. =average= is still accepted-and-ignored exactly as in
the legacy constructor. =Ms= remains a property returning a scalar DG0 =Field=;
its setter interpolates the constant and refreshes the cached =_Ms=/=Ms_av=.

Deliberate deferrals raise =NotImplementedError= by name rather than silently
degrading: =use_slonczewski= and =use_zhangli= (spin-transfer torque),
=sundials_jtimes=/=sundials_psetup=/=sundials_psolve= (native CVODE
preconditioner/Jacobian paths), and =set_alpha= with a non-scalar value
(spatially varying damping). =sundials_rhs= is kept as a backend-neutral
=ydot[:] = solve_for(y, t)= adapter for the coming SciPy driver slice. Multi-rank
ODE state is out of scope: the =xxx= state vector is rank-local owned-only, so
=solve=/=solve_for= and the =sundials_m= setter raise a serial-only guard when
=comm.size > 1= (construction and non-state methods still work on multiple
ranks). Thermal/SLLG dynamics live in separate untouched modules.

Reference strategy follows the fixture spec: the macrospin case uses the
closed-form Landau-Lifshitz right-hand side (no fixture -- analytic result fully
fixes sign, scale, and units), while the nonuniform case uses a coordinate-
ordered legacy oracle fixture. The fixture
(=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/llg_rhs_nonuniform.json=, generated by
=gen_llg_rhs_nonuniform.py= run at the immutable oracle via
=dev/bin/run-legacy-oracle=) is built on a 1D =IntervalMesh(4, 0, 4)=: a
1D mesh has identical edge connectivity in =dolfin= and =dolfinx=, so the box-
method Exchange field is isolated from tetrahedralisation-diagonal differences
that a 3D box mesh would introduce. It carries a spatially varying 3-component
m with Exchange + Zeeman and stores coordinate-sorted m, H_eff and dm/dt.

New focused tests (=src/finmag/tests/test_llg_dolfinx.py=, 19 cases) cover: the
import boundary; the closed-form macrospin RHS; precession sign (negative
gyromagnetic convention, m x H carries +x toward +y about +z); damping rotating
m toward H; the precession rate equalling =gamma_LL * H= (physical time scale);
=do_precession=False= removing only the precession component; unit-length
invariance plus full-period precession return under RK4 integration of
=solve_for=; registry-driven RHS (adding/removing an interaction changes dm/dt);
pinning zeroing dm/dt with out-of-range rejection; the =xxx= =solve_for= round
trip; every deferred surface raising by name; the serial-only multi-rank guard
(via a mocked =comm=); and the nonuniform oracle fixture within its declared
tolerances.

Verified on 2026-07-21:

- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-src-llg-pytest= reported =19 passed=;
- regressions unchanged: =dolfinx-src-import-pytest= =6 passed, 2 skipped=,
  =dolfinx-src-field-pytest= =26 passed=, =dolfinx-src-energies-pytest= =26
  passed=, =dolfinx-src-effectivefield-pytest= =15 passed=,
  =dolfinx-prototype-pytest= =105 passed=.

The new =dolfinx-src-llg-pytest= pixi task follows the established convention of
keeping direct production-port gates as separate focused tasks rather than
folding them into the frozen M4 verifier. No MPI gate is added for this slice;
the serial-only guard is covered by a focused serial test instead.
=Simulation=, the SciPy/Sundials drivers, STT, thermal, and demag consumers
remain unported. [Claude Opus 4.8]

- Deliberate =set_pins= deviation: legacy =set_pins= logged
  =logger.error(...)= for out-of-range pin indices and silently kept the
  previous =_pins= array unchanged; the ported =set_pins= instead raises
  =ValueError= for the same condition. This is a fail-fast improvement --
  silently keeping stale pins on a bad index is a latent correctness trap --
  and is covered by =test_out_of_range_pins_raise=. [Claude Sonnet 5]

** Direct DOLFINx SciPy Driver

Task 8 ports =src/finmag/drivers/scipy_integrator.py= and updates
=src/finmag/drivers/llg_integrator.py= to reuse SciPy's =scipy.integrate.ode=
(VODE, methods =bdf=/=adams=) directly against the Task 7 deterministic
=LLG=. SciPy (=1.18.0=) was already pinned in the =dolfinx= pixi environment by
Task 1; this slice only verifies it (=pixi run -e dolfinx python -c "import
scipy; print(scipy.__version__)"= reports =1.18.0=) rather than re-adding it.

The recorded raw/=xxx= ambiguity is resolved, not preserved: the porting map's
"Field =xxx= consumers" note flagged that the legacy driver seeded the ODE
state from =m_field.as_array()= (raw backend dof order) while =rhs= passed
that same array straight into =llg.solve_for=, which has always interpreted
its argument as the component-blocked =xxx= state vector. On DOLFINx's
interleaved blocked dof layout the two orderings are genuinely different
(not just occasionally coincident, as on some legacy meshes), so this port
routes both directions -- the initial seed and every write-back after
=advance_time= -- through =Field.get_ordered_numpy_array_xxx()= /
=set_with_ordered_numpy_array_xxx()=. A dedicated regression test
(=test_scipy_integrator_seeds_ode_state_with_xxx_not_raw_array=) asserts the
ODE's initial state equals the =xxx= array and differs from the raw array on
a multi-vertex fixture mesh where the two are provably different; a second
test (=test_feeding_raw_order_into_solve_for_gives_wrong_physics=) shows that
feeding the raw array into =solve_for= as if it were =xxx= changes the
computed dm/dt, i.e. reintroducing the legacy conflation would silently
change the physics.

=advance_time='s public interface and tolerance keywords are unchanged:
=ScipyIntegrator(llg, m0, reltol=1e-6, abstol=1e-6, nsteps=10000,
method="bdf", tablewriter=None, **kwargs)=, the =t == 0= first-call no-op
(matching Sundials, since re-integrating to =t=0= from =t=0= is rejected by
the underlying solver), and =n_rhs_evals= all behave exactly as before.
Two behavioural gaps are closed rather than carried forward:

- =advance_time(t)= with =t < self.cur_t= now raises =ValueError= by name
  before touching =scipy.integrate.ode=, instead of only surfacing as a
  =vode= "Illegal input" warning followed by a bare, optimisable-away
  =assert=.
- an unsuccessful =ode.integrate(t)= call now raises =RuntimeError= (naming
  the method and target time) instead of =assert self.ode.successful()=,
  which =python -O= would silently compile out.

=reinit()= previously only logged "This integrator doesn't support
reinitialisation." It now does real work, mirroring what the Sundials reinit
contract exists to serve (see =sundials_reinit_test.py=): it rebuilds the
underlying =scipy.integrate.ode= object and reseeds it from
=self.m_field.get_ordered_numpy_array_xxx()= at the current =cur_t=
(preserved unchanged), so an external modification to the field (e.g. a
changed applied field between =reinit()= calls) actually takes effect on the
next =advance_time=. Without =reinit()=, =advance_time= only ever reads from
=scipy='s own internal solver state, so an external field mutation is
silently overwritten by the stale continuation on the very next step; a
paired test demonstrates both sides of this (with vs. without =reinit()=,
=test_reinit_makes_external_field_modification_take_effect=). =n_rhs_evals=
is deliberately left as a lifetime total across =reinit()= calls (its
docstring never claimed "since last reinit"), unlike the native Sundials
counter, which is an implementation detail of the compiled CVode object, not
part of the documented public contract.

A small stiff-ODE probe (=test_vode_bdf_stiff_probe_matches_analytic_solution=)
confirms the installed =vode=/=bdf= path directly against
=scipy.integrate.ode=, independent of =LLG=: for =y' = lam(y - cos t) - sin
t= with =lam = -1000=, the closed-form solution =y(t) = cos(t) + (y0 - 1)
exp(lam t)= is matched to =1e-6= at =t=1=, confirming the classic
Sundials-era stiff-solver path still behaves correctly under the modern
pinned SciPy.

Backend selection (checklist item 6): =llg_integrator(llg, m0,
backend="scipy", **kwargs)= keeps exactly the same public shape and
=backend= argument (no new selection mechanism); only the *default value*
changes, from =backend="sundials"= to =backend="scipy"=. The native Sundials
extension remains unported to the modern conda-forge/DOLFINx stack, so an
explicit =backend="sundials"= request keeps raising =ImportError= by name
(unchanged pre-existing code path), while callers who omit =backend=
entirely now get the working =ScipyIntegrator= instead of an import failure.
This only changes behaviour for callers that relied on the bare default:
every automated legacy oracle test and =finmag.sim.sim.Simulation= /
=sim_with= already pass =backend= explicitly (=integrator_backend="sundials"=
by their own separate default), so none of them are affected; only a couple
of unexercised example/=__main__= scripts (e.g.
=compute_domain_wall_cobalt()= in =domain_wall_cobalt.py=) rely on the bare
factory default, and are out of scope for this slice's gates.

New focused tests (=src/finmag/tests/test_scipy_driver_dolfinx.py=, 19 cases)
cover: the import boundary; the stiff VODE/BDF probe; constructor defaults
and the =t=0= no-op; =cur_t=/=m_field= updates and =n_rhs_evals= growth after
=advance_time=; macrospin relaxation towards an applied field within a
damping-time-scaled physical duration; the resolved raw/=xxx= ordering
contract (three tests, including the "would fail under raw ordering"
regression above); backward-time and unsuccessful-integration rejection;
=reinit()= taking effect (and preserving =cur_t=); and the
=llg_integrator= default/explicit/=sundials=/unknown-backend behaviour.

Verified on 2026-07-21:

- =pixi run -e dolfinx python -c "import scipy; print(scipy.__version__)"=
  reported =1.18.0=;
- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-src-scipy-pytest= reported =19 passed= with
  pristine output (only the pre-existing =finmag/util/plot.py=
  =SyntaxWarning=s appear; the expected =vode= "Excess work done" warning
  from the unsuccessful-integration test is asserted with
  =pytest.warns(...)= rather than left as loose noise);
- regressions unchanged: =dolfinx-src-import-pytest= =6 passed, 2 skipped=,
  =dolfinx-src-field-pytest= =26 passed=, =dolfinx-src-energies-pytest= =26
  passed=, =dolfinx-src-effectivefield-pytest= =15 passed=,
  =dolfinx-src-llg-pytest= =19 passed=, =dolfinx-prototype-pytest= =105
  passed=.

The new =dolfinx-src-scipy-pytest= pixi task follows the established
convention of a separate focused production-port gate. No MPI gate is added;
the serial-only guard is inherited unchanged from =LLG.solve=/=solve_for=.
=Simulation= and the native Sundials driver remain unported; =solve_ivp= is
deliberately not introduced (out of scope per the plan). [Claude Sonnet 5]

** First M5 / DOLFINx Core Simulation Slice

M5 starts with a reduced core-path wrapper rather than a legacy API port:

- =dev/dolfinx/field_adapter.py= introduces a narrow DOLFINx-backed
  compatibility probe for the first legacy =finmag.field.Field= behaviours:
  scalar/vector inspection, constants, callables, nodal values, normalisation,
  and volume averages
- =dev/dolfinx/simulation.py= introduces =PrototypeSimulation=, composing the
  checked M4 DOLFINx helpers for mesh creation, magnetisation setup,
  exchange/Zeeman/anisotropy energy reporting, average magnetisation, one-step
  explicit LLG updates, and short relaxation histories
- =dev/dolfinx/test_simulation.py= covers supported energy terms, relaxation
  energy decrease, custom parameters, and invalid wrapper controls
- the wrapper exposes a JSON-compatible state summary that reports mesh label,
  average magnetisation, parameters, and energy terms without advancing the
  simulation
- the reduced wrapper exposes a JSON-compatible relaxation summary as a basic
  output path for the first M5 core sketch
- the wrapper summary is checked with the existing relaxation summary validator
  and includes =dolfinx_version= plus schema version
- the wrapper can write the validated relaxation summary to JSON, matching the
  practical output path used by the M4 example
- the wrapper can emit a JSON-compatible per-step relaxation trace with
  absolute prototype time, average magnetisation, and energy terms; this is a
  small data-I/O contract, not legacy NDT, VTK/XDMF, or scheduler support
- the wrapper tracks prototype time and exposes a narrow =run_until(...)=
  method as a compatibility-shaped probe for legacy =Simulation.run_until=;
  this still uses the explicit prototype stepper and is not a production
  driver
- the wrapper can write and read a narrow JSON restart state for the
  =PrototypeSimulation= unit-square path; this captures prototype time, nodal
  magnetisation values, and parameters, not a general Finmag restart format
- =dev/dolfinx/restart_example.py= is a runnable restart-state round-trip
  witness, and the DOLFINx verification wrapper now runs it through the
  =dolfinx-restart-example= pixi task

This is intentionally not =finmag.Simulation= compatibility. It is a small API
sketch to make the next DOLFINx core path reviewable. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]

The M5 wrapper work also exposed a DOLFINx/UFL zero-form edge case. The
prototype exchange and anisotropy energy helpers now return =0.0= before form
assembly when their constants are zero, avoiding missing-domain errors for
degenerate UFL forms. Local verification: =51 passed= for =pixi run -e dolfinx
dolfinx-pytest=. [Codex gpt-5.5 high]

** Direct DOLFINx Core Simulation Port

Task 9 ports =src/finmag/sim/sim.py= directly to DOLFINx. The core
=Simulation= public surface -- construction on a DOLFINx mesh with scalar
=Ms=/=unit_length=/=name=/scalar =alpha=/scalar =gamma=; =set_m=, =m=,
=m_field=, =m_average=, =t=, =dmdt=; the interaction registry (=add=,
=get_interaction=, =interactions=/=get_interaction_list=,
=remove_interaction=, =has_interaction=, =total_energy=, =compute_energy=,
=effective_field=); integrator creation (lazy, on first =sim.integrator=
access), =set_tol=, =advance_time=, =run_until=, =reset_time=,
=reinit_integrator=; and the =sim_with= convenience factory for Exchange,
Zeeman and uniaxial anisotropy -- is preserved and driven entirely through the
already-ported =LLG=/=EffectiveField=/=Field=/=ScipyIntegrator= stack. No
compiled =finmag.native= extension, legacy =dolfin=, scheduler, table writer,
or per-simulation log file is imported; the module-scope imports are
=finmag.field=, =finmag.physics.llg=, =finmag.drivers.llg_integrator=, and the
ported =finmag.energies= Exchange/Zeeman/UniaxialAnisotropy.

The lazy export seam is updated: =finmag.__init__._LAZY_EXPORTS= now marks
=Simulation= and =sim_with= as =requires_legacy_dolfin=False=, so
=from finmag import Simulation, sim_with= resolves cleanly in the DOLFINx
environment without the legacy dolfin bridge. =finmag/sim/__init__.py='s
=from .init import *= (which imports legacy =dolfin=/=ffc= for console/file
logging setup) is wrapped in =try/except ImportError= so the package -- and
therefore =finmag.sim.sim= -- imports in the DOLFINx environment; when legacy
=dolfin= is present (the FEniCS environment) the bootstrap runs unchanged.

Deliberate deviations from the legacy module (all preserve the public names):

- =integrator_backend= defaults to ="scipy"= (was ="sundials"=) on both
  =Simulation= and =sim_with=, because the native Sundials/CVODE extension is
  unported; an explicit ="sundials"= request still raises =ImportError= by
  name via =llg_integrator=. This matches the Task 8 factory default change.
- =m= and =dmdt= return the component-blocked coordinate-ordered =xxx= arrays
  (matching the =LLG= state-vector contract and =sim.set_m='s numpy-array
  input), not raw backend dofs. On the legacy reorder-disabled DOLFIN meshes
  the two coincided; on DOLFINx's blocked layout they differ, so =xxx= is the
  faithful port. =dmdt= is read from =llg._dmdt= re-exported as =xxx= so its
  reshape((3, -1)) (and =dmdt_max=) stays component-consistent with =m=.
- =t= reports =0.0= until an integrator exists rather than the legacy
  =hasattr(self, "integrator")= path, which -- because =integrator= is a
  property that lazily *creates* the integrator -- silently built an
  integrator just to read the clock. The port guards on =has_integrator()=.
- =set_tol= updates the live integrator's =reltol=/=abstol= and calls
  =reinit()= so the new tolerances take effect; the SciPy driver has no
  in-place =set_scalar_tolerances= counterpart to the native one.
- =reset_time(t0)= rebuilds the integrator and reseeds =cur_t= and the VODE
  initial value at =t0= directly, because =ScipyIntegrator= takes no =t0=
  constructor kwarg.
- =reinit_integrator()= calls =integrator.reinit()= with no arguments; the
  legacy =debug= flag (also accepted and ignored by =set_m=) has no SciPy
  counterpart.
- =Ms= getter returns =llg.Ms= (the DG0 Field the EffectiveField and
  interactions already reference in place), and the setter delegates to
  =llg.Ms=, so an Ms update propagates to registered interactions without a
  duplicate Field.
- =Volume= is computed with a DOLFINx =assemble_scalar(Constant*dx)= MPI
  all-reduce instead of =finmag.util.meshes.mesh_volume=.
- No table writer / scheduler / per-simulation log file. =add= and
  =remove_interaction= are pure registry pass-throughs (no NDT-entity
  bookkeeping). =run_until= advances physical time straight through the
  integrator with no scheduler (scheduler integration is Task 12 scope).

Judgment-call deferrals (legacy public names retained but raising an explicit
by-name =NotImplementedError= when requested; none break import or the core
paths):

- construction options: =pbc='1d'/'2d'= (periodic boundaries), =parallel=True=
  (multi-rank), =kernel='sllg'= and =kernel='llg_stt'=;
- =sim_with= demag (=demag_solver=, default ="FK"= kept for signature fidelity
  but now raising -- pass =demag_solver=None= for a demag-free simulation) and
  DMI (=D=);
- STT: =set_stt=, =toggle_stt=, =set_zhangli=;
- scheduler: =schedule=, =unschedule=, =clear_schedule=;
- restart: =save_restart_data=, =restart= (Task 12 scope);
- output: =save_ndt=/=save_averages=, =save_m=, =save_field=, =save_vtk=,
  =save_field_to_vtk=, =snapshot=, =render_scene=, =plot_dynamics=(=_3d=),
  =plot_mesh=;
- region/material machinery: =mark_regions=, =save_m_in_region=,
  =get_submesh=, region-restricted =get_field_as_dolfin_function=;
- point probing: =probe_field=, =probe_field_along_line=;
- drivers/analyses: =relax=, =hysteresis=, =hysteresis_loop=,
  =run_normal_modes_computation=;
- =pins= callable masks (coordinate->dof mapping) -- the plain owned-index
  array form still delegates to =LLG.pins=.

Legacy public names removed outright, with no stub and no by-name
=NotImplementedError= (they now raise a plain =AttributeError=, as accepted
by review): the =magnetisation_patterns=-backed initialisers
=initialise_helix_2D=, =initialise_skyrmions=,
=initialise_skyrmion_hexlattice_2D=, =initialise_vortex=; the
=sim_helpers=-backed =skyrmion_number=/=skyrmion_number_density_function=;
the =sim_details=-backed =length_scales=/=mesh_info=; =profile= (cProfile
wrapper); =close_logfile= (per-simulation log handler teardown); and the
=instances_*= class-level instance-management family (=instances_list_all=,
=instances_delete_all=, =instances_delete_all_others=,
=instances_alive_count=) plus =shutdown=; and, on the ported =LLG= itself, the
legacy full-moment =M=/=M_average= properties (A/m-scaled magnetisation,
superseded by the unit =m=/=m_average= contract) and the STT enable flags
=do_slonczewski=/=do_zhangli= (STT remains deferred behind =set_stt=/
=set_zhangli= raising by name; the underlying flags are not retained as dead
state). [Claude Sonnet 5]

Dead legacy branches inside the touched core methods were removed rather than
left half-referencing deleted helpers (the parallel/llg_stt/sllg integrator
branches, the =import ipdb= double-create branch, the tablewriter-entity
registration in =add=/=remove_interaction=/=save_m_in_region=, the instance
=shutdown=/cyclic-reference machinery, and the =dt=/=T= sllg-only properties).
Lightweight instance accounting (=instance_id=, =instances=) is retained with
no cyclic references. Long-tail modules outside the core import graph
(=sim_helpers=, =sim_savers=, =hysteresis=, =magnetisation_patterns=, the
legacy scheduler) are left untouched; the core =sim.py= no longer imports
them.

New focused tests (=src/finmag/tests/test_simulation_dolfinx.py=, 28 cases)
cover the import boundary; construction and core state (mesh, scalar Ms
average, unit_length, name, default alpha/gamma, Volume); scalar alpha/gamma
round-trip and the spatially-varying-alpha rejection; =set_m= (constant and
callable) with =m=/=m_field=/=m_average= accessors and node-wise unit norm;
=t=-before-integration (asserting no silent integrator creation); =dmdt=
shape; the interaction registry pass-through and energy accessors; lazy
integrator creation, =set_tol=, =advance_time=, =reset_time=, and
=reinit_integrator=-without-integrator; the end-to-end =run_until(1e-11)=
relaxation with Exchange + Zeeman + UniaxialAnisotropy on a small 3D box
(asserting node-wise unit norm to 1e-5, a directional tilt of =<m>= toward the
+z field/easy axis, and a physical average magnitude); =sim_with= building the
three ported interactions; and every deferral above (pbc, parallel,
sllg/llg_stt kernels, scheduler, STT, restart, output, regions, hysteresis,
sim_with demag/DMI).

The import-boundary suite is extended: =finmag.Simulation= no longer reports a
missing =dolfin= in the DOLFINx environment (that assertion moved to the still
unported =finmag.NormalModeSimulation=), and a new
=test_ported_public_names_bypass_legacy_dolfin= asserts =Simulation= and
=sim_with= import with no =dolfin=/=finmag.native= in =sys.modules=.

Verified on 2026-07-21 (=pixi run -e dolfinx=):

- =dolfinx-src-simulation-pytest= reported =28 passed=;
- regressions unchanged/green: =dolfinx-src-import-pytest= =7 passed, 2
  skipped= (was 6/2 before the added ported-export test),
  =dolfinx-src-field-pytest= =26 passed=, =dolfinx-src-energies-pytest= =26
  passed=, =dolfinx-src-effectivefield-pytest= =15 passed=,
  =dolfinx-src-llg-pytest= =19 passed=, =dolfinx-src-scipy-pytest= =19
  passed=, =dolfinx-prototype-pytest= =105 passed=;
- =git diff --check= clean; only intended files changed.

The new =dolfinx-src-simulation-pytest= pixi task follows the established
focused-source-gate convention. No MPI gate is added; the serial-only guard is
inherited from =LLG.solve=/=solve_for= (and =Simulation(parallel=True)= raises
by name). Demag, DMI, PBC, stochastic kernels, STT, scheduler, restart,
NDT/VTK output, regions, hysteresis, normal modes, and the native Sundials
driver remain unported. [Claude Opus 4.8]

** First Direct-Source DOLFINx Gate (Task 10, "M5")

Task 10 is an aggregation/verification slice over Tasks 3-9, not a new porting
slice. It adds no new production =src/finmag= behaviour -- only a new
verifier script, two new pixi tasks/test files, and this documentation.

*** Naming decision

The notes above already call the Task 9 core-=Simulation= slice "M5" (see
"First M5 / DOLFINx Core Simulation Slice" and "Direct DOLFINx Core
Simulation Port"). The new aggregate verifier is therefore named
=dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=, following the exact =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m4=
convention (frozen prototype gate) rather than inventing a
=verify-dolfinx-src-core= name. The CI workflow mirrors this as
=.github/workflows/dolfinx-m5.yml=, added because =dolfinx-m4.yml= already
proves the isolated DOLFINx pixi environment runs in this repository's CI (no
new CI infrastructure was introduced; the workflow only calls the new
verifier, exactly like the =python3-m2=/=python3-m3=/=dolfinx-m4= workflows
call their verifiers).

*** What the gate aggregates

=dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= reuses
=dev/bin/validation-common.sh='s tracked-diff cleanliness guard (the same
mechanism as every other verifier here) and then runs, in order:
=dolfinx-versions=; =dolfinx-src-import=; every existing focused
=dolfinx-src-*-pytest= gate (=import-boundary=, =field=, =energies=,
=effectivefield=, =llg=, =scipy=, =simulation=); every existing MPI probe
(=dolfinx-src-field-mpi=, =dolfinx-src-energies-mpi=,
=dolfinx-src-effectivefield-mpi=); and two new Task 10 witnesses:
=dolfinx-src-deferred-pytest= and =dolfinx-src-core-smoke=. Because the script
uses =set -euo pipefail=, any single sub-gate failure stops the run and
propagates a nonzero exit; this was verified directly (see "Fail-path
evidence" below) rather than only asserted.

*** New witness 1: the core smoke (=dolfinx-src-core-smoke=)

=src/finmag/tests/simulation_dolfinx_smoke.py= is a new executable-module
witness (runnable as =python -m finmag.tests.simulation_dolfinx_smoke=,
matching the existing =*_mpi_probe.py= convention rather than a pytest file).
It builds the Task 9 end-to-end workflow -- a small 3D tetrahedral box,
=Ms=8.6e5=, =unit_length=1e-9=, =alpha=0.5=, =m_init=(1,0,0)=, Exchange
(=A=13e-12=), Zeeman (=H_ext=(0,0,1e6)=), and =UniaxialAnisotropy=
(=K1=1e5=, axis =(0,0,1)=) on the default SciPy backend -- and calls
=sim.run_until(1e-12)=. The SciPy/VODE driver steps adaptively; =run_until=
does not take a fixed =dt=. It prints a short JSON witness and asserts
=sim.t >= 1e-12= and a nodal unit-norm tolerance before printing, so a failing
physics/tolerance check exits nonzero (a plain Python =assert=, no
try/except). Measured witness on 2026-07-21:

#+begin_example
{"integrator_backend": "scipy", "m_average": [0.9802594013891642,
0.1766200460318399, 0.08889758054126375], "max_unit_norm_deviation":
2.957244739087983e-06, "t": 1e-12, "t_target": 1e-12}
#+end_example

The unit-norm tolerance is =1e-5= (matching the tolerance already accepted in
=test_simulation_dolfinx.py::test_run_until_relaxes_towards_field= for the
same integrator/relaxation combination); the measured deviation at this very
short physical time is =~3e-6=, comfortably inside it.

Observation, not a defect: running this module directly (rather than through
pytest) makes visible a pre-existing, harmless stdout/stderr side effect --
=finmag.drivers.llg_integrator= eagerly *attempts* (at import time, not at
=backend="sundials"= call time) to import
=finmag.drivers.sundials_integrator=, which triggers the legacy native-module
Makefile probe; it fails (no CVODE headers in this environment) and the
build's compiler output is printed by =finmag.util.native_compiler.run_make=
before the failure is caught and stored as
=SUNDIALS_INTEGRATOR_IMPORT_ERROR= (Task 8's by-name =ImportError= mechanism).
Every =dolfinx-src-*-pytest= gate above triggers the identical probe on
collection; pytest's default output capture just hides it there. Nothing
observes this noise as a functional problem (exit code stayed =0= throughout),
so it is not treated as a defect to fix in this aggregation-only slice.

*** New witness 2: the deferred-surfaces sweep (=dolfinx-src-deferred-pytest=)

=src/finmag/tests/test_deferred_surfaces_dolfinx.py= is the single place a
reviewer can check "unsupported features fail by name, not through incidental
import errors" (Task 10 checklist item 5) without hunting across five
per-slice suites. Investigating the brief's headline list first showed every
item already has a dedicated by-name assertion somewhere:

- demag request via =sim_with(demag_solver=...)= default -- already pinned in
  =test_simulation_dolfinx.py::test_sim_with_default_demag_is_deferred_by_name=
- DMI via =sim_with(D=...)= -- already pinned in
  =::test_sim_with_dmi_is_deferred_by_name=
- scheduler, restart, STT, =kernel="sllg"=/="llg_stt"=, =parallel=True= --
  already pinned in =test_simulation_dolfinx.py=
  (=test_scheduler_api_is_deferred=, =test_restart_is_deferred=,
  =test_stt_is_deferred=, =test_nonstandard_kernels_are_deferred=,
  =test_parallel_flag_is_deferred=)
- native Sundials/STT/thermal/multi-rank =LLG= state paths -- already pinned
  in =test_llg_dolfinx.py=
- =TimeZeeman= instantiation -- already pinned in =test_energies_dolfinx.py=
- =Field.from_expression= -- already pinned in
  =test_field_dolfinx.py::test_legacy_only_features_fail_precisely=
- =backend="sundials"= at the bare =llg_integrator(...)= factory -- already
  pinned in
  =test_scipy_driver_dolfinx.py::test_llg_integrator_sundials_backend_raises_by_name=

The new sweep file therefore does not duplicate any of those; it documents
each with a direct cross-reference and adds only genuinely new coverage: the
=integrator_backend="sundials"= path reached through =Simulation=
construction/=create_integrator=/=sim_with= (only the bare factory call was
pinned before), each asserting the same by-name =ImportError= the factory
already raises.

Investigating this sweep also surfaced one pre-existing, out-of-scope gap,
which the sweep documents rather than silently omitting or fixing: accessing
a still-fully-unported optional energy class directly (=finmag.energies.Demag=,
=DMI=, =CubicAnisotropy=, =ThinFilmDemag=, =FixedEnergyDW=, =Demag2D=,
=MacroGeometry= -- every =requires_legacy_dolfin=True= lazy export) surfaces
the raw =ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'dolfin'= rather than a curated
message, because these modules still import legacy =dolfin= at module scope
and are not ported at all yet (Task 11+/"Later Value-Driven Slices" scope).
This is distinct from -- and does not contradict -- the demag/DMI *request*
paths above: =Simulation=/=sim_with= intercept those requests and raise a
curated =NotImplementedError= before ever reaching these modules. The gap is
a pre-existing Task 3 design point ("load only when requested"), not a
regression introduced by this gate; =test_deferred_surfaces_dolfinx.py::test_direct_legacy_only_energy_import_is_a_known_gap_not_this_slices_scope=
pins the current behaviour explicitly instead of leaving it undocumented, and
=dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md='s Near-Term Gaps section cross-references it.
No production code changes accompany this slice; per the Task 10 brief's
global constraints, a real defect found while wiring the gate is reported
rather than patched here -- this is exactly that case, and it is judged minor
and pre-existing rather than blocking.

*** Fail-path evidence

The Task 10 verification step requires evidence that the aggregate fails when
a sub-gate fails, not just an assertion that it would. This was demonstrated
directly rather than only argued from =set -e= semantics: the core smoke's
unit-norm tolerance was temporarily edited to an impossible value
(=UNIT_NORM_TOL = 1e-30=) in the working tree, =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= was
re-run and exited =1= with the =AssertionError= surfaced through the failing
=pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-src-core-smoke= step, and the file was then
restored to its original =1e-5= tolerance (confirmed identical to the
pre-edit copy by diff) before anything was committed. Every other sub-gate's
failure path is the same =pixi run= non-zero-exit-under-=set -e= propagation
already relied on by =verify-dolfinx-m4= and the =verify-python3-*= family; it
was not separately broken and restored for each one.

*** Supported API surface (this gate)

- =finmag= top-level lazy exports: =Simulation=, =sim_with=, =Field=, version
  access, plus =finmag.energies.{Exchange, Zeeman, UniaxialAnisotropy,
  TimeZeeman-and-siblings-as-deferred-stubs}=.
- =Field=: constants, callables, =set=, =from_array=, =from_field=,
  =from_function=, scalar/vector inspection, raw arrays, coordinate-ordered
  =xyz=/=xxx=, volume averages, normalisation, VTK/XDMF output (write-only).
  Serial and two-rank owned/ghost contracts are tested.
- =Exchange=/=Zeeman=/=UniaxialAnisotropy=: scalar =A=/=K1=/=K2=/constant
  axis/constant field only; box-assemble is the only supported energy method.
- =EffectiveField=: full registry (=add=/=get=/=exists=/=all=/=remove=, total
  field/energy, time-update callbacks).
- =LLG=: scalar =Ms=/=alpha=, =gamma=, =set_m=, =solve=/=solve_for=, serial
  only, pinning retained.
- Driver: SciPy is the default and only supported DOLFINx backend
  (=integrator_backend="scipy"=); real reinitialisation from current state.
- =Simulation=/=sim_with=: construction, scalar =Ms=/=unit_length=/=name=/
  =alpha=/=gamma=, =set_m=/=m=/=m_field=/=m_average=/=t=/=dmdt=, the
  interaction registry and energy accessors, integrator
  creation/tolerances/=advance_time=/=run_until=/=reset_time=/
  =reinit_integrator=, and =sim_with= for Exchange/Zeeman/UniaxialAnisotropy.

*** Deferred surfaces (this gate)

Pointer to the authoritative lists: =dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md='s "Near-Term
Gaps" section and the "Judgment-call deferrals"/"Legacy public names removed
outright" lists in the "Direct DOLFINx Core Simulation Port" section above.
Headline items, all raising a curated =NotImplementedError=/=ImportError= by

=integrator_backend="sundials"=. Known non-goal documented but not fixed here:

[Claude Sonnet 5]

** Native array-only FK BEM for DOLFINx (Task 11a)

Task 11a is the first half of "Port FK demag" (plan items 1-2 only): separate
the array-only LLG/BEM native bindings from the legacy SWIG-DOLFIN mesh
converters and the =-ldolfin= link dependency, and rebuild/test that surface on
Python 3.12 without installing legacy DOLFIN. The remaining items (DOLFINx
boundary-mesh probes, porting =src/finmag/energies/demag=, oracle BEM
comparisons, the barmini FK-demag workflow) are Task 11b and are *not* started
here.

*** The binding-separation problem

The compiled FK BEM lived in =native/src/llg/bem.cc=, registered by the =llg=
module (=native/src/llg/py_llg_module.cc=), which links =-ldolfin= and calls
=finmag::util::register_dolfin_swig_converters()=. =bem.cc= contained both the
legacy DOLFIN =BoundaryMesh= entry points (=compute_bem<>= using
=df::BoundaryMesh=, =CellIterator=, etc.) and the array-based
=compute_bem_from_arrays<>= / =compute_lindholm_formula<>= kernels added during
the M3/FEniCS-2019 work. The array kernels touch only =np_array= and =vector3=,
but they shared a translation unit and an umbrella header (=finmag_includes.h=,
which unconditionally =#include <dolfin.h>= and =#include <cvode/cvode.h>=) with
the DOLFIN-coupled code, so nothing array-only could compile in the =dolfinx=
env (no libdolfin, no SUNDIALS, Python 3.12).

*** Module/target design

Following the established pixi Boost.Python pattern (how =sundials.so= is handled
as a conditional pixi module in =native/Makefile=), a *new* module target was
added rather than mutating the legacy =llg.so= target, so the legacy M3 lane
keeps building unchanged:

- =native/src/llg/bem_arrays.h= (new): the array-only Lindholm/BEM template
  kernels (=lindholm_formula<>=, =compute_bem_from_arrays<>=,
  =compute_lindholm_formula<>=), moved verbatim out of =bem.cc= into a shared,
  dolfin-free header. Single source of truth; no numerical change.
- =native/src/llg/bem_arrays.cc= (new): =BOOST_PYTHON_MODULE(bem_arrays)=
  registering =compute_bem_fk_from_arrays=, =compute_bem_gcr_from_arrays=,
  =compute_lindholm_L=, =compute_lindholm_K=. No dolfin/SWIG-DOLFIN symbols.
- =native/src/llg/bem.cc= (edited): now =#include "bem_arrays.h"= and keeps only
  the DOLFIN =BoundaryMesh= path and the combined =register_bem()=; the legacy
  =llg= surface is byte-for-byte behaviourally unchanged (verified: it still
  reproduces the same golden Lindholm/BEM values after the refactor).
- =native/src/finmag_includes.h= (edited): =#include <dolfin.h>= and
  =#include <cvode/cvode.h>= are guarded by =FINMAG_NO_DOLFIN= /
  =FINMAG_NO_SUNDIALS=; when dolfin.h is skipped, the standard headers that used
  to arrive transitively through it (=<iostream>=, =<vector>=, =<string>=,
  =<stdexcept>=, =<csignal>=, ...) are pulled in explicitly.
- =native/src/llg/vector3.h= (edited): the two =dolfin::Vertex= / =dolfin::Point=
  constructors (used only by the legacy BoundaryMesh path) are guarded by
  =FINMAG_NO_DOLFIN=.
- =native/Makefile= (edited): a new =IS_DOLFINX_ENV= branch (matched on
  =/envs/dolfinx= in the Python prefix, checked *before* the general =/.pixi/=
  branch) sets =MODULES = bem_arrays.so=, restricts =SOURCES= to
  =bem_arrays.cc= + =np_array.cc=, and adds
  =-DFINMAG_NO_DOLFIN -DFINMAG_NO_SUNDIALS= to =CFLAGS_NOPCH= (so it also applies
  to the precompiled-header build). The =bem_arrays.so= target links only
  =bem_arrays.o np_array.o= with empty LDFLAGS -- no =-ldolfin=, no
  =swig_dolfin.o=. The legacy =llg.so=/=sundials.so=/=llb.so= branch is
  untouched.

*** Toolchain / pixi.lock

The base =dolfinx= feature shipped =gcc= and boost headers but no =g++=, no
=make=, and no =libboost_python=. Added to =[feature.dolfinx.dependencies]=:
=cxx-compiler=, =make=, =libboost-python-devel= (provides =libboost_python312.so=
matching boost 1.88). This regenerated =pixi.lock= (dolfinx env only; the
default FEniCS-2019 env is unchanged). This is a native-build toolchain
addition kept off the runtime import path.

Also added =[feature.dolfinx.activation.env] WRITE_FINMAG_VERSION = "0"=: the
DOLFINx lane can now compile a native surface, and importing finmag can trigger
that =make= as a side effect, so this keeps implicit builds from rewriting the
tracked =src/finmag/__version__.py= (the read-only validators already use this
convention).

*** Import-boundary fix (no native leak on the plain import path)

Making the native build *succeed* in the DOLFINx env exposed a latent coupling:
=finmag/drivers/llg_integrator.py= eagerly did
=from finmag.drivers.sundials_integrator import SundialsIntegrator= at module
scope, which imports =finmag.native=. On HEAD this stayed clean only *by
accident* -- the native build failed in the DOLFINx env, so Python unwound the
partial =finmag.native= import. With an array-only surface now buildable, the
=finmag.native= package =__init__= succeeds (building =bem_arrays.so=) before the
still-unported =sundials= submodule fails, leaving =finmag.native= in
=sys.modules= and violating the "plain import loads no native modules" boundary
asserted by =test_import_boundary= / =test_scipy_driver_dolfinx= /
=test_deferred_surfaces_dolfinx=.

Fix (=llg_integrator.py=): the sundials availability probe is now lazy. A PEP 562
module =__getattr__= resolves =SundialsIntegrator= /
=SUNDIALS_INTEGRATOR_IMPORT_ERROR= on first *access* (so the test sentinels and
the =backend="sundials"= raise-by-name path are unchanged and still reflect real
availability), and the plain =import finmag= / =sim_with= path never touches the
attribute, hence never imports =finmag.native=. When the probe fails, any
=finmag.native*= modules it added to =sys.modules= are rolled back, reproducing
HEAD's "graceful sundials absence with no native residue" without depending on a
build failure. On the legacy stack the probe succeeds and nothing is removed.

*** Test / verification evidence (2026-07-21, =pixi run -e dolfinx=)

New focused test =src/finmag/tests/test_native_bem_arrays_dolfinx.py= + pixi task
=dolfinx-src-native-bem-pytest= (=WRITE_FINMAG_VERSION=0=). The golden values are
captured from the known-good compiled =finmag.native.llg= in the FEniCS-2019
lane (the =dev/bin/native-fk-bem-smoke.py= reference path). Coverage:

- module imports in the dolfinx env with =dolfin not in sys.modules= and no
  =finmag.native.llg=;
- =compute_bem_fk_from_arrays= on the =BoundaryMesh(UnitCubeMesh(1,1,1))= arrays
  (8 vertices, 12 triangles) reproduces the legacy compiled BEM matrix to
  =atol=1e-14= (exact equivalence), plus finite/shape and =b2g= passthrough;
- solid-angle identity: FK BEM row sums equal =-1= (=B @ 1 = -1=, the
  Fredkin-Koehler double-layer-of-a-constant identity) to =atol=1e-12=;
- diagonal entries are negative (=solid_angle/4pi - 1 < 0=; uniform =-0.875= for
  the congruent cube corners);
- =compute_bem_gcr_from_arrays= finite; =compute_lindholm_L= matches the legacy
  golden and is invariant under a rigid rotation applied to all four points;
  =compute_lindholm_K= finite.

Result: =dolfinx-src-native-bem-pytest= = =8 passed=. RED evidence: before the
Makefile/source wiring, the same task failed with
=ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'finmag.native.bem_arrays'=.

Regression gates (all green): =dolfinx-src-import-pytest= =8 passed, 2 skipped=;
full =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= exit =0= with the tracked-diff guard reporting
no tracked-file changes; legacy =dev/bin/verify-python3-native-fk-bem-smoke=
green from a clean native build (=llg.so= with full DOLFIN coupling rebuilds and
=compute_bem_fk_from_arrays= still returns the expected =(8, 8)= matrix);
=git diff --check= clean.

The Task 11 plan checkboxes are intentionally left unticked; items 1-2 are
delivered by 11a and the whole of Task 11 is ticked when 11b completes the FK
demag capability.

[Claude Opus 4.8]

** Direct DOLFINx FK Demag Port (Task 11b)

Task 11b is the second half of "Port FK demag": the direct DOLFINx port of the
Fredkin-Koehler solver in =src/finmag/energies/demag/fk_demag.py=, wired into
=Simulation.add(Demag())= / =sim_with(demag_solver="FK")=, with all of Task 11's
plan checkboxes now ticked across 11a+11b.

*** Formulation mapping (legacy step -> DOLFINx step)

The discrete two-potential formulation is preserved verbatim; only the FEM-API
layer changed.

- =S1 = df.FunctionSpace(mesh, "Lagrange", 1)= -> =associated_scalar_space(m.functionspace)=.
- Neumann stiffness =assemble(dot(grad(u),grad(v))*dx)= -> PETSc
  =assemble_matrix(poisson_form)=; a constant =PETSc.NullSpace= is attached for
  the singular pure-Neumann =phi_1= solve (the additive constant is irrelevant
  to =H=-grad(phi)=).
- Legacy =KrylovSolver= (default CG) -> =PETSc.KSP= CG with the legacy tolerance
  dict mapped to =rtol/atol/max_it= and the legacy preconditioner name mapped to
  a PETSc PC (=default->hypre=, =ilu=, =none=, ...). =solver_type='LU'= is
  deferred by name (the singular Neumann matrix has no direct factorisation
  without pinning); =macrogeometry= / =MacroGeometry= / =Demag2D= / non-FK
  solvers raise =NotImplementedError= by name.
- =_Ms_times_divergence * m.vector= (matrix-vector) -> a linear form
  =assemble_vector(Ms.f*inner(m.f, grad(v))*dx)= re-assembled each solve (m
  changes); likewise the gradient =_gradient * phi.vector= -> linear form
  =assemble_vector(inner(v3, -grad(phi))*dx)= divided by unscaled nodal volumes.
  This avoids assembling rectangular S3<->S1 operators and matches the box
  pattern already used by =energy_base.py=.
- =phi_2= Dirichlet data: =DirichletBC(S1, phi_2, DomainBoundary())= ->
  =fem.dirichletbc(g_bc, boundary_dofs)= where =g_bc= is a Function whose
  boundary dofs are set to =B @ phi_1|_bnd= each solve; the stiffness matrix is
  assembled once with the BC, and each solve applies =apply_lifting= + =set_bc=
  to the (zero) RHS.
- Energy =-0.5 mu0 int H.M dx * unit_length**dim= and the lumped nodal energy
  density are assembled exactly as before (the density's =unit_length**dim=
  numerator/denominator cancel, so it is assembled in mesh units).

*** Boundary-ordering contract and its pinning evidence

The one genuinely new mechanic is =boundary_bem_arrays(domain, S1)=, which feeds
the compiled =compute_bem_fk_from_arrays= (Task 11a). The contract:

- BEM-local node =i= is a *CG1 boundary degree of freedom* of S1
  (=locate_dofs_topological= on the exterior facets); =coords[i]= is that dof's
  coordinate and =b2g[i]= is its S1 dof index, so =phi.x.array[b2g]= gathers
  boundary values in BEM-local order and the reverse scatter writes them back
  (no vertex/dof confusion -- the whole pipeline stays in S1 dof space).
- Boundary triangles are taken from the exterior facets and *explicitly
  oriented outward* (flip the winding when =cross(p1-p0,p2-p0).(p0-p_opposite) <
  0=); the sorted-index winding DOLFINx returns from facet->vertex connectivity
  is *not* consistent and gives a wrong (though row-sum -1) BEM.
- BEM-local indices for each triangle vertex are resolved by *coordinate*, so
  the mapping is robust to any DOLFINx submesh/entity reordering.

Pinned bit-for-bit: on the exact legacy Kuhn (main-diagonal) split of the unit
cube -- and, separately, on =create_unit_cube(1,1,1)=, which DOLFINx builds with
the same split -- the extracted BEM equals the Task 11a golden matrix to
=atol=1e-13= after the coordinate-induced row/col permutation
(=test_extraction_reproduces_golden_bem_*=). Without the outward-orientation
step the max entry error is ~0.18; with it, ~1e-17.

*** Fixture provenance

=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/fk_demag_oracle.json= holds coordinate-ordered
(lexicographic xyz) legacy references generated at the frozen oracle commit
=ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687= via =dev/bin/run-legacy-oracle= (the
unmodified pre-port =fk_demag.py=, which is byte-identical to the oracle commit).
Two cases: a uniformly magnetised =UnitCubeMesh(4,4,4)= (m=(1,0,0), Ms=8e5) and
the barmini bar =BoxMesh(Point(0,0,0),Point(3,3,10),2,2,4)= (m=(1,0,1),
Ms=0.86e6), unit_length=1e-9. The BEM golden matrix itself is reused from the
11a fixture. The cube also carries an *analytic* anchor stronger than any
numeric oracle: average demag field =-Ms/3= and energy =mu0 Ms^2 V / 6= (cube
demag factor 1/3 per axis).

*** Measured results (2026-07-21, =pixi run -e dolfinx=, serial)

DOLFINx =create_box=/=create_unit_cube= reproduce the legacy =BoxMesh=/
=UnitCubeMesh= vertex-for-vertex, so the comparison is coordinate-identical:

- cube (4x4x4): energy rel diff =4.7e-8=; average-field rel diff ~=5e-6=;
  pointwise field max abs diff / |H|_max = =6.9e-6=.
- barmini (2x2x4): energy rel diff =2.3e-9=; average-field rel diff ~=1e-7=;
  pointwise = =4.7e-6=.

The residual ~5e-6 was *suspected* to be the Krylov tolerance (=1e-6=), not the
method -- but simply tightening the port's own KSP rtol to =1e-12= alone left
the pointwise gap unchanged at =6.4e-6= (review round 1, Finding 1), which
falsified that claim as originally stated. The actual cause was demonstrated
empirically instead of asserted: the *oracle fixture itself* is frozen at the
legacy default =1e-6= Krylov tolerance. Regenerating the cube datum at the
oracle with both legacy Krylov solves (=phi_1=, =phi_2=) tightened to =1e-12=
and comparing it against the port at the same tightened KSP tolerance collapses
the pointwise gap to ~=1.4e-12= and the energy gap to ~=5.5e-14= -- see
=cube_tight_tolerance= in the regenerated
=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/fk_demag_oracle.json= (schema v1,
=gen_fk_demag_oracle.py=) and
=test_oracle_cube_tight_tolerance_isolates_krylov_residual=. This proves the
standard-tolerance residual is the oracle's own frozen 1e-6 solves, not a
systematic difference between the port and the legacy formulation. Declared
gate tolerances: energy rel =1e-5=, average rel =1e-3=, pointwise/|H|max =1e-4=,
BEM golden =1e-13=. The barmini =sim_with(..., demag_solver="FK")= workflow
builds, computes demag energy =9.99e-21 J=, and integrates
(=run_until(5e-12)=) with compiled FK demag present. The uniform-cube analytic
checks hold on a 6x6x6 mesh (avg Hx within 10% of =-Ms/3=, energy within 10% of
=mu0 Ms^2 V/6=; the coarse-mesh FK field converges toward the continuum result
from above).

*** Makefile build-hygiene (both lanes)

The three mandatory 11a-review carry-ins are done and verified on *both* lanes:

1. Per-env object tree =native/obj/$(PYTHON_PREFIX_TAG)/= and per-env
   =PRECOMPILED_HEADER_DIR=, keyed on the active Python prefix, so the legacy
   (libdolfin, NumPy-1 ABI) and DOLFINx (NumPy-2, =-DFINMAG_NO_DOLFIN=) builds
   compile shared units such as =np_array.cc= into disjoint paths and can never
   silently cross-link. Verified: the DOLFINx lane rebuilds =bem_arrays.so= and
   passes =dolfinx-src-native-bem-pytest=; the legacy lane rebuilds the full
   =-ldolfin= native surface and =dev/bin/verify-python3-native-fk-bem-smoke=
   returns the expected =(8,8)= matrix -- objects land in
   =obj/..._envs_default/= vs =obj/..._envs_dolfinx/= respectively. (This is
   also the exact hazard that made a co-collected pytest run fail: loading the
   stale NumPy-1 =sundials.so= into a NumPy-2 process corrupts the array ABI so
   the NumPy-2 =bem_arrays= rejects arrays; the per-env split and separate gate
   processes both avoid it.)
2. =bem_arrays.cc= is excluded from the legacy branches' =SOURCES= (it is
   compiled but never linked there).
3. =dolfinx-src-native-bem-pytest= and the new =dolfinx-src-demag-pytest= are
   folded into =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=.

*** Deferred / deliberate deviations

=solver_type='LU'=, =macrogeometry=/=MacroGeometry=, =Demag2D=, and the
=Treecode=/=GCR= solvers all raise =NotImplementedError= by name. The Task 10
fail-forward pins that expected =ModuleNotFoundError= for =Demag()= /
=sim_with(demag_solver="FK")= are replaced with curated-behaviour assertions
(=Demag()= now builds an =FKDemag=; the still-unported =DMI=/=CubicAnisotropy=/
=ThinFilmDemag=/=FixedEnergyDW= remain the documented Task 3 gap). The
=test_ported_simulation_does_not_load_legacy_dolfin_or_native= import-boundary
check now runs in a fresh subprocess so it is order-independent once the opt-in
demag interaction has loaded =finmag.native= elsewhere in the suite.

[Claude Opus 4.8]

** Direct DOLFINx Restart and Output Port (Task 12)

The final core slice ports restart persistence, the required output surfaces
(NDT tables and VTK/XDMF), and the scheduler directly onto the DOLFINx
=Simulation=. Everything is wired through the already-ported =Field=/=LLG=/
=ScipyIntegrator= stack; no legacy =dolfin=, =finmag.native=, or =aeon= enters
the DOLFINx import graph. Gate: =dolfinx-src-restart-output-pytest=
(=src/finmag/tests/test_restart_output_dolfinx.py=, 20 tests), folded into
=dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=.

*** Restart format decision (explicit deviation)

The legacy npz restart archive (=sim_helpers.save_restart_data=) stored the
magnetisation as =sim.integrator.llg._m_field.get_numpy_array_debug()= -- a
*raw, backend-dof-ordered* owned array. That layout is only meaningful for the
identical in-memory function-space dof numbering, so restarting into a
differently-ordered rebuild of the same mesh silently misassigns nodal values.
This is exactly the raw/=xxx=/coordinate ambiguity earlier slices resolved
explicitly.

The DOLFINx port therefore stores a *coordinate-aware* format (=format_version
= 2=; v1 = the legacy raw-dof layout):

- =coordinates= : owned mesh-vertex coordinates =(n, gdim)=;
- =m=           : coordinate-ordered magnetisation =(n, 3)=, row-aligned to
  =coordinates= (from =Field.coords_and_values()=);
- =mesh_hash=   : sha1 of the sorted/rounded coordinates (mesh identity);
- =Ms=/=alpha=/=gamma=/=unit_length= : material parameters;
- =interactions= : sorted interaction names present;
- =simtime=/=datetime=/=simname=/=driver='scipy'=/=format_version=.

On =restart= the stored values are remapped to the target field *by coordinate*
(=apply_restart_magnetisation=): a same-recipe rebuild with different vertex/dof
numbering is correctly restored, and a genuine mesh mismatch raises =ValueError=
(=restart mesh mismatch: ...=) rather than being silently misassigned. This is a
deliberate, documented deviation from the legacy raw-dof =m= key; =driver= moves
from ='cvode'= to ='scipy'= to match the ported default backend. Round-trip
(save -> mutate -> restart -> identical =m=/=t=/params), =t0= override,
cross-instance (fresh =Simulation=, same recipe), canonical-name load, and
loud-rejection are all pinned.

Note (Task 16 fix round 1): once spatially varying =alpha= is supported,
=save_restart_data= lumps it (and the already-averaged =Ms=) to a single
representative scalar (=mean(alpha)=/=Ms_av=) rather than erroring the way
legacy would have on =float(varying)= -- graceful, but it silently collapses a
field to one number; both =Ms=/=alpha= are informational metadata only
(=load_restart_data=/=apply_restart_magnetisation= never validate or reapply
them -- only =m= is actually restored on load), which is the accepted
behaviour from the Task 12 review. [Claude Sonnet 5]

*** NDT fidelity (Tablewriter/Tablereader)

=src/finmag/util/fileio.py= is preserved verbatim as the NDT column/format
contract, with only two minimal DOLFINx touchpoints:

1. =from aeon import timer= (profiler, absent in the DOLFINx env) is made
   optional -- a null =timer.method= no-op decorator is used when =aeon= is
   unavailable, so the legacy FEniCS lane still profiles;
2. =np.NAN= -> =np.nan= (NumPy 2 removed the =np.NAN= alias) in the dummy
   integrator-stat get-methods.

The write/read contract is otherwise untouched: comment symbol =# =, header line
of column names, units line, float format =%18.12g =, string format =%18s =. The
Tablereader Python-3 line-parsing already in =fileio.py= reads exactly what
=Tablewriter= writes. This is the same contract the frozen M3 gate
(=test_writing_data.py=, =barmini_test.ndt.ref=) defines; the port's NDT test
asserts the header/units/format directly and round-trips =time=/=m_x,y,z= through
Tablereader against =sim.t=/=sim.m_average=. =Simulation.tablewriter= is created
lazily (default entities: =time=, =m=, dummy =steps=/=last_step_dt=/=dmdt=),
=save_averages= (alias =save_ndt=) appends a row.

*** VTK/XDMF and .npy field snapshots

=sim.save_vtk=/=save_field_to_vtk= route through the write-only DOLFINx
=Field.save_pvd=/=save_xdmf= writers (=.xdmf= dispatched by extension). Read-back
stays explicitly unavailable by name (=Field.save_hdf5= raises the
=dolfinh5tools= error; DOLFINx VTK/XDMF has no legacy read-back either).
=sim.save_field=/=save_m= write =.npy= snapshots via =FieldSaver=, storing the
stable coordinate-ordered value table (a deviation from the legacy raw
=get_local()= dump, for the same coordinate-stability reason as restart);
region-restricted variants defer by name.

*** Scheduler (ported last, thinnest item)

The scheduler core (=finmag.scheduler.scheduler=/=derivedevents=/=timeevent=/
=event=) is backend-free Python and was already import-clean; it is ported
*as-is* -- only its =sim.py= callers were rewired. =run_until= now builds the
integrator, registers a =call_to_end_integration= stop event (=at=t=,
=at_end=True=), and drives =scheduler.run(integrator, callbacks)=, exactly
mirroring the legacy loop; =run_until= with no schedule is behaviourally
identical to the Task 9-11 direct-advance path (explicitly pinned as a
no-regression test). =schedule= supports callables and the shortcut strings
=save_ndt=/=save_averages=/=save_vtk=/=save_field=/=save_restart_data=/=eta=;
unknown shortcut strings raise =KeyError= by name. =unschedule=/=clear_schedule=
and =reset_time= (now also resetting the scheduler clock) are ported.
=sim.schedule("save_ndt", every=2e-12)= over =run_until(1e-11)= writes the
expected 6 rows (=t = 0, 2e-12, ..., 1e-11=).

*** End-to-end witness

=test_end_to_end_all_interactions_with_restart= builds =sim_with(..., A, K1,
H_ext, demag_solver="FK")= (all four ported interactions incl. compiled FK
demag), schedules NDT saves, runs to =6e-12=, saves restart, reloads into a
*fresh* same-recipe =sim_with= simulation, and continues to =1e-11=; continuity
of =t= and =m= across the reload is asserted (=atol 1e-12=), with nodal unit
norm preserved (=atol 1e-4=). The Task 10 core smoke and all prior gates
(import 8p/2s, field 26, energies 26, effectivefield 15, llg 19, scipy 19,
simulation 31, deferred 5, native-bem 8, demag 19, restart-output 20) remain
green; =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= passes end-to-end with a clean tracked tree.

*** Deferred / deliberate deviations

=render_scene=, =plot_dynamics(_3d)=, =plot_mesh=, =snapshot=, region/material
machinery (=mark_regions=/=save_m_in_region=/=get_submesh=), point probing,
=relax=/=hysteresis=, normal modes, and STT still raise =NotImplementedError= by
name. The dolfin-dependent =sim_helpers= helpers (=skyrmion_number=,
=skyrmion_number_density_function=, =get_submesh=, =run_normal_modes_computation=,
=plot_relaxation=), already dropped from the ported =Simulation= in Task 9, were
removed so =sim_helpers= is DOLFINx-import-clean. =pixi.lock= was not touched.

[Claude Opus 4.8]

** Direct DOLFINx DMI Port (Task 13)

Task 13 ports =src/finmag/energies/dmi.py= directly, closing the =DMI= entry in
the Task 10 fail-forward known-gap list (Demag/FK was closed in Task 11b; DMI
is the second of the =requires_legacy_dolfin=True= optional energies to be
ported). It follows the Task 5 =EnergyBase=/=Exchange=/=UniaxialAnisotropy=
pattern exactly: constant-scalar coefficient, ~box-assemble~ only, owned-array
=compute_field()=.

*** Form transcription (legacy line -> ported line)

Every UFL expression is transcribed verbatim from legacy =dmi.py= and
=finmag.util.helpers.times_curl= (never re-derived):

- Bulk/auto dispatch: legacy =self.dmi_factor*self.D.f*times_curl(m.f,
  dmi_dim)= -> =self.dmi_factor * self.D.f * _times_curl(m.f, dmi_dim)=, with
  =dmi_factor = fem.Constant(mesh, 1.0/unit_length)= matching legacy
  =df.Constant(1.0/unit_length)= exactly (not the exchange-style
  =1/unit_length**2=).
- 3D bulk: legacy =df.inner(v, df.curl(v))= -> UFL's native =inner(m,
  curl(m))= (identical operator, just the DOLFINx =ufl= namespace).
- 1D/2D reduced =times_curl=: legacy computes =curlx = dvzdy - dvydz=, =curly
  = dvxdz - dvzdx=, =curlz = dvydx - dvxdy= with =dvxdz=dvydz=dvzdz=0= always
  and =dvxdy=dvydy=dvzdy=0= additionally forced for =dim==1=; the port's
  =_times_curl= keeps exactly this structure (only the always-zero terms are
  folded away algebraically, e.g. =curly = -dmzdx= since =dvxdz= is always
  zero -- the *set* of nonzero derivatives kept per dimension is identical).
- Interfacial: legacy =DMI_interfacial= returns =D*(mx*dmzdx - mz*dmxdx) +
  D*(my*dmzdy - mz*dmydy)= with =dmxdy=dmydy=dmzdy=0= for =dim==1=, else the
  real =y=-gradient components -> ported =_dmi_interfacial= returns the
  identical bracket (the =D= factor is applied by the caller in both cases).
  The legacy public =DMI_interfacial(m, D, dim)= free function is preserved as
  a thin wrapper (=D * _dmi_interfacial(m.f, dim)=) for API compatibility,
  since it is (harmlessly) part of the legacy lazy-export surface and unused
  elsewhere in the codebase.
- Legacy =self.dmi_type is '1d'=-style identity comparisons on string
  literals (a latent legacy bug pattern, only harmless in practice because
  CPython happens to intern these particular literals) are replaced with
  plain equality comparisons in the port; the *dispatch table* (which
  =dmi_type= maps to which dimension/form) is unchanged.
- The undocumented legacy =dmi_type='D2D'= branch (present in the source but
  never mentioned in the class docstring's =Options=) is *not* transcribed
  this slice; it raises =NotImplementedError= naming =D2D= explicitly, rather
  than being silently absorbed into another branch.

*** Scaling-convention evidence

=EnergyBase.setup= multiplies the assembled mesh-coordinate energy by
=unit_length**self.dim= where =self.dim = m.mesh_dim()= (the *actual* mesh
dimension, unaffected by an explicit =dmi_type='1d'=/='2d'=/='3d'= override).
Combined with the =1/unit_length= factor baked into =dmi_factor=, this
reproduces the legacy =unit_length**(dim-1)= DMI convention exactly. Measured
directly against hand-derived affine fields (not just asserted):

- 1D mesh (interval, length 2 mesh units), =m=(0,b,c*x)=: analytic
  =E = D*(-b*c)*L= with *no* =unit_length= dependence at all (=dim-1=0=,
  =unit_length**0=1=). Verified at =unit_length in {1, 2, 5}=: all three give
  =E = -0.0021= exactly (=D=5e-3, b=0.3, c=0.7, L=2=).
- 2D mesh (unit square), =m=(p,q,c*x+d*y)=: analytic =E = D*(p*d-q*c)*Area*
  unit_length= (=dim-1=1=). Verified at =unit_length in {1, 3}=: =E= scales
  exactly linearly (=3.0e-4= then =9.0e-4=, i.e. exactly =3x=).
- 3D bulk affine twist =m=(-y/2, x/2, c)= on the unit cube: =m.curl(m) = c=
  everywhere exactly (transcribed straight from
  =finmag.tests.test_dmi_terms.test_dmi_term3d_matches_analytical_solution=),
  giving =E = D*c*V=; confirmed to =rel=1e-13=.
- 3D interfacial affine ramp =mx=a, my=b= constant, =mz=c*x+e*y=: every
  =mz=-multiplied derivative term vanishes (=mx=/=my= are constant), leaving
  exactly =E = D*(a*c+b*e)=; confirmed to =rel=1e-12=, and flipping the sign
  of =(c,e)= flips the sign of =E= exactly.

*** Chirality/sign pin: an exact FEM-level identity, not an approximation

A genuine helix =m=(0,cos(kx),sin(kx))= cannot be captured exactly by a CG1
interpolant (=cos=/=sin= are not affine), so comparing its assembled energy to
the *continuum* analytic value =E=-D*k*V= would carry mesh-dependent
discretisation error unrelated to the sign convention. Instead: replacing =k=
by =-k= leaves the =y=-component's nodal values *identical* (=cos= is even)
and *exactly negates* the =z=-component's nodal values (=sin= is odd). For a
field depending only on =x= with a zero =x=-component, that nodal transform
is shown algebraically to exactly negate =m.curl(m)= as an identity of the two
FEM functions themselves (not merely their continuum limit) -- so it holds to
machine precision regardless of whatever discretisation error each side
individually carries. Verified numerically on a =create_unit_cube(3,3,3)=
mesh, =k=3=: =E(k) + E(-k) == 0.0= to machine precision (not just =rel=,
literal bitwise cancellation), while =E(k) != 0=. This is a stronger, more
honest pin than an approximate comparison to the continuum helix formula
would have been, and is confirmed against the frozen legacy class itself (not
just self-consistency): running the identical uniform-=m= construction at the
oracle gives energy exactly =0.0= with a *nonzero* field (=max|H|=6.57e7=),
which is not a porting bug but the well-known DMI boundary/edge-canting
contribution (the weak-form derivative =D(v.curl(m) + m.curl(v))= need not
vanish in its second term merely because =curl(m)=0=), identical in both the
legacy and ported math.

*** Fixture provenance

=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/dmi_oracle.json= (schema v1, two cases:
=bulk_3d=/=interfacial=) is generated by
=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/gen_dmi_oracle.py= at the frozen oracle commit
=ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687= (byte-identical =dmi.py= at that
commit, confirmed by direct diff). Mesh:
=BoxMesh(Point(0,0,0),Point(0.7,0.7,0.7),4,4,4)= (matching DOLFINx
=create_box= vertex-for-vertex, the same box-mesh trick used for the FK demag
oracle). Magnetisation =m=(0.6, 0.8*cos(2*pi*x0), 0.8*sin(2*pi*x0))= has exact
unit norm everywhere. The box extent (0.7, not 1.0) is deliberate: an
interfacial density of =D*mx*dmz/dx= integrates =cos(2*pi*x0)= over =x0=,
which is exactly zero over a whole period and would make that case a
degenerate all-zero regression pin (discovered while first generating the
fixture over a =UnitCubeMesh=, where the interfacial case came out as
=-9.2e-37=, i.e. floating-point zero; regenerated over the 0.7-extent box,
where it is a genuine =-1.12e-21 J=). No iterative linear solve is involved
(direct box assembly), so declared tolerances (=atol=1e-9*scale=,
=rtol=1e-9=) reflect floating-point summation-order noise, not solver
residual; measured agreement is far tighter still (energy relative error
=0.0= and =7.1e-15= for the two cases; pointwise field relative error
=~5-8e-16=).

*** TDD evidence

Before this slice, =DMI(1e-3)= raised the raw =ModuleNotFoundError: No module
named 'dolfin'= (the class still imported legacy =dolfin= at module scope);
this was the natural RED state (pinned by
=test_deferred_surfaces_dolfinx.py='s known-gap test). The direct edit to
=src/finmag/energies/dmi.py= removes that import entirely; the same test is
updated in place (renamed
=test_demag_and_dmi_are_ported_and_the_others_remain_a_known_gap=) to assert
=DMI(1e-3).name == "DMI"= and move the still-genuinely-unported
=ModuleNotFoundError= pin onto =CubicAnisotropy= instead (the next class in
the same boundary gap, Task 14).

New gate =dolfinx-src-dmi-pytest= (=test_dmi_dolfinx.py=): 28 passed. All
existing gates remain green with the counts unchanged except as noted: import
9p/2s (one previously-fail-forward =DMI= reference in
=test_import_boundary.py= was retargeted to =CubicAnisotropy=, and the ported-
export test extended to cover =DMI=), field 26, energies 26, effectivefield
15, llg 19, scipy 19, simulation 31 (extended with
=test_sim_with_dmi_builds_ported_interaction=, replacing the old by-name

*** Deferred / deliberate deviations

Spatially varying =D= (=Field=/callable/string) and every non-=box-assemble=

The undocumented legacy =dmi_type='D2D'= variant also raises by name and is

** Direct DOLFINx Cubic Anisotropy Port (Task 14)

Task 14 ports =src/finmag/energies/cubic_anisotropy.py= directly, closing the
=CubicAnisotropy= entry in the Task 10 fail-forward known-gap list (Demag/FK
in Task 11b, DMI in Task 13; only =ThinFilmDemag=/=FixedEnergyDW= remain). It
follows the same =EnergyBase=/=UniaxialAnisotropy=/=DMI= pattern: constant
scalar/axis coefficients, ~box-assemble~ only, owned-array =compute_field()=.

*** Form transcription (legacy line -> ported line)

With =a = u1.m=, =b = u2.m=, =c = u3.m= and =u3 = u1 x u2=, the legacy energy
density

#+begin_example
E_term1 = K1*(u1msq*u2msq + u2msq*u3msq + u3msq*u1msq)   # K1*(a^2 b^2 + b^2 c^2 + c^2 a^2)
E_term2 = K2*(u1msq*u2msq*u3msq)                          # K2*(a^2 b^2 c^2)
E_term3 = K3*(u1msq**2*u2msq**2 + u2msq**2*u3msq**2 + u3msq**2*u1msq**2)  # K3*(a^4 b^4 + b^4 c^4 + c^4 a^4)
#+end_example

is transcribed verbatim into

#+begin_example
a = inner(u1.f, m.f); b = inner(u2.f, m.f); c = inner(u3.f, m.f)
E = K1.f*(a**2*b**2 + b**2*c**2 + c**2*a**2)
E += K2.f*(a**2*b**2*c**2)
E += K3.f*(a**4*b**4 + b**4*c**4 + c**4*a**4)
#+end_example

Legacy's conditional inclusion of the =K2=/=K3= terms only when
=K2_value != 0=/=K3_value != 0= is a pure optimisation (a zero-valued
constant coefficient contributes an algebraically identical zero either way);
the port always includes all three terms unconditionally, which is simpler
and numerically identical. There is no =method= constructor parameter on the
legacy class at all (unlike =DMI=/=UniaxialAnisotropy=): the energy is
*always* box-assembled, both in legacy and here.

*** Axis-handling investigation (the escalation-worthy question, resolved)

The legacy constructor stores =self.u1_value = u1= and =self.u2_value = u2=
completely unchanged -- no =np.asarray=, no normalisation, no orthogonality
check anywhere in the class. =self.u3_value = np.cross(u1, u2)= is the *only*
derived quantity. This is confirmed three ways, not assumed:
1. direct reading of the frozen legacy source (identical at the oracle
   commit =ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687= and at HEAD, verified by
   diff);
2. the legacy module's own =cubic_anisotropy_test.py= uses
   =u1=(0,-0.7071,0.7071)=, =u2=(0,0.7071,0.7071)= -- neither exactly unit
   (=0.7071**2*2 = 0.99980082 != 1=) nor (by construction, since they are not
   unit) exactly orthonormal to full precision -- and the test still passes
   to =rel<1e-10=, i.e. the legacy class runs unmodified with these axes;
3. =dev/dolfinx/prototype.py::cubic_anisotropy_energy=, the M4 probe witness,
   independently documents the same finding ("Unlike
   =uniaxial_anisotropy_energy=, u1/u2 are used as given and not renormalised,
   matching the legacy class, which documents them as should be unit vectors
   but does not enforce it").

The port therefore stores =u1=/=u2= exactly as given (only shape-=(3,)=/
finite validation is added, since legacy's own =np.cross= would otherwise
fail unhelpfully on malformed input) and computes =u3 = np.cross(u1, u2)=
identically. No normalisation, no orthogonality error. This is exercised by
=test_non_orthogonal_non_unit_axes_are_silently_accepted= (deliberately
non-orthogonal, non-unit axes still produce a finite, unmodified energy) and
=test_u3_is_cross_product_of_u1_and_u2=.

*** The =assemble= flag: what it actually gates, and the port's decision

Both legacy =CubicAnisotropy= and =UniaxialAnisotropy= always box-assemble
the *energy* (=E_integrand*dx=) regardless of =self.assemble=; the flag only
selects which algorithm computes the *field*: =assemble=True= uses the same
box-assemble weak-form derivative =EnergyBase= already provides;
=assemble=False= (the =CubicAnisotropy= *legacy default*) uses the native
routine =finmag.native.llg.compute_cubic_field= (=native/src/llg/energy.cc=).

**** Fix round 1 (Claude Opus 4.8): both field paths now ported

The initial Task 14 slice ported only =assemble=True= and raised
=NotImplementedError= on =compute_field()= for =assemble=False= -- but since
=assemble=False= is the *legacy default*, that made a default-constructed
=CubicAnisotropy= unable to participate in dynamics, a functional regression
against the full-parity rule (legacy *capability*, not merely legacy test
usage, is the bar). The Task 14 review flagged this as Important.

Remedy 2 (controller decision): port the native analytic field path for
=assemble=False= as a NumPy transcription
(=CubicAnisotropy._compute_field_analytic=), restoring the legacy default's
capability with the legacy default's *discretisation* (exact nodal analytic
field, not the box-assemble weak-form derivative). =assemble=True= is
unchanged. The analytic field is the closed form

: H = -1/(mu0*Ms) * dE/dm
: dE/da = 2*K1*a*(b^2+c^2) + 2*K2*a*b^2*c^2 + 4*K3*a^3*(b^4+c^4)   (cyclically for b,c)
: dE/dm = (dE/da)*u1 + (dE/db)*u2 + (dE/dc)*u3

hand-derived by the chain rule through =a=u1.m=, =b=u2.m=, =c=u3.m= from the
legacy energy density, and verified three ways: (i) against the native C++
=compute_cubic_field= transcription to 6e-17; (ii) against an independent
finite-difference gradient of =E= to 8e-11; (iii) against per-term legacy
native oracle fixtures below to ~2e-15. Now both flags support
=compute_field()= and dynamics; they differ only in discretisation, and the
box field converges toward the analytic field under refinement (see
=test_box_field_converges_to_analytic_field_under_refinement=: box-vs-analytic
relative L2 gap for =all_nonzero= at n=2,4,8 = 0.616, 0.355, 0.138; for
=k2_only= = 0.747, 0.574, 0.270).

Construction and =compute_energy()= still work identically regardless of
=assemble= (preserving the legacy default call exactly). The now-obsolete
"defers field" tests were rewritten to assert the working analytic field
(=test_default_assemble_false_computes_energy_and_analytic_field=,
=test_cubic_anisotropy_default_assemble_effective_field_works=), and a
default-constructed dynamics test was added
(=test_default_assemble_false_cubic_anisotropy_affects_dynamics=: =run_until=
moves =m= under the analytic effective field, dynamics effect asserted).

**** DELIBERATE DEVIATION -- legacy K2 native-field typo (USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING)

The reviewer flagged an apparent bug in =native/src/llg/energy.cc:116=: the K2
contribution's =hz= line reads

: hz[i] += K2[2]*(t1*u1_z+t2*u2_z+t3*u3_z);   // energy.cc:116

using a *fixed* node index =2= where the =hx=/=hy= lines (and every other
line) use the per-node =K2[i]=. The correct expression is

: hz[i] += K2[i]*(t1*u1_z+t2*u2_z+t3*u3_z);

Following the mandatory K2-typo protocol (derive by hand, do not eyeball; then
confront the native oracle): the typo is a *real* coding bug, but it is
**numerically dormant for the spatially constant K2 this slice supports**.
The native routine receives =K2= as a *nodal array*
(=self.K2 = assemble(K2_field*v*dx)/volumes=); for constant K2 every entry is
equal, so =K2[2] == K2[i]= and the wrong index selects the same value. The
K2-only native oracle (case =k2_only=) therefore matches the *correct*
hand-derived analytic field to 2.7e-15 (measured;
=test_native_oracle_k2_typo_is_dormant_for_constant_k2= pins this, whole field
incl. the =hz= component the typo touches). The port implements the *correct*
per-node field unconditionally; it would diverge from the legacy native output
*only* under spatially varying =K2=, which is deferred by name in this slice.
The independent arbiter (box-assemble field) converges toward the correct
analytic field, not a K2[2] variant (the convergence check above), confirming
the correct field is the continuum limit. Marked USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING:
the legacy K2 native-field bug is not reproduced (it cannot manifest under the
supported constant-K2 case, and the port is correct where legacy is buggy).

*** Cubic-symmetry easy/hard-axis ordering (verified, not assumed)

For the standard orthonormal axes =u1=(1,0,0)=, =u2=(0,1,0)= (so
=u3=(0,0,1)=) and =K1>0=, with =a=m.u1=, =b=m.u2=, =c=m.u3=:
- =m=[100]=: =(a,b,c)=(1,0,0)= so =a^2 b^2 = b^2 c^2 = c^2 a^2 = 0= -> =E=0=.
- =m=[110]=: =a=b=1/sqrt(2)=, =c=0= -> =a^2 b^2 = 1/4=, others =0= ->
  =E = K1/4 * V=.
- =m=[111]=: =a=b=c=1/sqrt(3)= -> each of the three products is =1/9=,
  summing to =1/3= -> =E = K1/3 * V=.
Measured directly (=K1=1e4=, unit cube, =assemble=True=):
=E([100])=0.0=, =E([110])-E([100])=2500.0000000000145= (expected =2500.0=),
=E([111])-E([100])=3333.33333333332= (expected =3333.33...=) -- confirming
the brief's algebra rather than trusting it, and pinning =<100>= easy,
=<111>= hard for =K1>0=, exactly as cubic anisotropy theory predicts.

*** Fixture provenance

=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/cubic_anisotropy_oracle.json= (schema v1, one case
=cubic_3d=) is generated by
=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/gen_cubic_anisotropy_oracle.py= at the frozen
oracle commit =ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687= (byte-identical
=cubic_anisotropy.py= at that commit, confirmed by direct diff). Constants
and axes are the exact =K1=-8608726=, =K2=-13744132=, =K3=1100269=,
=u1=(0,-0.7071,0.7071)=, =u2=(0,0.7071,0.7071)= used by
=cubic_anisotropy_test.py=; mesh =BoxMesh(Point(0,0,0),Point(0.7,0.7,0.7),
4,4,4)= and magnetisation =m=(0.6, 0.8*cos(2*pi*x0), 0.8*sin(2*pi*x0))=
(exact unit norm) match the Task 13 DMI oracle for consistency. The legacy
class is constructed with =assemble=True= so the recorded =H_vertex= is the
box-assemble field the DOLFINx port actually reproduces (the energy is
identical to the legacy default regardless of =assemble=). A quartic
(=a^4 b^4=) term over a non-polynomial (=cos=/=sin=) magnetisation expression
made FFC's automatic quadrature-degree estimate large enough to print a
"number of integration points" warning directly to the OS-level stdout file
descriptor (bypassing Python's logging and =sys.stdout=, which would have
corrupted the JSON emitted on stdout); the generator redirects file
descriptor 1 to =/dev/null= for the assembly calls only, leaving the physics
untouched. Measured agreement between the ported DOLFINx computation and this
oracle: energy relative error =8.3e-14=, field relative error =1.4e-15= --
both far tighter than the declared fixture tolerances (=rtol=1e-9=), so
observed disagreement is floating-point summation-order noise, not a solver
residual or a transcription error.

Fix round 1 (Claude Opus 4.8) adds a companion native-field fixture
=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/cubic_anisotropy_native_oracle.json= (schema v1,
generator =gen_cubic_anisotropy_native_oracle.py=), same oracle commit and
same mesh/=m=/axes/=Ms= family, but the legacy class is constructed with the
*default* =assemble=False= so the recorded =H_vertex= is the *native*
=compute_cubic_field= analytic field. Four sub-cases isolate each term
(=k1_only=, =k2_only=, =k3_only=) plus =all_nonzero=, so the pure
transcription of each of K1/K2/K3 can be checked independently; the =k2_only=
case is the executable evidence for the =energy.cc:116= K2[2] typo being
dormant for constant K2. Ms is placed on a CG1 (nodal) space in this
generator because the native routine expects a per-vertex Ms array, not the
per-cell DG0 one (Ms is constant, so the value and the box-assembled energy
are unchanged). Measured agreement, ported analytic field vs native oracle:
=k1_only= 2.1e-15, =k2_only= 2.7e-15, =k3_only= 2.2e-15, =all_nonzero= 2.0e-15
-- machine precision, confirming pure transcription.

*** Deferred / deliberate deviations

Spatially varying =K1=/=K2=/=K3= (=Field=/callable/string) and spatially

=NotImplementedError= by name (Task 16). As of fix round 1 (Claude Opus 4.8)
*both* field paths are ported: =assemble=True= (box-assemble weak-form
derivative) and the legacy-default =assemble=False= (native analytic field,
=_compute_field_analytic=); neither raises. Legacy's own =sim_with= never had
cubic-anisotropy parameters, so there is no =sim_with= wiring gap to close;
=Simulation.add(CubicAnisotropy(...))= is the full integration surface,
exercised with genuine dynamics effects for *both* the =assemble=True=
(=test_cubic_anisotropy_affects_dynamics=) and legacy-default
=assemble=False= (=test_default_assemble_false_cubic_anisotropy_affects_dynamics=)
constructors -- a low-symmetry initial =m= precesses/relaxes under the
