** Direct DOLFINx Time-Zeeman and Hysteresis Port (Task 15)

Task 15 ports the entire legacy time-Zeeman family directly on top of the
Task 5 static =Zeeman= -- =TimeZeeman=, =DiscreteTimeZeeman=,
=TimeZeemanPython=, =OscillatingZeeman= and =DipolarField= -- replacing the
=_DeferredZeeman= curated stubs, and ports =sim.relax=/=hysteresis=/
=hysteresis_loop= (=src/finmag/sim/sim_relax.py=/=hysteresis.py=, which are
the *untouched* legacy modules -- verified byte-for-byte identical to the
frozen oracle checkout before editing).

*** Input-contract decision: =field_expression= as a callable of time

Legacy's =TimeZeeman(field_expression, t_off=None)= accepted a =dolfin=
=Expression= carrying a mutable =.t= attribute, mutated by =update(t)=
before re-interpolation, or (as a safety-checked special case) a constant
3-array (only meaningful together with =t_off=). DOLFINx has no Expression
equivalent. The ported contract (=_as_time_field_function= in
=finmag/energies/zeeman.py=) is a plain Python callable
=field_function(t) -> value=, where =value= is anything
=finmag.field.Field.set= already accepts -- a constant vector/array, a
per-point/vectorized callable =g(x)=, a =dolfinx.fem.Function=, or another
=Field=. This preserves the *full* legacy capability (spatially *and*
temporally varying fields, via the nested =lambda t: (lambda x: ...)=
form) without the Expression string mechanism, and composes directly with
the existing =Field.set=/=Zeeman.set_value= machinery -- no new
interpolation path was needed. A bare constant 3-array/tuple/list is still
accepted directly (wrapped into a callable returning that same constant),
matching legacy's "constant array requires =t_off=" safety check verbatim
(same =ValueError= condition and message, ported test:
=test_time_zeeman_init_constant_array_requires_t_off=).

=TimeZeemanPython(H0_value, time_fun, t_off=None)= keeps legacy's
performance rationale exactly: =H0_value= (any =Field.set=-acceptable
value) is interpolated *once* in =setup()= into a cached NumPy array
(=self._H_init=); every =update(t)= only rescales that cached array by
=float(time_fun(t))= and writes it back via =Field.from_array= -- no
re-interpolation per step. =compute_field=/=compute_energy=/
=average_field=/=energy_density=/=energy_density_function= are all
*inherited unchanged* from =Zeeman= (the class only overrides =setup=,
=update= and =switch_off=), since after =setup= builds =self.H=/=self.E=/
=self.S1=/=self.m=/=self.Ms= the exact same way =Zeeman.setup= does, every
read-only accessor is already correct. The legacy scalar-spatial-envelope-
with-vector-=time_fun= branch (one shared scalar envelope multiplied
independently per vector component, e.g. for a rotating field) is *not*
exercised by any legacy test (=zeeman_test.py= only uses the vector-H0/
scalar-time_fun path, the same one =OscillatingZeeman= uses) and is out of
scope for this slice -- a documented, narrow reduction of one class's
capability, not a stub of the whole class.

=OscillatingZeeman(H0, freq, phase=0, t_off=None)= is a =TimeZeemanPython=
subclass built from =amplitude(t) = cos(2*pi*freq*t + phase)=, transcribed
verbatim from the legacy formula (ordinary, not angular, frequency; cosine
not sine; phase inside the argument so =H(0) = H0*cos(phase)=), pinned
analytically at 59 sample points against the exact closed form
(=test_oscillating_zeeman_waveform=, no fixture needed -- the formula is
transcribed and tested directly).

=DipolarField(pos, m, magnitude=None)= is ported directly as a =Zeeman=
subclass using the closed-form point-dipole field as a plain Python
callable -- legacy's own =zeeman.py= already defined this exact closed form
as =H_fun= but never used it (its constructor called
=super().__init__(H_expr, ...)= with the =Expression= string version
instead; the =H_fun= closure is commented out immediately below). DOLFINx
has no Expression equivalent, so this port uses precisely the closed form
the legacy author had already written, vectorised over
=Field='s callable-interpolation contract (handling both the vectorized
=(gdim, npoints)= and single-point =(gdim,)= calling conventions explicitly,
rather than relying on the generic vectorized/pointwise exception-driven
fallback in =Field._interpolation_callable=). Verified against the closed
form directly (=test_dipolar_field_matches_closed_form=, points near the
dipole singularity excluded).

*** Two legacy quirks discovered and preserved verbatim

1. *=t_off=0.0= is falsy-disabled.* Legacy's switch-off test is
   =if self.t_off and t >= self.t_off= (truthiness, not =is not None=), so
   =t_off=0.0= is silently *never* triggered. Transcribed verbatim (pinned
   by =test_time_zeeman_t_off_zero_is_falsy_disabled_quirk=); a small
   positive =t_off= should be used instead if switching off at (near) t=0
   is the actual intent.

2. *=DiscreteTimeZeeman='s stale energy after an interval update.*
   =TimeZeeman.update()= refreshes the field via =self.H.set(value)=,
   *mutating* the existing =H= =Function= in place, so the cached energy
   form =self.E= (built once in =setup()=, referencing that same Function
   object) stays valid. =DiscreteTimeZeeman.update()= instead assigns a
   *brand-new* =Field= object directly to =self.H= (bypassing
   =set_value()=, which is what rebuilds =self.E=). Consequently
   =self.E='s UFL form keeps referencing the *original* (setup-time) =H=
   Function forever, so =compute_energy()= silently returns the
   setup-time energy value on every call after the first interval update,
   even though =compute_field()=/=energy_density()= (which read
   =self.H='s current array directly, not through =self.E=) correctly
   reflect the updated field. This is a genuine, previously-undocumented
   legacy defect, discovered while building the oracle fixture (see
   =timezeeman_oracle.json='s =discrete_time_zeeman= case: =energy= stays
   exactly the setup-time value across every snapshot even as =H_vertex=
   visibly changes) and independently reproduced against the frozen oracle
   itself. It is transcribed verbatim (DELIBERATE PRESERVATION, USER
   ACCEPTANCE PENDING), not silently fixed -- see
   =test_discrete_time_zeeman_energy_staleness_quirk=. The interval-timing
   quirk (=t_last_update= never advances, so once =t= first reaches
   =dt_update= every later =update(t)= call refreshes the field again) was
   already visible in the legacy source and is likewise preserved (see
   =test_discrete_time_zeeman_continuous_after_first_crossing_quirk= and
   the =discrete_time_zeeman= oracle case, which deliberately samples =t=
   values that cross =dt_update= more than once to pin it).

*** Important finding: =hysteresis()=/=hysteresis_loop()= do not actually
    re-relax after the first stage

=src/finmag/sim/sim_relax.py= and =src/finmag/sim/hysteresis.py= are
untouched legacy modules (diffed byte-for-byte against the frozen oracle
checkout before any edit); the only change made anywhere in this slice is
replacing their =from finmag.util.helpers import ...= (legacy
=dolfin=-backed) import with a local, dolfin-free reimplementation
(=norm=/=compute_dmdt= added to =finmag.sim.sim_helpers=, matching the
=clean_filename= precedent from Task 12). =Simulation.relax=/=hysteresis=/
=hysteresis_loop= are bound exactly the way legacy did
(=relax = sim_relax.relax=, etc).

While building the tiny-loop oracle fixture, repeated =relax()= calls
within one =hysteresis()= loop (uniaxial anisotropy, a field reversal well
past the Stoner-Wohlfarth coercive field) were found to *not* actually
achieve an independent re-relaxation after the very first stage: the
magnetisation stays essentially frozen near its stage-1 value for every
subsequent stage, regardless of how strongly the field reverses. Root
cause: each =relax()= call's scheduler trigger is registered with
=after=sim.t= once =sim.t > 1e-14=, so the *first* scheduled tick of stage 2
onward coincides exactly with the integrator's already-advanced clock
(=t == integrator.cur_t=); =Scheduler.run()= explicitly skips integrating
in that case (see the comment in that method and in =Simulation.run_until=)
to avoid confusing the integrator, so the very first (and, empirically, the
decisive) trigger evaluation of every stage after the first sees virtually
no time evolution. This was independently *confirmed against the frozen
legacy oracle itself*, using the native Sundials backend (the true legacy
default, not the DOLFINx port's temporary SciPy default) -- the oracle
reproduces the identical symptom (=gen_hysteresis_oracle.py='s module
docstring has the full trace). The legacy test suite even documents this
exact symptom with a wink: =src/finmag/sim/hysteresis_test.py::
test_hysteresis_loop_and_plotting= asserts
=np.allclose(m_vals, [1.0]*2N, atol=1e-4)= for a whole field-reversing loop
with the comment "Check that the magnetisation values are as trivial as we
expect them to be ;-)". This DOLFINx port reproduces the same (surprising,
but genuinely legacy, oracle-confirmed) behavior verbatim -- see
=test_oracle_hysteresis_loop_matches_legacy= (pinning the tiny K1 loop
against the oracle to ~2e-3 relative, with the stage *times* matching the
oracle bit-for-bit since the schedule is pure-Python and backend-
independent) and =test_hysteresis_loop_legacy_trivial_invariant= (porting
the legacy test's own "trivial" assertion directly). DELIBERATE
PRESERVATION, USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING: this is a real, if narrow,
capability gap in the convenience =hysteresis()=/=hysteresis_loop()=
wrappers as legacy actually implemented them, not a DOLFINx-port
regression, and not something this slice silently "fixes".

The underlying Stoner-Wohlfarth-like switching *capability* -- the reason
=relax()=/=hysteresis()= exist -- works correctly and is witnessed
separately in =test_stoner_wohlfarth_like_loop_witness=, which builds a
proper hysteresis loop out of *fresh* =Simulation=/=relax()= calls per
field step (field applied at a slight angle to the easy axis, avoiding both
the above limitation and the unrelated, much slower, exactly-on-axis
critical-slowing-down approach to the bifurcation): the macrospin flips
sign between the two coercive-field steps in each direction and the loop
closes (first and last point, both at +H_max, agree to within 0.05).

=sim.relax= needed no porting decision beyond the import fix above: it is
a thin wrapper over the already-ported =scheduler=/=integrator=/
=EffectiveField= stack (its stopping criterion --
=dmdt <= stopping_dmdt*ONE_DEGREE_PER_NS=, with the adaptive
=dt=/divergence-counter logic -- is transcribed verbatim, unedited). One
more preserved legacy quirk, noted directly in =sim_relax.py=: the module
imports =ONE_DEGREE_PER_NS= from =finmag.util.consts= (=17453292.52=) and
then immediately *shadows* it with a local literal (=17453292.5=, missing
the trailing =2=) -- a pre-existing legacy inconsistency, not introduced or
fixed by this port.

- =DiscreteTimeZeeman='s cached energy form goes stale after the first
  interval update (preserved legacy quirk, discovered this slice, USER
  ACCEPTANCE PENDING).
- =hysteresis()=/=hysteresis_loop()= do not achieve an independent
  re-relaxation after their first stage (preserved legacy quirk, discovered
  this slice via the oracle, USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING) -- the underlying
  =relax()= capability itself is unaffected and independently witnessed.
- Spatially varying field-valued parameters and regions remain out of scope
  (Task 16), as before.

[Claude Sonnet 5]; fix round 1 [Claude Opus 4.8]

** Field-Valued Parameters and Regions (Task 16)

Extends every ported box-energy, the LLG damping, and the Simulation region
machinery to accept spatially varying material parameters, with the DG0-vs-CG1
placement of each coefficient established from the frozen oracle (not assumed)
and pinned node-for-node on 1D interval meshes (identical connectivity in
dolfin and dolfinx).

*** Per-parameter placement (legacy evidence, oracle commit ba92809)

Placement is exactly the function space each legacy energy class built its
coefficient =Field= in (legacy interpolates -- never Galerkin-projects --
callables/expressions into that space, so DG0 samples cell midpoints and CG1
samples vertices):

- =A= (exchange): *DG0* (=FunctionSpace(mesh,'DG',0)=).
- =D= (DMI): *DG0*.
- =K1=/=K2=/axis (uniaxial): *CG1* scalar / *CG1* vector.
- cubic =K1=/=K2=/=K3=: *CG1* scalar; =u1=/=u2=/=u3=: *CG1* vector.
- =Ms=: whatever space the caller built it in -- =EnergyBase= uses =Ms.f=
  verbatim in the =E_integrand/Ms.f= derivative (Simulation supplies DG0).
- =alpha= (LLG): *CG1* scalar =Function= (legacy
  =helpers.scalar_valued_function(value, S1)=).

The DOLFINx port's coefficient spaces already matched legacy exactly, so this
slice only relaxes the per-class by-name gates to route spatially varying
values (callable / =Field= / =dolfinx.fem.Function=) through =Field.set= into
those same spaces. Legacy *string* =Expression= coefficients raise
=NotImplementedError= by name (DOLFINx has no =Expression=; pass a callable) --
a documented deviation shared with the rest of the port. (=Field.from_function=
now interpolates on a space mismatch instead of erroring -- a tested superset
of the legacy contract; see =dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md='s Field entry.)

*** Axis contract: constant-axis-normalised vs varying-axis-as-given (register clarification)

=axis_coefficient= (=finmag/energies/energy_base.py=) treats the uniaxial
anisotropy easy axis differently depending on whether it is constant or
spatially varying, and both halves are deliberate, evidence-based decisions,
not an oversight:

- a *constant* axis (3-tuple/list/array) is validated finite and then
  normalised to a unit vector, restoring the *intended* legacy contract that
  the dot product =axis.m= represents an angle cosine (=E = K1*(1 -
  (axis.m)^2)= only means what it says for a unit =axis=) -- a pre-Tier-1
  (Task 5) =UniaxialAnisotropy= deviation from the letter of the legacy
  class, documented in =finmag/energies/anisotropy.py='s own docstring.
- a *spatially varying* axis (callable / =Field= / =dolfinx.fem.Function=,
  Task 16) is used exactly as given, with no normalisation applied per node
  -- this is bug-compatible with legacy, which interpolates a varying axis
  =Expression=/callable directly into its coefficient space and never
  renormalises the result either.

Cubic anisotropy's =u1=/=u2= are a different, third case: they are *never*
normalised, constant or varying (see "Axis-handling investigation" above) --
the legacy =CubicAnisotropy= class does not normalise its axes at all, for
either input form.

*** Spatially varying alpha (per-node in gamma_LL AND damping)

Evidence: native =calc_llg_dmdt= (=native/src/llg/llg.cc=) loops over nodes
passing =*alpha[i]= into both =damping_i= (=gamma_LL = gamma/(1+alpha*alpha)=,
=damping_coeff = -alpha*gamma_LL=) and =precession_i= (same per-node
=gamma_LL=). The port's =LLG._dmdt_numpy= now uses a per-node alpha array
(=self._alpha_node=, coordinate-ordered to align with the =(3,N)= m/H columns)
in exactly those two places. =LLG.alpha= returns a =float= when uniform
(preserving the scalar contract and =float(sim.alpha)= restart metadata) and
the per-node array otherwise. The =test_spatially_varying_alpha= invariant
(scalar =1= -> uniform nodal vector of ones) is ported; the varying-alpha RHS
is pinned against =spatially_varying_alpha_rhs.json=.

*** Regions

Legacy exposed NO region-scoped =set_alpha= / per-region material-parameter
setter (the task premise was wrong on this point). Region support is:
=mark_regions(fun)= (a =fun(pt)->id= partition, built into contiguous
=region_ids=, per-cell =MeshTags= and a subdomain-restricted measure) plus
per-region energy via =compute_energy(dx=measure)= (legacy =Zeeman= already had
this; extended consistently to =EnergyBase=) and region =m_average=. The
=test_energies_in_regions= additivity invariant (region energies sum to the
whole-mesh energy, rtol=1e-12/atol=1e-18) is ported and oracle-pinned
(=two_region_zeeman=). Region-restricted submesh *field output*
(=save_m_in_region=/=get_submesh=/=get_field_as_dolfin_function(region=...)=)
stays deferred by name. Serial only.

*** DELIBERATE DEVIATION now LIVE (USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING): K2 native typo

The Task 14 K2 native-typo deviation (=native/src/llg/energy.cc:116=
=hz[i] += K2[2]*(...)= -- a fixed node index =2= where every other line uses
per-node =K2[i]=) is now LIVE: this slice supports spatially varying =K2=, for
which the nodal =K2= array is non-uniform. The port implements the *correct*
per-node =hz=; the legacy native =hz= uses =K2= at the single fixed index =2=
(=v*=). The =k2_only= divergence is pinned executably
(=test_k2_varying_diverges_from_legacy_native_in_hz_only=) against
=cubic_k2_varying_oracle.json=:

- measured =hx=/=hy=: port == legacy native to ~1e-9 (max ~1.2e-9 A/m);
- measured =hz=: diverges by up to ~2.59e5 A/m (predicted
  =-2/(mu0 Ms)(K2[v*]-K2[i]) termz[i]=);
- the implied per-node K2 reproducing the legacy =hz= is a single constant
  =K2[v*] = -1.5925e7= (std ~4e-9), confirming the fixed-index typo signature.

The box-assembled *energy* never uses the native field path (verified: it
assembles =E_integrand= with the CG1 K2 field) and matches legacy. The port
ships the corrected =hz=; USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING.

*** Fix round 1 (Finding 1): per-node Ms in the native analytic field

The =assemble=False= (legacy-default) cubic-anisotropy native field originally
used =self.Ms.as_constant()=, silently dropping legacy's support for a
spatially varying =Ms= there and raising a generic =RuntimeError= instead of a
documented one. Legacy evidence
(=dev/bin/run-legacy-oracle -- cat src/finmag/energies/cubic_anisotropy.py=):
=__compute_field_directly= sets =self.Ms = self.Ms.get_numpy_array_debug()= --
the *raw* (not mass-lumped) nodal array of whatever =Ms= =Field= the caller
passed to =setup= -- and feeds it straight to the compiled
=native_llg.compute_cubic_field=, whose C++ implementation
(=native/src/llg/energy.cc=) hard-requires
=Ms_arr.check_shape(nodes, ...)=: exactly as many entries as =m= has nodes.
Legacy's own working usage (=cubic_anisotropy_test.py=, and every
=gen_cubic_*_native_oracle.py= fixture generator) always placed =Ms= on the
CG1 scalar space matching =m= for this reason.

This matters because =finmag.sim.sim.Simulation=/=finmag.physics.llg.LLG=
*always* place =Ms= in DG0 (`self._Ms_dg = Field(self.DG, ...)` in both the
port and the legacy =sim.py=), which has a different dof count than CG1
nodes for any non-degenerate mesh. Verified empirically against the oracle:
constructing a legacy =Simulation= with =CubicAnisotropy(assemble=False)= and
calling =effective_field()= raises
=ValueError: compute_cubic_field: Ms: Expected array of shape (42), got (40)=
on a 20x1 =RectangleMesh= (42 CG1 vertices, 40 cells) -- i.e. legacy itself
does not support the DG0 =Ms= Simulation always builds through this native
path, constant or varying; it only ever worked because the constant-=Ms=
oracle/unit tests happened to pass =Ms= on the CG1 space directly (never
through =Simulation=, and never actually invoking =compute_field()= when a
DG0 =Ms= was used).

The port (=CubicAnisotropy._ms_per_node=) now: (1) keeps the cheap
global-value fast path for spatially uniform =Ms= in *any* space (preserving
every existing =assemble=False= test, including through =Simulation=, whose
constant DG0 =Ms= never hit the size mismatch because =as_constant()= only
checks value uniformity); (2) for a genuinely varying =Ms=, uses
=Ms.as_array()= node-for-node, raising a clear documented =ValueError=
(mentioning the CG1-vs-DG0 requirement and =assemble=True= as an alternative)
if the array length does not match the number of =m= nodes, reproducing --
not silently dropping -- legacy's own DG0-vs-CG1 limitation.

New oracle fixture =cubic_varying_ms_oracle.json=
(=gen_cubic_varying_ms_oracle.py=, generated at the frozen oracle commit):
=K1=-8608726=, =K3=1100269=, =K2=0= (deliberately, to sidestep the K2[2]
typo per the Task 16 review note), =Ms=876626*(1+0.35 x0/0.7)= (spatially
varying), same mesh/axes/=m= as the constant-Ms native oracle. The port's
field matches the legacy native field at the fixture's declared tolerance
(=rtol=1e-9=, per =cubic_varying_ms_oracle.json='s =H_vertex= tolerances --
looser than the constant-K native-oracle cases' =rtol=1e-11= because this
fixture also exercises a non-uniform per-node =Ms= divisor); the generator
additionally verifies its own =H_vertex= against an independent NumPy
analytic derivation with the same per-node =Ms= before serialising
(=rtol=1e-9=), as a self-consistency check. New tests in
=test_variable_params_dolfinx.py=:
the oracle pin, a finite-and-nonzero direct check, an =EffectiveField=-driven
dynamics check (per-node =Ms= actually enters =LLG.solve='s right-hand side,
built directly against =LLG=/=EffectiveField= since =Simulation=/=LLG= force
DG0 =Ms=), and the documented-error check for a varying DG0 =Ms= through
=Simulation=. [Claude Sonnet 5]

*** Verification

New gate =dolfinx-src-varparams-pytest=: 28 tests at the initial Task 16
slice, now 32 (fix round 1 above adds 4). All prior gates unchanged and green
(import 9p/2s, field 26, energies 26 -- the
=test_invalid_or_deferred_material_inputs= case now pins the string-Expression
deferral instead of spatially-varying deferrals --, effectivefield 15, llg 19,
scipy 19, simulation 32, deferred 5, native-bem 8, demag 19, restart-output 20,
dmi 28, cubicanis 25, timezeeman 27). =verify-dolfinx-m5= passes end-to-end
with a clean tracked tree; =pixi.lock= untouched.

*** Deferred / deliberate deviations

- Legacy string =Expression= coefficients (pass a callable).
- Spatially varying cubic =u1=/=u2= axes (no oracle; =u3=u1 x u2= would need
  per-node evaluation) -- spatially varying cubic =K='s are supported.
- Region-restricted submesh field output (=save_m_in_region=/=get_submesh=/
  =get_field_as_dolfin_function(region=...)=).
- K2 native-typo hz divergence for varying K2 (LIVE, USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING).
- A varying =Ms= for =assemble=False= cubic anisotropy must be on a scalar
  space with the same per-node (CG1) layout as =m=; a mismatched space (e.g.
  the DG0 space =Simulation=/=LLG= always use) raises a documented
  =ValueError= rather than being supported, reproducing legacy's own
  limitation there (fix round 1, Finding 1, above).

[Claude Opus 4.8]; fix round 1 (per-node =Ms=, doc updates) [Claude Sonnet 5]

** Mesh Tooling Bridge (Netgen/Gmsh -> DOLFINx) (Task 18)

=src/finmag/util/meshes.py= and =src/finmag/util/mesh_templates.py= are now the
direct DOLFINx port of the mesh-generation surface. Gate:
=dolfinx-src-meshes-pytest= (=src/finmag/tests/test_meshes_dolfinx.py=, 27
tests), folded into =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=.

*** Gmsh Python-API bridge (no dolfin-XML)

The legacy path built Netgen =algebraic3d= CSG text, shelled out to
=netgen=/=gmsh= CLIs, went through DIFFPACK/=.msh= -> dolfin-XML ->
=df.Mesh(...xml.gz)=. The port instead drives Gmsh through its Python API
(OpenCASCADE kernel) and converts the model to a =dolfinx.mesh= in-memory via
=dolfinx.io.gmsh.model_to_mesh= (the module is =gmsh= in DOLFINx 0.10, not the
older =gmshio= name; =model_to_mesh(model, comm, rank, gdim)= returns a
=MeshData= whose =.mesh= is the dolfinx mesh). No dolfin-XML intermediates, no
CLI shelling. =gmsh= and the dolfinx runtime are imported *lazily* inside the
generators, so =import finmag= never pulls in gmsh (plain-import boundary; the
import-boundary gate stays green, and an explicit assertion confirms =gmsh not
in sys.modules= after importing =finmag.util.meshes=).

OCC primitives used: =addBox=, =addSphere=, =addCylinder=, =addCone=; elliptic
cylinders/ellipsoids are a unit primitive followed by =occ.dilate=; =ring= and
=MeshDifference= use =occ.cut=; =MeshSum= uses =occ.fuse=; =pair_of_disks= puts
two disjoint cylinders in one physical group. A physical group of the
top-dimensional volumes is required by =model_to_mesh= (it raises otherwise).

*** Preserved hashing/naming contract; internal geometry change

The template classes (=MeshTemplate=, =MeshPrimitive=, =Sphere=, =Box=,
=EllipticalNanodisk=, =Nanodisk=, =MeshSum=, =MeshDifference=) keep their
public names, parameters, and =csg_string()=/=csg_stub()=/=hash()=/
=generic_filename()= semantics *unchanged*. The Netgen-CSG text is retained
byte-for-byte and used purely as the md5 cache key, so the frozen digests in
the legacy =mesh_templates_test.py::test_hash=
(=50f3b55770e40ba7a5f8e62d7ff7d327=, =1ee55186811cfc21f22e17fbad35bfed=) still
hold. The *internal* change is that geometry is built through the Gmsh OCC
kernel from the stored numeric parameters rather than by parsing the CSG text.
=vec2str= was inlined into =mesh_templates.py= (it previously came from
=finmag.util.helpers=, which imports =dolfin= at module scope and so is not
import-safe in the dolfinx env).

Per-primitive =maxh_NAME= discretisation for combined meshes is reproduced with
a spatially varying Gmsh mesh-size callback: each leaf primitive contributes a
=(contains_fn, size)= region and the callback returns the min matching size
(with =MeshSizeFromPoints/FromCurvature/ExtendFromBoundary= disabled so the
callback dominates). This keeps the legacy invariant that a finer
=maxh_sphere2= yields more vertices than a coarser one.

*** Caching contract on an XDMF store (first internal XDMF read)

The legacy contract (same inputs -> cache hit, distinct inputs -> distinct
files, =directory= override honoured, md5-of-CSG key when no filename given) is
preserved with a DOLFINx-native store: meshes are cached as XDMF (=.xdmf= +
=.h5=). A cache hit reads the mesh back via =XDMFFile.read_mesh=. This is the
repo's first XDMF *read* usage, but it is kept strictly internal to the cache;
it is NOT general XDMF read-back support (that remains Task 26). A legacy
=.xml.gz= (or =.xdmf=) filename argument is honoured by stripping the suffix.
Cache files are only ever written under caller-chosen directories (tests chdir
into =tmp_path=), so the tracked-tree cleanliness guard stays satisfied.

*** Vertex/cell counts not pinned

Legacy Netgen produced different tetrahedralisations than Gmsh OCC, so the
frozen pixi-tip vertex/cell counts are not meaningful to compare. Validation is
by analytic volume (planar boxes exact to =TOL3=1e-14=; curved meshes within
=TOL1=1e-2=, matching the legacy tolerances) plus the caching-determinism and
FK-demag smokes. The box-difference volume is exact (29000.0) because OCC boxes
have planar faces.

*** Physics smoke

=test_fk_demag_on_generated_sphere_smoke= generates a sphere through the bridge
(=sphere(r=20, maxh=3)=), runs the ported =FKDemag= on it with uniform m along
z, and asserts the average demag field is -Ms/3 (sphere demag factor 1/3)
within 5%, with small transverse components -- proving the bridge feeds real
physics end-to-end.

*** Deferrals (documented, Task 29 review items)

- *Netgen backend*: conda-forge =netgen= is not in the dolfinx env and the
  =.geo= -> netgen -> DIFFPACK -> dolfin-XML path does not port cleanly.
  =netgen_is_usable()= returns =False=; the Gmsh path covers the geometry set
  the legacy tests exercise.
- =nmesh_to_dolfin.py=: unchanged and unported -- it emits legacy dolfin-XML
  (unreadable by DOLFINx) and is consumed only by the unported Nmag
  reference-generation scripts (=tests/**/run_nmag*.py=, Task 27 scope). Not
  ported speculatively.
- Textual entry points =from_geofile= / =from_csg= (Netgen-CSG/dolfin-XML) are
  by-name =NotImplementedError= stubs -- the Gmsh-API generators replace them.
- Multi-region "airbox" generators =sphere_inside_box= /
  =elliptical_nanodisk_with_cuboid_shell= (subdomain-marked meshes), the 2D
  gmsh-script helpers =regular_polygon= / =regular_polygon_extruded= /
  =disk_with_internal_layers=, and the dolfin-based analysis/plotting utilities
  (=mesh_info=, =mesh_quality=, =nodal_volume=, =longest_edges=, =mesh_size=,
  =mesh_size_plausible=, =describe_mesh_size=, =print_mesh_info=,
  =mesh_is_periodic=, =build_mesh=, =embed3d=, =line_mesh=, =plot_mesh=,
  =plot_mesh_with_paraview=, =plot_mesh_regions=) are by-name stubs.
  =mesh_volume=/=num_vertices=/=order_of_magnitude= ARE ported (dolfinx-native).

*** Env dependency change

=gmsh= and =python-gmsh= were added to the =feature.dolfinx.dependencies= in
=pixi.toml= (conda-forge). =pixi.lock= changed accordingly. They are only ever
imported lazily inside the generators.

[Claude Opus 4.8]

*** Fix round 1 (review findings on commit 867c9ca1)

Four review findings against this slice were fixed (gate stays
=dolfinx-src-meshes-pytest=, now 34 tests):

1. *CSG<->OCC cache-drift guard*: the md5-of-CSG cache key (=hash()=/
   =generic_filename()=) and the OCC geometry (=_occ_solids=) are parallel
   encodings both derived from the same constructor parameters, with nothing
   enforcing that a parameter can't move one without the other. A new
   =test_csg_occ_cache_drift_guard= (in =test_meshes_dolfinx.py=) perturbs
   every geometry-affecting constructor parameter of a representative sample
   (=Sphere=, =Box=, =EllipticalNanodisk=, and one =MeshSum=/=MeshDifference=
   pair -- the sampling rationale is documented in the test docstring) and
   asserts both the hash changes and a (volume, centroid) geometry fingerprint
   changes, at coarse =maxh= to keep it fast.
2. *=ring(with_middle_plane=True)= by-name*: this legacy kwarg was accepted but
   silently ignored by the Gmsh port -- a silent narrowing of the by-name-
   deferral rule. It now raises =NotImplementedError= (the default
   =with_middle_plane=False= path is unaffected), tested by
   =test_ring_with_middle_plane_deferred_by_name=.
3. *Five deleted sibling helpers*: =mesh_size_plausible=, =describe_mesh_size=,
   =print_mesh_info=, =plot_mesh_with_paraview=, =plot_mesh_regions= were
   dropped outright in the initial port (bare =AttributeError= on lookup)
   instead of getting by-name stubs like their siblings (=mesh_size=,
   =mesh_info=, =plot_mesh=, ...). They now have explicit =_deferred=-style
   stubs in =meshes.py=, are named explicitly (replacing the "=mesh_size= and
   friends"/"=plot_mesh*=" wildcard language) in this file and in
   =dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md=, and are covered by a parametrized
   =test_deleted_mesh_helpers_raise_by_name=.
4. *TOL2 cross-check*: the legacy =mesh_templates_test.py::test_mesh_sum=
   cross-checked the fused three-sphere mesh's volume against the sum of the
   three spheres meshed separately (at =TOL2=, looser than the analytic-volume
   =TOL1= because a combined mesh is discretised slightly differently than its
   components). This cross-check was not carried into the ported
   =test_template_mesh_sum_volume=; the port's =TOL2=1e-5= constant was
   declared but dead. Ported (not documented as a deviation): measured on Gmsh
   OCC for the same geometry (=r1,r2,r3=10,18,12=, =maxh=2.0=), fused volume
   =35613.142170867324= vs. separately-meshed sum =35612.955630276556=, a
   relative deviation of =~5.24e-6= -- comfortably inside =TOL2=1e-5=
   (reproducible across repeated runs). Note this is *looser*, not tighter,
   than the legacy Netgen comment's own =TOL2=1e-7=; Gmsh OCC is not
   unconditionally "more consistent" than Netgen here, but it is well within
   the tolerance this port had already declared for the purpose.

[Claude Sonnet 5]

** Remaining Optional Energies (Task 19)

=ThinFilmDemag= and =FixedEnergyDW= were the last two energy classes still
raw-importing legacy =dolfin= at module scope. Gate: folded into
=dolfinx-src-energies-pytest= (=test_thin_film_demag_dolfinx.py= and
=test_dw_fixed_energy_dolfinx.py=, two new files added to the existing pixi
command rather than a new gate), 45 tests total, folded into
=dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=.

*** ThinFilmDemag: direct port

Legacy's analytic thin-film demagnetising approximation: =Hj = Hk = 0=,
=Hi = -strength_i * m_i= for a single chosen axis =i= (=direction=, default
=\"z\"=). Legacy is not an =EnergyBase= subclass either (it hard-codes
=compute_energy() == 0= -- no energy functional is associated with this
field-only approximation), so the port keeps legacy's own bespoke,
non-=EnergyBase= structure exactly, the same way =zeeman.py= does for its
non-=EnergyBase= interactions.

=field_strength=, legacy's constructor argument, is =Ms= by default: when
=None=, =setup= box-averages the (possibly spatially varying) =Ms= Field onto
=m='s associated CG1 scalar space --
=df.assemble(Ms.f * TestFunction(S1) * dx).get_local() / volumes.get_local()=
in legacy, which is *exactly* the same lumped nodal-average math
=EnergyBase='s box-assemble path uses elsewhere in this port (transcribed via
the same =_assemble_vector_owned=/=_nodal_volume_owned= helpers, imported
from =energy_base.py=). Passing an explicit =field_strength= bypasses that
average entirely and is used verbatim -- legacy applies no validation or
coercion to a user-supplied =field_strength= either, so the port doesn't
either.

=compute_field()= recomputes fresh from the currently bound =m= on *every*
call (reads =self.m.as_array()= each time, exactly like legacy reads
=self.m.get_numpy_array_debug()= each time) -- it is not cached at =setup=
time. This is called out explicitly (and pinned by a dedicated test,
=test_compute_field_is_recomputed_fresh_every_call=) because of the
documented =DiscreteTimeZeeman= legacy caching defect elsewhere in this port
(Task 15): that history made "does this energy correctly reflect live state
on every call" worth confirming rather than assuming.

**** Serialisation-order deviation (documented, not a behavior change)

Legacy's =compute_field= returns =self.H.ravel()= where =self.H= is shape
=(3, n)= -- component-major (\"xxx\" convention: all-x, then all-y, then
all-z). The port instead returns the flat owned array in DOLFINx's own
interleaved-per-node dof order (matching every other ported interaction's
=Field.as_array()= convention: =m_values = self.m.as_array().reshape((-1,
3))=, write into column =self.direction=, =reshape(-1)= back). The physical
per-(node, component) values are bit-identical; only the serialisation order
changes, exactly like every other ported energy's =compute_field()= already
does relative to legacy. This is a deliberate, documented convention choice,
not an invented behavior.

**** Validation

- Transcribed legacy =thin_film_demag_test.py= invariants: =name= attribute
  (=test_interaction_accepts_name=), zero field for magnetisation
  perpendicular to =direction= (=test_zero_thin_film_demag=), exact analytic
  field for magnetisation aligned with =direction=
  (=test_thin_film_demag=), and the slow cross-check against the ported
  =FKDemag= on a thin-film-like bar
  (=test_thin_film_demag_against_real_demag=, =@pytest.mark.slow=, matched to
  legacy's exact mesh resolution =50x50x1= over =500nm x 500nm x 1nm= --
  a coarser =20x20x1= mesh initially undershot the legacy 5%/0.5% tolerances,
  confirming the mesh resolution genuinely matters for this cross-check, not
  just the formula).
- Two legacy oracle fixture cases
  (=src/finmag/tests/fixtures/thin_film_demag_oracle.json=, generated by
  =src/finmag/tests/fixtures/gen_thin_film_demag_oracle.py= at the frozen
  FEniCS-2019 oracle commit =ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687= via
  =dev/bin/run-legacy-oracle=): =varying_ms_default_strength= (direction
  =\"z\"=, =field_strength=None=, a spatially VARYING DG0 =Ms= -- this is the
  case that actually exercises the box-average machinery non-trivially, since
  the legacy test module only ever pins a *uniform* =Ms=) and
  =explicit_strength_x_direction= (direction =\"x\"=, an explicit scalar
  =field_strength=, confirming the direction-index dispatch and the
  \"bypasses the Ms lookup entirely\" branch). Both cases share the same
  =m = (0.6, 0.8 cos(2 pi x0), 0.8 sin(2 pi x0))= unit-norm expression already
  used by =gen_dmi_oracle.py=/=gen_cubic_varying_ms_oracle.py=.
- Standard construction/setup validation consistent with every other ported
  interaction: Field type checks, the CG1-magnetisation requirement
  (=_require_cg1_magnetisation=), matching-mesh requirement, and an invalid-
  =direction= =ValueError= (legacy used a bare =assert=; the port raises
  =ValueError= instead for consistency with the rest of this port's
  validation style -- the invalid-direction path is not legacy-tested, so
  this is a style choice, not a behavior regression).
- Legacy never wired =ThinFilmDemag= into =Simulation=/=sim_with= (grepping
  the legacy tree at the oracle commit finds it referenced only in one
  now-broken-output ipython notebook, plus its own module/test files); the
  only integration surface is =sim.add(ThinFilmDemag())=, ported and tested
  as such (=test_thin_film_demag_added_to_simulation_directly=).

*** FixedEnergyDW: curated by-name deferral, not a port (Task 29 review item)

Per the Task 19 escalation guidance (\"when in doubt between a shaky port and
a curated deferral, prefer the deferral\"), =FixedEnergyDW= is NOT ported.
Constructing it now raises =NotImplementedError= naming the class and the
rationale below, instead of the previous raw =ModuleNotFoundError= for
=dolfin=.

Rationale (full detail in the module docstring,
=src/finmag/energies/dw_fixed_energy.py=):

1. *Untested even on legacy master.* No =dw_fixed_energy_test.py= (or any
   test) exists anywhere in the legacy tree. The only usage is an
   interactive notebook
   (=doc/ipython_notebooks_src/ref-domain-wall-energy-class.ipynb=) whose own
   committed output cell IS a Python traceback, and legacy's own todo list
   records: \"[2014-01-16 Thu] The computation with the FixedEnergyDW class
   is broken.\" (=doc/ipython_notebooks_src/
   todo-notebooks-to-review-fix-write.txt=). There is no verified legacy
   behavior to port faithfully *to*.
2. *Depends on an already-deferred demag solver.* =__compute_field= always
   constructs =Demag(solver='Treecode')=; this port's own
   =finmag.energies.demag= package already defers the Treecode/GCR solver
   variants by name (only Fredkin-Koehler, =solver='FK'=, is ported --
   Task 23 territory). Porting =FixedEnergyDW= would require *also* porting
   a BEM solver variant that is independently out of scope for Task 19.
3. *Round-trips through legacy dolfin-XML on disk.* =bias_mesh=/=write_xml=
   serialise a hand-duplicated mesh to a bespoke =<dolfin>= XML file and
   immediately re-read it with =df.Mesh(filename)= -- a format DOLFINx
   cannot read, and legacy's own =write_xml= docstring already flags the
   function as broken (\"this function is broken at the moment\", attributed
   to Weiwei/Max in the source).
4. *Uses the raw legacy dolfin =Function=/vector API directly*
   (=Ms.vector().array()=, =m.vector().set_local()=) with no
   =finmag.field.Field=-mediated equivalent, so a \"faithful\" translation
   would require inventing untested semantics rather than transcribing
   verified ones.

This is a Task 29 review item: a full port would require an accompanying
Treecode/GCR demag solver port (Task 23 territory) plus inventing the legacy
XML-mesh-duplication semantics from scratch, with no way to validate the
result against a working legacy reference. Covered by
=test_dw_fixed_energy_dolfinx.py= (module import boundary, the
=NotImplementedError= contract, and that the deferral message names both the
Treecode dependency and \"Task 29\").

*** Lazy-export table and deferred-surfaces sweep

Both classes are now =requires_legacy_dolfin=False= in
=energies/__init__.py='s =_LAZY_EXPORTS= table -- zero optional energies
remain =True=. =test_import_boundary.py='s
=test_unported_energy_access_reports_the_real_missing_dependency= (which
probed the raw =ModuleNotFoundError= for =ThinFilmDemag=, the last such
class) is retired and replaced by
=test_no_unported_optional_energies_remain=, which asserts the
=_LAZY_EXPORTS= table directly instead of probing an error that no longer
occurs; =test_ported_energy_exports_bypass_legacy_dolfin= gains both classes.
=test_deferred_surfaces_dolfinx.py='s aggregated known-gap test is renamed
(from =..._and_the_others_remain_a_known_gap= to
=..._are_ported_or_curated_deferrals=) and updated to assert
=ThinFilmDemag().name == \"ThinFilmDemag\"= and the =FixedEnergyDW=
=NotImplementedError= by name, rather than the stale =ModuleNotFoundError=
pin.

[Claude Sonnet 5]

** Native Sundials/CVODE on DOLFINx (Task 20)

The modernized SUNDIALS-7 native wrapper (from Phase 1's M2/M3) is rebuilt
dolfin-free for the DOLFINx env, ~SundialsIntegrator~ is wired against the
ported deterministic ~LLG~, and ~backend="sundials"~ is a fully validated
opt-in. Gate: new =dolfinx-src-sundials-pytest= (18 tests after fix round 1;
17 in the initial slice), folded into =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=.

*** Build separation: objects/flags only, no wrapper-source edits

Every sundials source unit was audited for legacy =dolfin= coupling:
=src/sundials/py_sundials_module.cc=, =sundials_cvode_impl.h=,
=numpy_malloc.{h,cc}=, and =util/np_array.{h,cc}= contain zero dolfin symbols;
the only coupling is the shared umbrella header =src/finmag_includes.h=,
which pulls =dolfin.h= unless =-DFINMAG_NO_DOLFIN= (already added by Task 11a)
and =cvode.h= unless =-DFINMAG_NO_SUNDIALS=. This is exactly the Task 11a
=bem_arrays= situation: the separation is a pure compile-flag + link-set
change in the =IS_DOLFINX_ENV=-guarded branch of =native/Makefile= --
=MODULES= gains =sundials.so=, =SOURCES= gains the two non-dolfin sundials
source files, and =CFLAGS_NODOLFIN= drops =-DFINMAG_NO_SUNDIALS= (SUNDIALS is
now genuinely present in the env). No line of the wrapper itself was edited.
The per-env object tree (keyed on =PYTHON_PREFIX_TAG=) and per-env PCH are
inherited unchanged, so the DOLFINx (NumPy-2, no-dolfin) and legacy
(NumPy-1, dolfin) object trees never cross-link. The legacy and legacy-pixi
Makefile branches are structurally untouched (=make -n print-MODULES= on a
non-dolfinx prefix still yields the original module set).

*** Hook inventory (=src/finmag/physics/llg.py=)

The legacy default driver method is =bdf_gmres_prec_id=: SPGMR + a left
identity preconditioner + an analytic Jacobian-times-vector product. All four
hooks that path needs:

- =sundials_rhs(t,y,ydot)= -- already real (backend-neutral =solve_for=);
  unchanged by this task.
- =sundials_psetup(t,m,fy,jok,gamma,tmp1,tmp2,tmp3)= -- ported. Records the
  linearisation state when =jok= is false and sets the vestigial
  =self._reuse_jacobian= flag; the flag is set for parity with the legacy
  wrapper's bookkeeping but is not read anywhere in the ported =jtimes= path
  below (see the pure-NumPy jtimes decision).
- =sundials_psolve(t,y,fy,r,z,gamma,delta,lr,tmp)= -- ported. Identity
  preconditioner, =z[:] = r=.
- =sundials_jtimes(mp,J_mp,t,m,fy,tmp)= -- ported. Analytic
  ~J(m,t) mp = d/da rhs(m + a mp, H(m + a mp))|_{a=0}~, with
  ~H' = H(mp)~ from the ported =EffectiveField.compute_jacobian_only=.

**** The pure-NumPy jtimes decision (and the vestigial =_reuse_jacobian= flag)

The node-local Jacobian kernel =_jtimes_numpy= is a direct NumPy
transcription of native =dm_precession_i=/=dm_damping_i=/=dm_relaxation_i=
(=native/src/llg/llg.cc=), the same transcription pattern =_dmdt_numpy=
already uses for =calc_llg_dmdt=. This means every call to =sundials_jtimes=
recomputes the effective-field directional derivative from scratch via
=EffectiveField.compute_jacobian_only=, rather than reusing a cached
Jacobian the way the legacy wrapper's =_reuse_jacobian=/=jok= bookkeeping was
designed to allow. The flag is still set in =sundials_psetup= (matching the
legacy call signature CVODE expects) but nothing in the ported path branches
on it -- it is vestigial in this port. This only affects Krylov linear-solve
*speed* (recomputing per call vs reusing a stale Jacobian between CVODE's own
=jok= decisions), never the *trajectory*, which is controlled by
reltol/abstol; the cross-backend agreement and finite-difference checks below
validate that the recomputed-every-call analytic Jacobian is correct.

Validation: =sundials_jtimes= is checked against a symmetric finite-difference
of =sundials_rhs= and matches to *<1e-5 relative* -- direct evidence the
analytic transcription is correct, independent of any downstream integration
outcome.

**** Remaining by-name hooks

=use_slonczewski=/=use_zhangli= (spin-transfer torque) remain by-name
=NotImplementedError=, unchanged -- neither is needed by the default
=bdf_gmres_prec_id= path, and both are already tracked as Task 29 items. No
new by-name registration was required for this slice.

*** Cross-backend agreement

Same Task 9/10 core workflow (Exchange 13pJ/m + Zeeman (0,0,1e6) +
UniaxialAnisotropy 1e5 z on a 5nm box, alpha=0.5, m0=+x), both integrators at
reltol=1e-8/abstol=1e-10, =run_until(1e-11)=:
~max|m_scipy(t_end) - m_sundials(t_end)| < 1e-6~. Each integrator controls
local truncation error to reltol=1e-8/abstol=1e-10; the cross-integrator
difference at t_end is dominated by independent accumulation of that
controlled error, so ~1e-6 (~100x rtol, |m| ~ O(1)) is the physically
justified bound. Honest caveat: this is validated only over the short
=1e-11= s horizon the Task 9/10 workflow uses -- it is not a claim that the
two backends agree to 1e-6 over arbitrarily long integration windows, where
independent error accumulation from two different stiff-ODE codes could grow
further apart. The default =bdf_gmres_prec_id= sundials path is what's
exercised here, so this number also validates the analytic jtimes + identity
preconditioner hooks end-to-end, not just the rhs.

*** Version note: SUNDIALS 7.8.0 (dolfinx) vs 7.2.1 (legacy default)

=pixi.toml='s =[feature.dolfinx.dependencies]= adds ~sundials = ">=7,<8"~,
pinned to the 7.x major the wrapper's SUNContext/SUNNonlinSol/
=CVodeSetLinearSolver= wiring targets. The dolfinx env resolves *7.8.0*
(=sundials-7.8.0-h8a7dba9_0=, =pixi.lock= +126 lines); the legacy default env
keeps *7.2.1*. Both are SUNDIALS-7 major and the wrapper's 7.x API usage is
stable across the minor-version gap -- no new compatibility shims were added
to bridge 7.2 vs 7.8; the same wrapper source builds and passes against both.

*** Default-backend resolution (Task 20 review, fix round 1)

The initial Task 20 slice validated =backend="sundials"= as a fully working
opt-in but conservatively left the =llg_integrator= factory default at
=\"scipy\"=, deferring the default-flip decision to review. The Task 20
review's controller decision: flip =llg_integrator='s =backend= default back
to =\"sundials\"=, restoring the legacy default semantics, per the original
plan wording (\"restore the legacy default-backend semantics ... ONLY if the
sundials path passes the full validation\" -- validation had already passed).
This is low blast radius: =finmag.sim.sim.Simulation= passes
=backend=self.integrator_backend= explicitly in both =create_integrator= and
=reset_time=, and every other in-tree caller passes =backend= explicitly
(verified in the Task 8 review) -- nothing in the tree relied on the bare
=llg_integrator(llg, m0)= default. Without the native extension built, the
bare call now raises =ImportError= by name (the same failure mode an explicit
=backend="sundials"= request always had; only *which* call sites trigger it
changed).

=Simulation.integrator_backend='s own default is a *separate* knob and stays
=\"scipy\"= -- Phase 1 Task 8/9 sanctioned it as the temporary DOLFINx
default, and flipping it would couple every =run_until= gate across the M5
suite (simulation, restart-output, demag, dmi, cubicanis, timezeeman,
varparams, meshes) to the native sundials build and to the CVODE trajectory,
a larger re-validation deliberately not done in this slice.

**** USER ACCEPTANCE PENDING

~Simulation.integrator_backend~ default is ~"scipy"~ on DOLFINx vs legacy
~"sundials"~ -- temporary, pending native-default hardening or user
acceptance. (Mirrored in =dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md= and the Task 20 section
of =docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-21-dolfinx-full-parity.md=; cross-referenced
from =test_simulation_dolfinx.py::test_construction_core_state='s docstring.)

*** Gate-count flips

- =dolfinx-src-sundials-pytest=: 17 (initial slice) -> 18 (fix round 1 adds
  =test_llg_integrator_default_backend_is_sundials_and_advances=, a bare
  ~llg_integrator(llg, m0)~ call with no =backend= argument, guarded on
  sundials availability, asserting both construction of a
  =SundialsIntegrator= and a working =advance_time=).
- =dolfinx-src-scipy-pytest=: unchanged total (18 passed + 1 skipped in the
  initial slice's environment, later re-measured as 18 passed + 2 skipped once
  the availability-guarded default-backend pin pair was added -- see the
  count-change note below); the "checklist item 6" section's
  =test_llg_integrator_default_backend_is_scipy= is retired and replaced by
  the availability-guarded pair
  =test_llg_integrator_default_backend_is_sundials= /
  =test_llg_integrator_default_backend_raises_when_sundials_unavailable=,
  exactly one of which runs (the other skips) in any given environment.
- =dolfinx-src-simulation-pytest=: unchanged (32 passed); only a docstring
