[Claude Sonnet 5]

** Packaging (Task 21)

*** Correcting the record

The commit message for =82b0fde7= ("Make finmag installable: pyproject
packaging for the DOLFINx lane") stated that it was "finishing the 'not the
final M2 install story' note left in transition-notes.org" -- but that
commit did not actually edit this file. This section is the real closure of
the M2-era note above ("that is acceptable for the first probe, but it is
not the final M2 install story"), written in the review fix round that
followed =82b0fde7=, not in that commit itself.

*** The final install story

=src/finmag= is now installed as a regular editable Python package in the
=dolfinx= pixi environment via a new top-level =pyproject.toml= (setuptools
build backend, src-layout, =package-dir={"": "src"}=). The supported
sequence is:

- =pixi install -e dolfinx= -- sync the conda/pixi environment.
- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-install-editable= -- =python -m pip install -e
  . --no-deps --no-build-isolation= (see the =pixi.toml= comment on this
  task for why both flags are needed; =python -m pip=, not a bare =pip=, to
  avoid the wrong-interpreter finding below).
- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-native-build= -- the documented second step of
  the native-extension story: compiles =bem_arrays.so=/=sundials.so= via the
  existing =native/Makefile= dolfinx lane (no build-backend hook).
- =pixi run -e dolfinx dolfinx-provenance-check= -- asserts =import finmag=
  resolves under this checkout's =src/= tree, not some other installed copy.

Every =dolfinx-src-*= gate (22 tasks) now runs against this installed
package instead of =PYTHONPATH=src=; exactly one task,
=dolfinx-src-import-pythonpath-fallback=, keeps the old =PYTHONPATH=src=
mechanism exercised as a retained fallback/gate step, per the Task 21 brief.
=dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= runs this whole sequence and is green end-to-end
against the installed package.

*** Dependency-split rationale

=pyproject.toml='s =[project.dependencies]= lists only =numpy=/=scipy= --
the only two third-party libraries actually imported at module scope
anywhere on the reachable-in-the-DOLFINx-lane import paths (verified by
grepping =src/finmag= and cross-checked against the =dolfinx= pixi
environment, which does not list =matplotlib=/=aeon=/=sh=). The finite
element/MPI/SUNDIALS stack (=fenics-dolfinx=, =ufl=, =mpi4py=, =petsc4py=,
=sundials=) stays a conda/pixi dependency (=pixi.toml='s =dolfinx= feature),
never a pip one: those are not reliably pip-installable for this project and
must stay linked against the conda-provided build: declaring them as pip
dependencies too would invite pip to resolve/build a second, conflicting
copy.

*** The static =0.1.0= vs. git-SHA version split

=pyproject.toml='s =project.version = "0.1.0"= is static packaging metadata
only, pinned to match =pixi.toml='s own workspace =version=. It has no
runtime meaning. Runtime provenance is, and remains, =finmag.__version__= --
a git revision SHA written into =src/finmag/__version__.py= by
=native/Makefile='s =add_version= target (gated by the =WRITE_FINMAG_VERSION=
convention). The two are intentionally decoupled: wiring =project.version=
as =dynamic= off =finmag.__version__= was tried and fails hard --
=pip install -e .= raises =packaging.version.InvalidVersion= because
setuptools' =egg_info= normalizes/validates whatever an =attr:= version
returns as a PEP 440 version, and a raw git SHA is not a valid PEP 440
version string. Reformatting the git-SHA convention itself would be a
behavior change to =src/finmag= and was out of scope for a packaging-only
task.

*** Finding: a bare =pip= can resolve to the wrong interpreter

Before =pip=/=setuptools= were added to =[feature.dolfinx.dependencies]= in
=pixi.toml=, the =dolfinx= pixi environment had no =pip= conda package of its
own. Running a bare =pip install -e . --no-deps= inside =pixi run -e
dolfinx= silently resolved =pip= to the base conda/system installation on
=PATH= (not the pixi environment's), installing =finmag= into the wrong
=site-packages= entirely (confirmed via =pip show finmag= pointing at
=~/miniconda3/lib/python3.12/site-packages= instead of
=.pixi/envs/dolfinx/...=). Fixed by adding =pip=/=setuptools= to the
=dolfinx= feature and using =python -m pip ...= (which always resolves to
the active interpreter's own pip module) in every packaging task, instead of
a bare =pip=.

*** Wheel non-goal

A non-editable, built wheel is an explicit non-goal for this lane, not an
oversight: the compiled =.so= native extensions (=bem_arrays.so=,
=sundials.so=) are linked directly against this specific conda/pixi
environment's compiler ABI, Boost.Python tag, and SUNDIALS-7 sonames, so a
built wheel would not be portable/redistributable the way a wheel implies.
The supported, and only, install path for this lane is the editable install
above.

[Claude Sonnet 5]

** STT (Task 22)

The Slonczewski/Xiao and Zhang-Li spin-transfer torques are ported into the
ported deterministic ~LLG~ as pure-NumPy transcriptions of the native STT
kernels. The compiled STT kernels are NOT rebuilt for the DOLFINx lane -- same
decision, and same rationale, as the =_dmdt_numpy= transcription of
=calc_llg_dmdt= in Task 7 and the =_jtimes_numpy= transcription in Task 20:
the node-local physics is a handful of vector ops, so a faithful NumPy copy
keyed to the C++ source lines is simpler, dolfin-free, and just as exact as
re-linking the OpenMP kernel. Gate: new =dolfinx-src-stt-pytest= (14 tests),
folded into =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=.

*** Formula transcription (native line -> NumPy line)

All citations are to =native/src/llg/llg.cc= at the oracle
(=ba9280934e188d7f3800e7b9865e70a9422f7687=). Physical constants
(=e=1.602176565e-19=, =h_bar=1.054571726e-34=, =mu_B=9.27400968e-24=,
=mu_0=pi*4e-7=) are transcribed verbatim from llg.cc:21-25 into module-level
constants in =llg.py=, deliberately NOT from scipy CODATA, so the RHS matches
the compiled kernel bit-for-bit.

Slonczewski (=slonczewski_xiao_i=, llg.cc:207; driver =calc_llg_slonczewski_dmdt=,
llg.cc:252) -> =LLG._dmdt_slonczewski_numpy=:
- =gamma_LL = gamma/(1+alpha^2)= (llg.cc:214).
- =beta = J*h_bar/(mu_0*Ms*e*d)= (llg.cc:216). =Ms= is PER NODE (see below).
- =epsilon = P*lambda^2/(lambda^2+1+(lambda^2-1)*(m.p))= (llg.cc:217).
- =perpendicular = alpha*epsilon - epsilonprime= (llg.cc:223);
  =parallel = epsilon - alpha*epsilonprime= (llg.cc:224).
- =dm += gamma_LL*beta*(perp * (m x p) - par * (mp*m - mm*p))= (llg.cc:225-227),
  where =mp*m - mm*p= is the BAC-CAB expansion of =m x (m x p)=.
- The driver adds =damping_i= (llg.cc:35), =relaxation_i= (llg.cc:105) and
  =precession_i= (llg.cc:74) around the torque; precession is UNCONDITIONAL in
  the STT kernel (llg.cc:289), so the STT RHS includes precession regardless of
  =do_precession=. Transcribed identically.

Zhang-Li (=calc_llg_zhang_li_dmdt=, llg.cc:406) -> =LLG._dmdt_zhangli_numpy=:
- =coeff_stt = u0/(1+alpha^2)/Ms=, zero where =Ms==0= (llg.cc:439-445).
- perpendicular projection of the gradient field: =mht = m.H_gradm=,
  =hp = H_gradm - mht*m= (llg.cc:447-451).
- =mth = m x hp= (llg.cc:453-455).
- =dm = coeff_stt*((1+alpha*beta)*hp - (beta-alpha)*mth)= (llg.cc:457-459),
  followed by damping/relaxation/precession (precession unconditional,
  llg.cc:464).
- =u0 = P*mu_B/e=, divided by =1+beta^2= unless =using_u0= (=use_zhangli=,
  matching the legacy =u0= computation).

*** The Zhang-Li discrete gradient operator

=H_gradm = (J.grad) m= is the one place the physics is not node-local, so its
discretization must be transcribed, not reinvented (textbook central
differences would be a different operator). Legacy =compute_gradient_matrix=
assembles the matrix =gradM= from the bilinear form
=sum_k J_k * dot(grad(tau)[:,k], sigma) * dx= (=tau= trial, =sigma= test, both
on the vector CG1 space; =J_k= the k-th component of the current-density
Function), then forms =H_gradm = gradM @ m / nodal_volume_S3=. Because
=gradM @ m= is exactly that linear form evaluated at the current =m=, the port
(=LLG._compute_zhangli_gradient=) assembles the linear functional
=sum_k J_k * dot(grad(m.f)[:,k], sigma) * dx= directly -- no stored matrix,
identical result, and it automatically picks up a time-updated =J=. The
lumped divisor is =nodal_volume(S3) * unit_length= (llg legacy
=compute_gradient_matrix=): the single =unit_length= power converts the one
spatial derivative from mesh units to physical units (assemble uses
mesh-coordinate =dx= and =grad=). This is pinned DIRECTLY by the =H_gradm=
quantity in the Zhang-Li oracle fixture (see below), independent of the dm/dt
pin.

*** Per-node Ms

The native STT kernels index =Ms[i]= per NODE, but the ported =LLG._Ms= is the
DG0 (per-cell) array. =LLG._ms_nodal= reproduces the legacy =Ms= setter
(=llg.py:140-142= at the oracle): a lumped-mass projection of the DG0 =Ms= onto
the scalar CG1 nodes, =assemble(Ms*v_S1*dx)/assemble(v_S1*dx)=, returned in
coordinate-ordered (owned-vertex) layout aligned with the m/H node columns. For
a spatially uniform =Ms= this is exactly the constant at every node.

*** Oracle fixtures

Both generated at the frozen FEniCS-2019 oracle via
=dev/bin/run-legacy-oracle=, 1D =IntervalMesh(4, 0, 4)= (identical connectivity
in dolfin and dolfinx, isolating STT physics from tetrahedralisation), schema
v1, coordinate-sorted:
- =src/finmag/tests/fixtures/slonczewski_rhs.json= (generator
  =gen_slonczewski_rhs.py=): uniform =m=(0.6,0,0.8)= at an angle to =p=(0,0,1)=
  (=m.p=0.8=, not parallel), =J=1e12=, =P=0.4=, =d=2e-9=, =Lambda=2=,
  =epsilonprime=0.1= (nonzero secondary torque), plus Zeeman. Pins =m=,
  =effective_field=, =dmdt= (dmdt tol abs=1.0, rel=1e-9).
- =src/finmag/tests/fixtures/zhangli_rhs.json= (generator =gen_zhangli_rhs.py=):
  spatially varying =m=(cos0.4x, sin0.4x, 0.5)= so =(J.grad)m != 0=,
  =J=(1e12,0,0)=, =P=0.5=, =beta=0.02=, plus Exchange + Zeeman. Pins =m=,
  =effective_field=, the discrete =H_gradm= (tol abs=1e-3, rel=1e-9), and
  =dmdt= (tol abs=1.0, rel=1e-9), plus a =u0= scalar pin.

Tolerances are tight enough that a sign or prefactor error cannot hide (the
dmdt magnitudes are ~1e11 1/s with abs tol 1.0; H_gradm ~1e20 1/m with rel tol
1e-9).

*** Documented deviations (deliberate design choices)

The following implementation choices are deliberate deviations representing
design trade-offs accepted during the port:

- *Mutual exclusivity of STT flags*: the ported =use_slonczewski=/=use_zhangli=
  each disable the other flag (last call wins); legacy set only its own flag and
  its =solve= dispatch made Slonczewski win if both were enabled. Corner-case
  behavioural deviation, deliberate. [Claude Haiku 4.5]

- *Ordering self-consistency*: legacy passed raw dof-ordered =J=/=Ms=/=p= arrays
  into the native STT kernels alongside coordinate-ordered =m=/=H=/=alpha= (a
  latent legacy inconsistency, invisible for uniform parameters); the port uses
  coordinate-ordered arrays throughout. Identical results for all pinned cases;
  for spatially varying =J=/=Ms=/=p= under legacy dolfin dof reordering, legacy
  and port would differ --- the port is the self-consistent physics. DELIBERATE
  DEVIATION. [Claude Haiku 4.5]

- *Known pin limit*: the 1D Zhang-Li =H_gradm= fixture cannot distinguish the
  lumped-box FEM gradient operator from a plain finite-difference scheme (they
  coincide in 1D); the implementation is form-based like legacy, so 2D/3D
  generalisation matches legacy by construction. [Claude Haiku 4.5]

*** Analytic pins and dynamics witnesses (=test_stt_dolfinx.py=)

- Slonczewski torque is linear in =J= (=t(2J)==2 t(J)=), flips sign with =J=,
  and VANISHES exactly when =m || p= (both =m x p= and =m x (m x p)= are zero,
  llg.cc:225-227, even with =epsilonprime != 0=) -- preserving what the native
  kernel does at the singular alignment.
- Zhang-Li STT vanishes for uniform =m= (gradient zero), is linear in =J=, and
  flips sign with =J=.
- Zhang-Li DOMAIN-WALL displacement witness: direct port of the legacy
  =zhang_li_test.test_zhangli= invariant (=IntervalMesh(50,0,100)=, Exchange +
  UniaxialAnisotropy, =alpha=0.01=, =J=1e12=, =beta=0.02=): after
  =run_until(2e-12)= the wall moves along +u so =m_average[0]= drops from ~0 to
  a resolvably negative value (=< -1e-3=).
- Slonczewski TILT witness: for =m= near =p=+z=, the change in =m_z= over a
  short run has OPPOSITE sign for +J and -J (the anti-damping/damping character
  of the torque flips with current), the classic spin-torque behaviour.
- scipy-vs-sundials cross-backend agreement on the Zhang-Li domain-wall case
  (=m_average= agree to rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-6).

*** =llg_stt.py= (=LLG_STT=): deferred by name, distinct capability

=src/finmag/physics/llg_stt.py= is the NONLOCAL spin-transfer-torque class
(=set_parameters(D, lambda_sf, lambda_J, ...)=, native
=calc_llg_nonlocal_stt_dmdt=, llg.cc:472) -- a spin-accumulation model with an
auxiliary field =delta_m= and a =2*nxyz= state vector, NOT the in-LLG
Slonczewski/Zhang-Li flags. It is reached only via =Simulation(kernel="llg_stt")=,
which is already a by-name deferral (=test_nonstandard_kernels_are_deferred=).
Task 22 therefore leaves =LLG_STT= deferred by name (distinct capability,
Task 29-registered), and ports only the in-LLG =use_slonczewski= /
=use_zhangli= surfaces and their =Simulation= pass-throughs
(=set_stt=/=toggle_stt=/=set_zhangli=).

*** Time-dependent current

Both legacy =set_*= accepted a =with_time_update= callback and Task 22 preserves
it: Slonczewski's =fun_slonczewski_time_update(t)= returns a spatially uniform
current density (a number) broadcast over =self.J[:]= each RHS eval;
Zhang-Li's =fun_zhangli_time_update(t)= returns a new =J= profile, re-set on the
current-density Field so the next gradient assembly picks it up (the legacy
gradient-matrix rebuild). Both match the legacy =solve= dispatch exactly.

*** Test-count deltas

- New gate =dolfinx-src-stt-pytest=: 14 tests.
- =test_llg_dolfinx.py=: 17 -> 16. The 2-case parametrized
  =test_deferred_surfaces_raise_by_name[use_slonczewski|use_zhangli]= (STT
  raising by name) is replaced by the single ported witness
  =test_stt_surfaces_are_ported_not_deferred= (both surfaces now activate their
  flag). Net -1, as expected for a deferral->port flip.
- =test_simulation_dolfinx.py=: 32 -> 32. =test_stt_is_deferred= became
  =test_stt_is_ported_not_deferred= (same count).
- =test_deferred_surfaces_dolfinx.py=: 1p/4s unchanged (the ported-surfaces
  docstring inventory updated to move STT to the ported list).

[Claude Opus 4.8]

** Treecode/PBC demag (Task 23)

Task 23 is the third native-extension slice: it rebuilds the =treecode_bem=
Cython extension for the DOLFINx env and ports the two demag surfaces that
consume it -- the treecode-accelerated Fredkin-Koehler solver
(=Demag(solver='Treecode')=) and the periodic macro-geometry demag
(=Demag(macrogeometry=MacroGeometry(...))=). Both ride the Task 11b ported
=FKDemag= unchanged except for the one boundary-element step each replaces.

*** dolfin-coupling audit (checklist item 1)

The native sources =native/src/treecode_bem/= (=common.{c,h}=, =bem_pbc.c=,
=treecode_bem_I.c=, =treecode_bem_II.c=, =treecode_bem_lib.pyx=) were audited
symbol-by-symbol: =grep -niE 'dolfin|swig|boost'= returns nothing. They are
pure C octree fast-summation + Lindholm double-layer BEM kernels facing NumPy
through Cython buffer syntax -- NO libdolfin, NO Boost.Python, NO SWIG-DOLFIN.
This is unlike =bem_arrays=/=sundials= (Boost.Python C++): =treecode_bem= is a
*Cython* extension built by its own =setup.py=. The built
=treecode_bem.cpython-312-*.so= links only =libgomp= and =libm= (=ldd=
confirmed: no libdolfin/libboost), and importing it leaves =sys.modules= free
of =dolfin=. The Python consumers import the native symbols lazily (inside
=setup=/matvec), so plain =import finmag= never pulls the extension.

*** Cython / NumPy-2 porting inventory (checklist item 1)

The module had NEVER been built on any modern lane (audit M4: collected-skip in
the pixi M3 gate). Porting it to Python 3.12 / Cython-current / NumPy 2.x:

- =setup.py=: rewritten from the removed-on-3.12 =distutils= +
  =Cython.Distutils.build_ext= to =setuptools= + =Cython.Build.cythonize=
  (=compiler_directives={'language_level': '3'}=). Added =-fopenmp= to
  =extra_link_args= (was compile-only).
- =treecode_bem_lib.pyx=: added a =# cython: language_level=3= header; removed
  a duplicate =compute_boundary_element= def (the second, identical definition
  was dead). No Python-2 statements existed in the code itself (only in
  comments); the =np.ndarray[double, ndim=2, mode="c"]= buffer syntax and
  =np.ndarray[int, ...]= (C =int= = int32, callers already pass =dtype=np.int32=)
  compile cleanly under NumPy 2.4.6. The deprecated-NumPy-C-API warning is
  emitted but is non-fatal; =NPY_NO_DEPRECATED_API= was deliberately NOT set
  (it would break the legacy buffer protocol the =.pyx= relies on).
- =native/Makefile=: added =treecode_bem.so= to the DOLFINx =MODULES= list and
  rewrote its rule for Task 11b per-env hygiene. Two build subtleties:
  1. The conda compiler activation exports the Makefile's own =CFLAGS= (finmag
     PCH =-include=, =-DFINMAG_NO_DOLFIN=, =-std=c++0x=) into the =setup.py=
     subshell, poisoning the C build (a =.h= PCH cannot be =-include='d into a C
     compile). Fixed by clearing =CFLAGS== for the treecode recipe only -- the
     C kernels need none of the finmag C++/PCH flags; =setup.py= supplies the
     NumPy include and =-fopenmp=/=-lm= itself.
  2. Object files build into a per-prefix =OBJ_DIR/treecode_bem= build-temp so
     legacy and DOLFINx ABIs never share a path; the ABI-tagged =.so= is also
     copied to the plain =treecode_bem.so= target name so =make= sees the target
     satisfied and does not rebuild every invocation.
- =pixi.toml=: added =cython = "*"= to =[feature.dolfinx.dependencies]= (genuine
  build-time dependency; =pixi.lock= changed). It is compile-time only -- the
  extension is dolfin-free and lazily imported, so =import finmag= never pulls
  Cython.

*** Validation basis: cross-method + analytic, NOT oracle fixtures

This is the plan's accepted-evidence exception. The frozen FEniCS-2019 oracle
env itself never built =treecode_bem= (the =demag_pbc_test.py= was a collected
SKIP on every modern lane and last green only on the ancient M1 docker lane),
so there are NO obtainable treecode/PBC oracle reference numbers. Validation is
therefore by cross-method comparison against the already-oracle-validated dense
FK demag (Task 11b) plus analytic limits:

1. *Single-tile periodic BEM == dense golden FK BEM, bit-for-bit.* The periodic
   BEM assembled with =Ts=[(0,0,0)]= (one tile) reproduces the Task 11a golden
   dense Fredkin-Koehler BEM matrix to =max|diff| = 3.5e-17= on the Kuhn unit
   cube. This proves the treecode Lindholm kernels use the *same*
   convention/orientation as the array BEM, and that the ported
   coordinate-driven boundary extraction (=boundary_bem_arrays=) feeds them
   consistently. Row sums are -1 (closed-surface solid-angle identity).
2. *Treecode field cross-checks the dense FK demag.* On a 30nm cube the
   treecode demag field matches the dense FK field to =rel < 1e-6= in the
   direct-sum limit (=mac=0.7=; the octree accepts no multipole approximation
   and evaluates the boundary sum exactly).

   **Round-1 review correction (approximation-regime witness, [Claude Sonnet
   5]).** The original claim here -- "at the legacy default knobs (mac=0.3,
   p=3) the agreement is ~4e-5, the documented treecode accuracy" -- was
   overclaimed: it was never reproduced on the *box* geometries actually used
   by the encoded tests, and a review sweep (=mac \in {0.2..0.9}=, =p \in
   {1,3,5,8}=, =num_limit \in {1,5,20,100}=, boundaries up to 1178 nodes)
   confirmed treecode-vs-FK stays at machine precision (=~1e-14=) for *every*
   combination on compact convex boundaries (cubes/spheres) -- the multipole
   approximation is simply never entered on those geometries at these knobs,
   so the test's 5e-3 bound was vacuous.

   *Why* (cited from =native/src/treecode_bem/=): the far-field acceptance
   test in =treecode_bem_I.c= (=bulid_indices_single_I= and
   =compute_potential_single_target_I=, e.g. line 18/175:
   =plan->mac_square * R > tree->radius_square=) compares the squared distance
   =R= from a query point to an octree cluster's centre against that cluster's
   own bounding =radius_square=. On a compact convex boundary, every
   evaluation point sits ON the same surface whose diameter bounds every
   cluster's distance from any other point on it, so no cluster can ever be
   "far enough" relative to its own size at any reasonable =mac< 1= -- the
   near-field sparse matrix built once by =bulid_indices_I= (the exact Lindholm
   sum for every rejected cluster) ends up covering the *entire* sum, and the
   multipole/moment path in =compute_potential_single_target_I= is never
   exercised. This is a property of compact convex geometry, not a porting
   defect, and it is reproducible with the ported code (confirmed
   independently here, not merely inferred).

   *Genuine approximation-regime witness (measured, replaces the
   unreproducible figure).* A 50:1 aspect-ratio bar (6nm x 6nm x 300nm, 490
   boundary nodes) gives the octree well-separated end clusters and DOES enter
   the approximation regime. Measured on this exact geometry (=p=3,
   num_limit=100, correct_factor=10= -- legacy defaults except =mac=):
   =mac=0.7 -> 8.818e-4=; =mac=0.5 -> 3.230e-4=; =mac=0.3 -> 4.628e-5=;
   =mac=0.1 -> 3.059e-7=. This reproduces (on the *correct* geometry) the
   originally-claimed =~4e-5 at mac=0.3= figure almost exactly, and the trend
   over this range is monotonic: tightening =mac= (smaller value, stricter
   far-field acceptance) strictly improves accuracy. Encoded as
   =test_treecode_approximation_regime_reachable_on_elongated_bar= in
   =test_treecode_pbc_demag_dolfinx.py=.

   *A mac non-monotonicity, documented not hidden.* =mac= also gates the
   octree's *subdivision*-stopping criterion in =common.c='s =create_tree=
   (line ~569: =tree->num_particle > plan->num_limit && r2*(1-plan->mac) >=
   plan->r_eps*plan->mac=). As =mac -> 1= this condition stops the tree from
   subdividing at all (single-node root), so the near-field path again covers
   everything and accuracy jumps back to machine precision regardless of
   geometry -- measured =mac=0.9 -> 9.377e-15= on the same bar. This is a
   degenerate case outside the monotonic range asserted in the test (=mac \in
   {0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7}=), not a counterexample to the trend; =mac= plays a dual
   role (subdivision threshold AND far-field acceptance threshold) that is
   worth knowing about but does not need its own witness.

   *=p= confirmed dead, from source and empirically.* The multipole expansion
   in =compute_coefficient_directly=/=compute_moment_directly= (=common.c=,
   ~lines 916-993) is hard-coded to a fixed 35-term (4th order) array --
   literally commented "Always suppose N=35, n=5,
   size=n*(n+1)*(n+2)/6" -- and never indexes past =a[20]=/=mom[35]= using the
   =p= argument threaded through the API. Measured =p \in {1,3,5,8}= at
   =mac=0.3= on the bar witness: identical =rel=4.628e-05= to 9+ significant
   figures for every =p=. The near-field =correct_factor= (which sets =r_eps=
   and therefore where subdivision stops, hence how large the residual leaves
   are and how much of the sum multipole ever touches) DOES move accuracy --
   measured on the same bar at =mac=0.3=: =correct_factor=1 -> 2.006e-4=;
   ==5 -> 2.006e-4=; ==10 (default) -> 4.628e-5=; ==20 -> 1.2e-5=; ==50 ->
   9.377e-15= (back to the direct-sum limit, same mechanism as the =mac->1=
   case above: a larger =r_eps= stops subdivision earlier).
3. *Analytic checks.* A uniformly-magnetised sphere (generated mesh) gives the
   treecode demag factor =Nz ~ 1/3= (within 0.05). A thin film tiled
   periodically in-plane and magnetised out of plane converges to the analytic
   =Nz -> 1= limit (=<Hz>/Ms -> -1=, error < 1e-3 already at 3x3 tiles).
4. *Periodic image-sum convergence* (the plan's named PBC sanity). A cube tiled
   along x approaches an infinite chain: =<Hx>/Ms= descends monotonically
   =-0.334 -> -0.165 -> -0.125 -> -0.109= for =nx = 1,3,5,9= with shrinking
   increments toward a stable limit.

   **Round-1 review note on the plateau value ([Claude Sonnet 5]).** The
   encoded test (=test_pbc_image_sum_converges_1d=) asserts only the
   monotonic-decrease trend up to =nx=9=, deliberately, and does not pin a
   numeric plateau: there is no independent closed-form expression for the
   =nx -> infinity= limit of a periodic array of finite cubes, so any pinned
   number would just be this same method run at higher image counts, not
   independent validation. For context (not asserted, not encoded as a fast
   test -- image count does not blow up mesh DOFs, so this ran in a few
   seconds per point, but is not worth adding to the hot gate): extending the
   same sweep to =nx=51,71,101,151,251,351= on this mesh gives =-0.10101,
   -0.10089, -0.10083, -0.10080, -0.10078, -0.10077=, consistent with a slow
   =~1/nx= approach to a plateau near =-0.1007= (least-squares =a + b/nx= fit).
   This is an extrapolation of the same numerical method, not an
   independently-derived limit -- it corroborates the originally-documented
   "~-0.10 at nx=31" figure (still directionally correct) without over-pinning
   precision the port cannot independently justify. The MacroGeometry path's
   actual independent analytic anchor is item 3 above (the thin-film =Nz -> 1=
   limit), which the test docstring now says explicitly.

*** Periodic-BEM faithfulness and a documented conditional-convergence caveat

=build_periodic_bem= reproduces the legacy =BMatrixPBC.compute_bmatrix=
algorithm exactly: sum =build_boundary_matrix= (Lindholm) over the translation
lattice =Ts=, then add the boundary solid-angle diagonal =once=. Because the
single-tile case is bit-for-bit identical to the oracle-validated dense BEM and
the multi-tile summation is the identical algorithm, the port is faithful to
legacy. Two honest caveats, both inherent to the finite periodic dipole sum
(not port defects):
- The legacy =demag_pbc_test.py::test_field_1d= asserted a 3-tile periodic
  sample (=mesh_1=, a 20nm cube, =nx=3=) matches a finite long bar (=mesh_3=, a
  60nm x 20nm x 20nm bar, =nx=1=) to 1.2%. That is NOT reproduced and NOT
  asserted here.

  **Round-1 review correction ([Claude Sonnet 5]).** The prior headline here
  -- "the periodic image sum converges to the infinite-array limit, which is a
  *different* physical quantity from a finite bar" -- was imprecise: =mesh_1=
  with =nx=3= is a *finite* 3-image periodic sum, not an infinite-array limit,
  and it is being compared against another finite quantity (a genuinely 3x
  longer, non-periodic bar), so "different physical quantity" overstates the
  case: both sides approximate the same finite-bar-like configuration, just by
  different constructions. The REAL and now EMPIRICALLY VERIFIED reason the
  legacy numbers are not reproduced/asserted is the *next* bullet:
  =mesh_1='s inferred tile spacing (=dx=20=, from the mesh's own bounding-box
  extent) exactly equals its own extent, i.e. =nx=3= tiles it with adjacent
  tiles' boundary faces touching -- exactly the coincident-node
  ill-conditioning case documented below. Directly probing
  =BMatrixPBC(mesh_1, Ts=compute_Ts(...))= at this spacing confirms it: of 98
  boundary DOFs, 74 have the expected solid-angle row sum =~-1=, 5 are near
  =-2= (measured minimum =-1.9999999999999996=, i.e. shared-face nodes'
  solid angles double-counted by two overlapping tiles), and 1 is an exact
  =NaN= (a doubly-coincident corner vertex hitting the Lindholm kernel's
  zero-distance singularity: a genuine =0/0=, not a large-but-finite number).
  A non-coincident control (=dx=30>20=, a real gap between tiles) gives clean
  row sums of =-1.0 +/- 1e-15= for all 98 DOFs. So the legacy 1.2%/2% numbers
  were never a reliable target: they were measured at a spacing the algorithm
  is analytically ill-conditioned at, not merely "a different physical
  quantity". The convergence-with-image-count witness (this section, item 4
  above) and the thin-film analytic limit replace it, per the brief, using
  non-coincident (or self-consistently centred) geometries throughout.
- When the tile spacing exactly equals the sample extent, adjacent-tile boundary
  nodes coincide and the double-layer sum is ill-conditioned (row sums reach -2
  on shared faces, or exactly =NaN= at doubly-coincident corners -- both
  confirmed above; a coarse mesh can then diverge). The robust witnesses avoid
  this by construction (out-of-plane thin film; a centred-cube 1D chain that
  stays well-conditioned; the new =Simulation.add= end-to-end MacroGeometry
  test below uses an explicit non-coincident =dx=). Documented as a method
  property of the finite periodic BEM.

*** Demag2D decision (checklist item 4)

=Demag2D= is DEFERRED by name (Task 29 review item). It does NOT ride the
treecode kernels at all -- it builds an auxiliary extruded 3D mesh via the
legacy =dolfin.MeshEditor= and interpolates =Ms= through legacy
=dolfin.Expression= objects, plus =vertex_to_dof_map= plumbing; none of these
have a cheap DOLFINx equivalent on the current foundations. It is out of scope
for a treecode/PBC slice and its dependency footprint (mesh extrusion +
Expression interpolation) belongs with the deferred mesh/Expression work.

*** =pbc=1d/2d= (Simulation constrained_domain) stays deferred -- separate
capability

=util/pbc2d.py='s =PeriodicBoundary1D/2D= are =dolfin.SubDomain= objects used as
=constrained_domain=periodic function *spaces* (the =Simulation(pbc=...)= path,
for exchange etc.). That is a DIFFERENT mechanism from MacroGeometry demag:
DOLFINx has no =constrained_domain=; periodic spaces require =dolfinx_mpc=
(MultiPointConstraint), which is not in the env. =Simulation(pbc=...)= remains a
by-name deferral (unchanged), and =pbc2d.py= is left dormant (imported only by
the still-deferred LLB physics). The behavioral PBC-demag contract
(=demag_pbc_test.py=) uses =pbc=None= and gets its periodicity entirely from
=MacroGeometry=, so the ported capability is complete without =dolfinx_mpc=.
Registered as a Task 29 candidate.

*** Legacy-lane activation note

Closing the pixi-era treecode skip on the *legacy* FEniCS-2019 lane was NOT done
(the brief allows it only if trivially safe). The legacy =BoundaryMesh=-based
=treecode_bem.py=/=fk_demag_pbc.py= consumers were fully replaced by the DOLFINx
ports, and the legacy Makefile branch does not list =treecode_bem.so=; wiring it
back would mean maintaining a second (dolfin) consumer path. Left as a
=porting_map= follow-up rather than done speculatively.

*** demag_treecode.py

=demag_treecode.py= (the standalone =FastDemag=/=Demag= experiment) is NOT

** Examples conversion (Task 30)

Executed out of audit order (user directive, 2026-07-22), after Task 23.
Purpose: user-level end-to-end proof that the ported package runs real legacy
=examples/= workflows with MINIMAL interface changes and the SAME practical
results. Interface drift discovered here is a first-class deliverable (see the
drift table below), not something to paper over.

The 19 =examples/= subdirectories were inventoried and each script classified
=(a) convertible now= or =(b) requires an unported feature=. Converted scripts
keep their legacy structure; every non-mechanical change is an interface-drift
finding. Each converted script embeds its own validation and is run as a
SUBPROCESS by the new gate =dolfinx-src-examples-pytest= (closest to real
installed-package usage); exit 0 == its validation passed.

*** Controller amendment: from_geofile partially un-deferred

Task 18 deferred =finmag.util.meshes.from_geofile= / =from_csg= (the Netgen
=.geo= -> dolfin-XML path). The user's minimal-diff mandate makes =from_geofile=
*working* a requirement of this slice, so (controller-approved src exception) a
Netgen =algebraic3d= CSG-subset loader was implemented in
=src/finmag/util/geofile.py=, building geometry through the SAME Task 18
OCC/Gmsh machinery (=meshes._mesh_from_geometry=). Example scripts keep their
=from_geofile("*.geo")= lines UNCHANGED. The netgen *binary* backend stays
deferred.

Inventory of Netgen-CSG constructs found across the example =.geo= files, and
whether the loader supports them:

| construct                    | in files                              | supported |
|------------------------------+---------------------------------------+-----------|
| orthobrick                   | std_prob_4, cubic_anisotropy, disp.   | yes (box) |
| sphere                       | demag/sphere1, sphere_fine            | yes       |
| 6x axis-aligned plane (box)  | exchange_demag, nmag_example_2        | yes (box) |
| cylinder (+ capping planes)  | edge_damping/film                     | yes       |
| and / and not (intersect/cut)| edge_damping/film                     | yes       |
| multiple tlo (single-material)| edge_damping/film                    | yes (fused)|
| multitranslate               | dispersion_curves (T25-deferred)      | no (by-name)|
| oblique plane                | (none)                                | no (by-name)|

Notes: multiple =tlo= objects are fused into ONE single-material mesh (subdomain
markers are not preserved -- that multi-region case stays out of the
single-material slice, matching =sphere_inside_box= etc.). Netgen's =cylinder=
is formally infinite; the loader takes the two axis points as the finite extent
and treats axis-aligned capping planes that coincide with those ends as
redundant (the common idiom, e.g. =film.geo=). Unsupported constructs raise
=NotImplementedError= naming the construct (fail-forward). The mesh cache is
content-keyed (md5 of the =.geo= text), mirroring the legacy contract, written
beside the =.geo= as =<md5>.xdmf/.h5= (gitignored). Focused tests live in
=dolfinx-src-meshes-pytest= (=test_from_geofile_*=, 10 cases: per-file analytic
volume checks, the cylinder+plane+not+multi-tlo path, from_csg, cache
round-trip, and the two by-name errors).

*** SRC FIXES beyond the from_geofile exception (flagged for review)

Drift #11 is a genuine ROBUSTNESS BUG, not example drift: =field.py='s
=_owned_vertex_to_dof= matched dof<->vertex coordinates by an EXACT
12-decimal-rounded dictionary lookup. This is bit-exact (and passes) for
structured =dolfinx.mesh.create_box= meshes, but Gmsh/=from_geofile= meshes
carry ~1e-13 coordinate FP noise that can straddle a 12th-decimal rounding
boundary, so =set_alpha= / =set_m= / =m_average= / =relax= crashed with "could
not match DOLFINx dofs to owned mesh vertices" on EVERY generated mesh. Since
=from_geofile= working end-to-end (examples call =m_average=/=relax= on those
meshes) is the point of the controller amendment, this was fixed minimally: a
tolerance-based nearest-vertex match (=scipy.spatial.cKDTree=, tol =1e-9 * mesh
span=), which is exact for bit-exact structured meshes and robust to generator
noise, preserving the distinctness and owned-dof guarantees. This is called out
explicitly for controller/user review as a src change beyond the authorized
=from_geofile= exception.

Fix round 1 (dual-review) hardened =geofile.py= / =field.py= and added committed
tests (field gate 27 -> 29, meshes gate 44 -> 49):
- =geofile.py= =_And.emit= no longer drops cylinder capping planes blindly: each
  dropped plane must be axis-parallel and coincide (tol =1e-9*len=) with a
  cylinder axis endpoint, else it raises =NotImplementedError= naming the
  offset-plane-truncated-cylinder construct (fail-forward, not silent-wrong).
- =from_geofile=/=from_csg= made =maxh= keyword-only so the legacy positional
  =from_geofile(f, False)= binds =save_result= as before.
- CSG keyword matching is now case-insensitive (Netgen semantics).
- Committed field-gate tests assert the =_owned_vertex_to_dof= guard RAISES on a
  distance-violating synthetic input and round-trips on a generated (from_csg)
  mesh (drift #11 regression).

*** "Stale example" note (NOT port drift)

=cubic_anisotropy/hysteresis.py= called =CubicAnisotropy(K1, u1, K2, u2, K3,
u3)=, but the LEGACY pixi-tip class signature is already
=CubicAnisotropy(u1, u2, K1, K2=0, K3=0)= (u3 = cross(u1,u2) computed
internally). The example predated a legacy signature change and was broken
against legacy master too; converted to the actual =(u1, u2, K1, K2, K3)=
signature (the example's explicit u3=(-1,0,0) equals cross(u1,u2), so the
physics is unchanged). This is recorded as a stale-example fix, not port drift.

*** Reduced-resolution / honest-tolerance notes

- exchange_demag runs the Gmsh OCC mesh of =bar30_30_100.geo= at its native
  =maxh=4= (~1733 vertices). The nmag reference was generated on a fine Netgen
  tet mesh, so tolerances are honestly loosened from the legacy values:
  averages 5e-4 -> 1.5e-2 (the legacy per-axis normalisation divides
  the small m_y by ~0.23, inflating its figure to ~1.0e-2; the raw m(t)
  agreement is ~2.4e-3), demag energy density kept at 5e-3
  (measured 3.5e-3), exchange energy density 2e-3 -> 3.5e-2 (measured 2.8e-2;
  the exchange energy density is the resolution-sensitive quantity, while the
  physically important m(t) trajectory and demag energy stay tight).
  The restored per-point energy-DENSITY comparison (=test_compare_energy_density=,
  sampled along the central z-axis via DOLFINx point-in-cell eval, drift #12)
  keeps the legacy EXCHANGE tolerance verbatim (3e-2; measured 2.6e-2); the
  legacy DEMAG density tolerance (1e-2) is exceeded by a hair on this coarser
  mesh (measured 1.005e-2) and is loosened to 1.1e-2, disclosed like the others.
- std_prob_3 gate runs the flower state only at div=8 (the vortex relaxation is
  much slower); flower E_total 0.2985 vs the doc.rst reference 0.302 (Rave) /
  0.305 (Hertel), demag-dominated as documented. The full single-domain-limit
  bisection is FULL-only.
- std_prob_4 gate asserts the average-m_x zero-crossing lands in the physical
  0.10-0.18 ns window rather than the tight 4e-12 band around the Martinez
  0.13949 ns reference, because the Gmsh OCC mesh differs from the legacy Netgen
  tet mesh; FULL-only.
- time-dependent-applied-field: CORRECTED -- no loosening. The sundials factory
  default is used (drift #10 corrected), and the legacy assertion
  =(fittedomega-omega)/omega < 1e-4= passes verbatim (measured rel err ~-1.0e-4).

[Claude Opus 4.8]

** Public field-array component-ordering correction (Task 31)

De-registers the interleaved-ordering interface drift (drift rows #3 and #6 in
the Task 30 table above, now marked CORRECTED). USER-DIRECTED, CONTROLLER
DECISION: every PUBLIC field-array surface restores the legacy
component-blocked ordering =[x1..xN, y1..yN, z1..zN]= -- the
owned-vertex-coordinate-ordered =xxx= view (=xyz.reshape(-1,3).T.reshape(-1)=,
the same ordering =sim.m=/=llg.m= already return). Internal DOLFINx storage
(node-interleaved backend order) is UNCHANGED; only the value returned at each
public boundary changes. This is a CRITICAL-class change: a wrong ordering fix
does not raise, it silently scrambles physics.

*** Surfaces restored to component-blocked ordering

- Every interaction's =compute_field()=: =EnergyBase= box path (Exchange,
  UniaxialAnisotropy, DMI, cubic =assemble=True=), =Zeeman= + the whole time /
  dipolar Zeeman family, =CubicAnisotropy= analytic =assemble=False= path,
  =FKDemag= (and =TreecodeBEM=, inherited), =ThinFilmDemag=.
- =EffectiveField.H_eff= / =compute()= / =compute_jacobian_only()= and
  =sim.effective_field()=: these accumulate interaction =compute_field()=
  outputs, so once each term is blocked the accumulation is blocked with NO
  change to the summation (=output_size= unchanged).
- =Field.get_numpy_array_debug()=: returns the blocked view (legacy dolfin
  local-vector semantic); =set_with_numpy_array_debug()= kept as its exact
  inverse (both blocked), matching legacy's =get_local=/=set_local= pair.

*** One conversion, one place

A single shared helper =finmag.field.owned_raw_to_blocked(functionspace,
raw_array)= performs the raw->blocked conversion. It reuses the canonical
=_owned_vertex_to_dof= coordinate permutation (NOT a naive interleave-transpose)
and is applied at each class's public return point. =EnergyBase= /
=CubicAnisotropy= / =FKDemag= keep an internal =_compute_field_raw()= (raw
node-interleaved) that =average_field()= (an order-invariant nodal mean) and
=FKDemag._load_H_func= consume, because those pair elementwise with the raw
=nodal_volume_S3= weights / write straight into a DOLFINx Function's
backend-order storage. =Zeeman= and =ThinFilmDemag= convert at their single
return.

*** Double-conversion is THE failure mode (removed)

=LLG.solve= previously converted the raw =H_eff= into =xxx= via
=Field.from_array(...).get_ordered_numpy_array_xxx()=; =sundials_jtimes=
did the same for =H_eff= and =compute_jacobian_only=. With those already
blocked, that conversion was REMOVED (=H = self.effective_field.H_eff.reshape
((3,-1))=), otherwise it would double-convert and scramble. Reconstructing a
Function from a blocked array (=EffectiveField.get_dolfin_function=, and the
oracle/probe test rebuilds) must invert the ordering with
=set_with_ordered_numpy_array_xxx=, never the raw =Field(space, array)=
constructor.

*** Anti-scramble net

New dedicated contract gate =test_ordering_contract_dolfinx.py= (4 tests):
component-asymmetric analytic layouts where blocked, interleaved and
double-converted orderings differ element-for-element -- pins the blocked
=compute_field= layout, the blocked =H_eff=, =LLG.solve= consuming =H_eff=
without double-conversion (the exact node-local kernel applied to the intended
uniform (m,H)), and the blocked =get_numpy_array_debug= round-trip. Verified
demonstrably RED against the pre-fix source (3/4 fail, incl. the
double-conversion catch). Full =verify-dolfinx-m5= green end-to-end
(composed-physics + cross-backend + examples). Ordering-sensitive oracle/probe
comparisons updated to convert the now-blocked field to owned-vertex per-node
rows (=reshape((3,-1)).T=) paired with owned-vertex coordinates -- fixture
VALUES untouched, only the conversion applied before comparison. The two
example workarounds were reverted to their legacy one-liners.

[Claude Fable 5]

** CI (Task 32): diagnosis, fixes and trigger scoping

=dolfinx-parity= CI was diagnosed from real =gh run= logs (not guessed), on
the HEAD commit at the time (=3b41190e=) and cross-checked against the same
workflows on the =pixi= branch (all green there as of =ba928093=).

*** What each active workflow proves

- =dolfinx-m5= (pixi =dolfinx= env, native builds, editable install,
  =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5=): the headline gate. Proves every
  =dolfinx-src-*= slice (Field, energies, LLG, restart/output, DMI, cubic
  anisotropy, time-Zeeman, var-params, meshes, SUNDIALS, STT, treecode/PBC
  demag, examples, ~30 named gates) still passes end-to-end against the
  direct-source DOLFINx port. Was already green on =3b41190e=; unchanged in
  behaviour by this task -- only its CI step now tees output to a Job Summary
  (see below) for legibility. No change to =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= itself.
- =dolfinx-m4= (frozen DOLFINx prototype witness): unaffected, still green;
  untouched.
- =python3-m1= / =python3-m2= / =python3-m3= / =python3-core-suite=: the
  M1-M3 legacy-dolfin lanes, gating the =pixi= branch (the "FROZEN LEGACY
  ORACLE", per =docs/superpowers/HANDOVER.md= branch lineage). Now scoped to
  skip on =dolfinx-parity= (see table below) -- see rationale.
- =workflow.yml= / =docker-image.yml=: pre-pixi ad hoc Docker workflows,
  already =workflow_dispatch=-only (never auto-triggered); left as-is with a
  dated comment explaining why, rather than deleted.

*** Why M1/M2/M3/core-suite fail on this branch, and why that is not a bug

Log evidence, all four lanes, same root cause:
- =python3-m2= (run 29899800058): =pixi run barmini-construct= in the legacy
  =default= pixi env raises =ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'dolfinx'=
  importing =finmag.field=.
- =python3-m3= (run 29899799995): pytest collection of
  =comparison/anisotropy/conftest.py= raises the same
  =ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'dolfinx'= via =from finmag.field
  import Field=.
- =python3-core-suite= (run 29899764428): the legacy dolfin-2017 Docker
  container's pytest collection raises =ImportError: No module named
  'dolfinx'= for the same reason.
- =python3-m1= (run 29899800070): the legacy dolfin-2017 Docker container's
  lazy =finmag.example= accessor (=finmag/__init__.py='s =__getattr__=)
  converts the same underlying =ModuleNotFoundError= into
  =AttributeError: module 'finmag' has no attribute 'example'=.

=src/finmag/field.py= now does =from dolfinx import fem, io= unconditionally
(Tasks "Port Field directly to DOLFINx" / "Port common energies directly to
DOLFINx" on this branch) -- an intentional, permanent architectural
commitment of the direct-source port, not a regression to fix forward. The
legacy =default= pixi env and the dolfin-2017 Docker images never install
=dolfinx=, and per the documented branch lineage (=master -> pixi ->
dolfinx-port -> dolfinx-parity=), =pixi= -- not =dolfinx-parity= -- is the
frozen oracle these four lanes exist to gate. Confirmed empirically: all four
lanes are green on =pixi= (e.g. run 27933593829 =python3-m2=, 27933593850
=python3-m3=, 27933593867 =python3-m1=, 27933593832 =python3-core-suite=, all
=success=). Making them pass on =dolfinx-parity= would require giving
=src/finmag= a legacy-dolfin code path again, which is out of scope (and
contrary to the whole point of this branch) -- =src/finmag= was left
untouched by this task, as required.

*** Trigger-scoping table

| Workflow              | Trigger before | Trigger after                                          | Rationale |
|-----------------------+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------+-----------|
| python3-m1            | push, PR, dispatch (unconditional) | same events; job =if:= skips when branch/head is =dolfinx-parity= | Legacy dolfin-2017 Docker lane; =dolfinx= is a hard =src/finmag= dependency on this branch now (see above) |
| python3-m2            | push, PR, dispatch (unconditional) | same, +job =if:= skip on =dolfinx-parity=              | Legacy pixi =default= env lane; same reason |
| python3-m3            | push, PR, dispatch (unconditional) | same, +job =if:= skip on =dolfinx-parity=              | Legacy FEniCS-2019 pytest lane; same reason |
| python3-core-suite    | push, PR, dispatch (unconditional) | same, +job =if:= skip on =dolfinx-parity=              | Legacy dolfin-2017 Docker regression suite; same reason |
| dolfinx-m4            | push, PR, dispatch | unchanged                                              | Frozen prototype, unaffected, still green |
| dolfinx-m5            | push, PR, dispatch | unchanged triggers; CI step now tees + summarizes       | Headline gate; behaviour unchanged, output made legible |
| workflow.yml          | dispatch only  | unchanged (dated comment added)                        | Already manual-only; superseded by the pixi/dolfinx workflows |
| docker-image.yml      | dispatch only  | unchanged (dated comment added)                        | Already manual-only; probes external Docker Hub images unrelated to any branch's changes |

The =if:= condition is
=github.ref_name != 'dolfinx-parity' && github.head_ref != 'dolfinx-parity'=,
which skips the job (shown as "skipped", not silently absent or green) for
both direct pushes to =dolfinx-parity= and pull requests whose head branch is
=dolfinx-parity=, while leaving every other branch (=pixi=, =master=, future
feature branches) running exactly as before. This is scoping, not deletion:
the workflow files, jobs, and full legacy verification logic are all still
present and still run everywhere except this one branch line where they
cannot pass by design.

*** Usefulness upgrade: dolfinx-m5 Job Summary

=.github/workflows/dolfinx-m5.yml= now tees =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5='s
output to a log file (=pipefail= keeps the job's exit code tied to the
verifier, not the tee) and a second, =if: always()= step
(=dev/bin/ci-summarize-dolfinx-m5=, a new additive-only script, not a change
to the verifier) renders a Job Summary: which of the ~30 named
=dolfinx-src-*=/=pixi run -e dolfinx= gates ran and passed, a pass/fail
headline, a log excerpt on failure, and a pointer to
=docs/superpowers/HANDOVER.md= (section 2) for the capability matrix. Purely
additive: =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5='s own tracked-files guard and exit-code
behaviour are unchanged (verified by inspection -- the file was not edited).

** I/O utility parity (Task 26a)

Decision-free subset of the Task 26 long tail: three legacy capabilities
restored with no owner sign-off required, because each is either a faithful
mechanical port of a mechanic already proven elsewhere in this branch, or a
pure analytic transcription of legacy's own formula. HDF5 read-back,
region-restricted field output, and legacy plotting remain deferred (Task 26b).

*** Point evaluation: =Field.probe=/=Field.__call__= (drift #12 CORRECTED)

Legacy (pixi =ba928093=) =Field.__call__=/=Field.probe= were both a one-line
forward to dolfin's own =Function.__call__=: =return self.f(x)=/
=return self.f(coord)=. DOLFINx =fem.Function= objects are not directly
callable at a point, so the Task 30 =examples/exchange_demag/
test_exchange_demag.py= conversion carried a local
=_eval_scalar_function= workaround (bb_tree + compute_colliding_cells +
Function.eval) to sample =energy_density_function()='s raw =Function=
along a line -- recorded as drift #12 in the table below.

Task 26a promotes that exact mechanic into a single shared helper,
=finmag.field.evaluate_at_point(function, point)=, used by both:
- =Field.probe(point)=/=Field.__call__(point)= (restores the legacy
  signature and return shape: a Python =float= for a scalar field, a
  =(value_dim,)= NumPy array for a vector field), and
- directly on any raw =dolfinx.fem.Function= -- e.g. the one
  =EnergyBase.energy_density_function()= returns, matching legacy's own
  contract exactly (legacy's docstring: "return a dolfin function to allow
  probing" -- it deliberately returned the raw dolfin =Function=, not a
  =finmag.Field=, precisely so callers could point-call it directly).

Only a single point per call is supported -- legacy's own call sites (e.g.
=exch_energy([15, 15, i])=, called once per point in a Python =for= loop)
never vectorised over many points either, so this is not a narrowing.
Outside-mesh points raise =RuntimeError= ("point ... is not inside the
mesh"), mirroring legacy dolfin's default (=allow_extrapolation=False=)
failure mode -- grepped the pixi-tip tree; nothing in this codebase set
=allow_extrapolation=True=. Serial-only, consistent with the rest of
=Field='s MPI documentation: a bounding-box-tree point-in-cell search only
sees the calling rank's local (owned+ghost) cells, so it is not collective
and a point owned only by another rank raises even though it exists in the
global mesh; this is a documented-by-name limitation, not separately tested
in parallel.

=examples/exchange_demag/test_exchange_demag.py= REVERTED: the local
=_eval_scalar_function= helper is deleted; the example now calls
=evaluate_at_point= directly, one point per call, in the exact legacy loop
shape (=for i in R: finmag_exch.append(evaluate_at_point(exch_energy,
[15, 15, i]))=). All three of that example's tests still pass
(=test_compare_averages=, =test_compare_energies=,
=test_compare_energy_density=) with the same tolerances as before the
revert -- the numeric results are identical (same underlying eval
mechanic), only the workaround/plumbing changed.

*** Spherical conversion: =Field.get_spherical=

Transcribed verbatim from legacy: =theta = atan2(m_r, m_z)= (the polar angle
from the +z axis, =m_r = sqrt(m_x**2 + m_y**2)= the cylindrical radius) and
=phi = atan2(m_y, m_x)= (the azimuthal angle), both in radians on
=numpy.arctan2='s =(-pi, pi]= branch (=theta= is non-negative since
=m_r >= 0=, so it lies in =[0, pi]=). Legacy assembled
=dot(expr, TestFunction(S1)) * dP= -- dolfin's point measure, which for
Lagrange-1 elements is EXACT node-by-node evaluation (a Dirac comb at the
vertices; each basis function is 1 at its own vertex, 0 elsewhere), not an
L2 projection. The DOLFINx port computes the mathematically identical
result directly as a NumPy expression on the owned nodal array -- same
numbers, no FEM assembly needed to reproduce a pointwise identity.

Returns =(self.theta, self.phi)=, each a raw =dolfinx.fem.Function= on the
CG1 scalar space associated with the field's function space -- matching
legacy's raw =dolfin.Function= attributes exactly (not a =finmag.Field=).
Pinned against 5 analytic points (+z, x, y, -z, and a 45-degree xy vector)
and a normalised-vector round-trip
(=m = (sin(theta)cos(phi), sin(theta)sin(phi), cos(theta))= reconstructs the
original unit vector to 1e-10).

There is no legacy =set_spherical=: the pixi-tip tree was grepped
end-to-end and only =get_spherical= exists (called from
=physics/neb_cartesian_modified.py='s
=theta0, phi0 = self.sim._m_field.get_spherical()=). Nothing was invented
for a surface legacy never had.

*** Topological charge: =Simulation.skyrmion_number=/=skyrmion_number_density_function=

Task 9 had dropped these outright during the initial port (an accepted
judgment-call deferral -- a plain =AttributeError=, not a by-name
=NotImplementedError=; see =dev/dolfinx/porting_map.md=). Task 26a restores
both, transcribing legacy's formula verbatim:

  S = -1/(4*pi) * integral( m . (dm/dx cross dm/dy) )

via =ufl.Dx=/=cross=/=dot= -- the identical UFL API legacy's
=df.Dx=/=cross=/=dot= used, so this is not a formula change. For a 2D mesh
the integral runs over the whole domain; for a 3D mesh (the formula is only
defined for a 2D spin texture) it runs over the TOP surface only
(=z == z_max=), matching legacy's own top-surface restriction (legacy's
docstring cites the FEniCS lift-drag demo for the general "functional over a
mesh subset" technique). Legacy located the top surface with a
=df.SubDomain=/=MeshFunction= marking facets within =df.DOLFIN_EPS=
(~2.22e-16) of the mesh's maximum z coordinate, then integrated over
=df.ds[markers](1)=; the DOLFINx equivalent is
=dolfinx.mesh.locate_entities_boundary= + =meshtags= + a
=ufl.Measure("ds", subdomain_data=...)=. The bare legacy epsilon is
bitwise-exact only for FEniCS's own box-mesh coordinates; DOLFINx's mesh
generators can differ in the last few bits (the same category of FP noise
as the drift #11 =_owned_vertex_to_dof= KDTree tolerance), so a small
mesh-scale-relative tolerance is used instead -- exact for bit-identical top
faces, and never loose enough to pull in an adjacent mesh layer.

=skyrmion_number_density_function= lumped-mass-projects the same integrand
onto the CG1 space associated with =self.S3=, reusing
=_assemble_vector_owned=/=_nodal_volume_owned= from
=finmag.energies.energy_base= (the same unscaled mesh-coordinate lumped
pair =thin_film_demag.py= already reuses for the same reason). Legacy scaled
numerator and denominator by the same =unit_length ** dim= factor, which
cancels exactly, so dropping that scaling changes nothing about the
resulting VALUE. Returns a raw =dolfinx.fem.Function= (matching legacy's raw
=dolfin.Function=, "to allow probing"), not a =finmag.Field=.

Pinned analytically (no legacy oracle fixture needed -- the checks below are
either exact arithmetic or a well-known convergent limit, not a
mesh-specific legacy number to match bit-for-bit):
- a uniform state gives exactly 0 (both the 2D whole-domain and 3D
  top-surface code paths);
- a compactly-supported Bloch-type skyrmion ansatz (radius 20 mesh units,
  core =m_z=-1= at the centre, =m_z=+1= background -- reproduced inline in
  the test as self-contained analytic data, since the legacy
  =initialise_skyrmions= profile lives in the untouched, DOLFINx-unimportable
  =finmag.sim.magnetisation_patterns= module, which still does
  =import dolfin= at module scope and is off the core import graph) converges
  towards the analytic +/-1 topological charge as the mesh is refined:
  measured n=20 -> 0.8668, n=40 -> 0.9651, n=80 -> 0.9912;
- the 3D top-surface integration path reproduces the 2D whole-domain result
  exactly (to 1e-8) for a z-invariant profile with a matching xy
  footprint/resolution;
- the density function, weighted by nodal volume and summed, reproduces
  =skyrmion_number()= to ~1e-13 (both derive from the same lumped machinery,
  so this is an internal-consistency check, not an independent validation).

=initialise_skyrmions=/the other =magnetisation_patterns= initialisers were
NOT restored -- explicitly out of scope for 26a (26b territory), left
untouched/unimportable as before.

18 tests) folded into =dev/bin/verify-dolfinx-m5= right after
=dolfinx-src-examples-pytest=.

** P2.2 =sim_with= MacroGeometry wiring (2026-07-23)

Commit =2cf4647f= (parent =f97d4088=) wires =nx=/=ny=/=spacing_x=/=spacing_y=
through =sim_with= to the already-ported dense-FK =MacroGeometry= path.
Two follow-up commits, =571df59f= and =899d3906=, complete a witness
correction and close a guard gap respectively. No demag-algorithm change: no
Treecode factory selector, GCR or Demag2D work.

=sim_with(nx=, ny=, spacing_x=, spacing_y=)= now forwards its four arguments
unchanged into =MacroGeometry(nx=nx, ny=ny, dx=spacing_x, dy=spacing_y)=,
matching the legacy contract exactly (=git show
b5015c5a:src/finmag/sim/sim.py=, lines 1443-1447): no transformation, no
=unit_length= scaling. =spacing_x=/=spacing_y= are the tile PITCH --
centre-to-centre translation of the image lattice -- in mesh coordinate
units, not a gap; the docstring now states this explicitly (the legacy
wording was ambiguous). Legacy quirks are preserved: =nx = nx or 1=, and
even/non-positive tile counts raise from =MacroGeometry='s own validation
(=ValueError=, narrowing legacy's bare =Exception= with the same message).

A =MacroGeometry= is built only when at least one of the four arguments is
given (divergence 1 from legacy, which built one unconditionally whenever a
demag solver was requested); the plain default keeps routing through the
dense array BEM rather than the native treecode kernels. This is numerically
inert -- the 1x1 image lattice agrees with plain FK demag to 1.005e-15
relative -- and it preserves the import boundary (no =finmag.native.*= import
for a demag-free or default simulation).

*** The touching/overlapping guard (divergence 2)

The legacy default when =spacing_*= was omitted meant "the tiles touch": the
image lattice pitch defaults to the mesh bounding-box extent. A new guard,
=_reject_touching_macro_geometry=, refuses this by name, because the ported
periodic BEM assembly does not handle the resulting coincident tile boundary
nodes -- the solid angle is double counted (row sums reach =-2= instead of
=-1=), giving a ~158% error on a cube, and on a flat slab the matrix acquires
non-finite entries so the phi_2 solve fails with =KSP_DIVERGED_NANORINF= and
the returned field is silently =-M=.

=899d3906= hardens the guard after finding that it originally rejected only
the exact-equality case (=pitch == extent=). A pitch strictly *below* the
extent (=pitch < extent=, interpenetrating tiles) is equally broken -- the row
sums diverge further from =-1= (maxdev =1.0= at =0.9*extent=, i.e. row sums
=-2=; =2.0= at =0.5*extent=, i.e. row sums =-3=) -- but slipped past the
original guard and returned a silently wrong field. The TRUE validity
condition is =pitch > extent=: the ported periodic BEM is correct only when
neighbouring image tiles have a positive gap. The guard now rejects
=effective <= extent * (1 + 1e-9)= on each active axis (a one-sided
tolerance, so a genuinely larger gap like =extent * (1 + 1e-6)= is still
accepted), raising =NotImplementedError= naming the axis, the requested
pitch, the mesh extent, and suggesting =extent * (1 + 1e-6)=. Fixing the
underlying BEM kernel is a demag-algorithm change and stays out of scope for
this slice; =sim_with= refuses the configuration by name instead of exposing
a silently wrong field.

*** P2.2a: an invalid PBC witness, and its completion

Before this slice's wiring landed, =f97d4088= found and corrected a false
positive in the PBC physics test suite:
=test_pbc_out_of_plane_thin_film_analytic_limit= used the *touching*
=MacroGeometry= default (tile pitch == mesh extent) on a 40x40x2 slab and
asserted =|Nz - 1| < 1e-3=. It passed for entirely the wrong reason: that
configuration assembles a periodic BEM with 6 non-finite entries, the phi_2
Krylov solve then fails with =KSP_DIVERGED_NANORINF= (reason -9, 0
iterations), phi_2 stays identically zero, and =H = -grad(phi_1) = -M=
exactly -- which satisfies =|Nz - 1| < 1e-3= trivially (measured =2.085e-10=)
while the very same configuration also reports the unphysical =Nx =
1.000000= for in-plane =m=, the opposite of the correct limit =Nx -> 0=.

=f97d4088= replaced it, test-only:

- =test_pbc_out_of_plane_thin_film_analytic_limit= now uses a non-coincident
  tile pitch (=extent * (1 + 1e-6)=, a =1e-6= relative gap, physically
  indistinguishable from a continuous film), asserts BEM finiteness and the
  BEM row-sum identity (=sum_j B_ij == -1=) as explicit preconditions, and
  asserts monotonic approach to =Nz = 1= over =nx=ny = 1, 3, 5= (=0.709851 ->
  0.983976 -> 0.990758=) rather than an absolute =Nz ~ 1=.
- =test_pbc_in_plane_thin_film_demag_factor_tends_to_zero= is added as a
  companion falsifier: the failed-solve signature =H = -M= shows up there as
  =Nx = 1=, the opposite limit, so the degenerate answer that made the old
  test green cannot satisfy it (measured =0.054464 -> 0.007920 -> 0.004588=).
- =test_pbc_coincident_tile_spacing_produces_a_non_finite_bem= pins the
  underlying defect directly: touching tiles give a non-finite BEM on a
  slab, and on a cube (where entries stay finite) the row sums reach =-2=
  instead of =-1=, i.e. the solid angle is double counted.
- The touching-tile expectation itself is kept as a strict
  =xfail(strict=True)= so it stays visible and stops being counted as
  passing; fixing the coincident-node handling will make this file fail
  loudly.

No demag algorithm was changed and no tolerance was loosened to make a
broken configuration look green. The coincident-node fix is out of scope:
the native kernel does have an explicit coincident branch
(=solid_angle_single_reduced=, =native/src/treecode_bem/common.c:151-183=,
so the case was intended to be supported at some point), but fixing it is a
demag-algorithm change.

=571df59f= completes this correction: two more tests,
=test_pbc_image_sum_converges_1d= and =test_pbc_end_to_end_runs=, were found
still building =MacroGeometry= with no spacing -- the same touching default.
Both now use the established =extent * (1 + 1e-6)= pitch and the same
finiteness + row-sum precondition, verified RED beforehand: at the touching
pitch the image-sum precondition fails with row sums =-2= and the end-to-end
precondition fails with a non-finite slab BEM. The image-sum sequence is
re-measured on the correct gapped geometry: =nx = 1, 3, 5, 9 -> -0.3337,
-0.0638, -0.0245, -0.0078=, still monotonically decreasing with shrinking
increments and =nx=1= at the cube =1/3=. The stale "plateau near -0.1007"
comment in this test was a broken-path number; the gapped sequence does not
plateau there -- extended offline to =nx = 51..351= it decays toward 0
(=-0.00025, -0.00006, -0.00003, -0.00002, -0.00001, -0.00001=). The comment
is corrected accordingly.

- Cross-geometry equivalence (the legacy =demag_pbc_test.py= acceptance
  check): a 3x-tiled 20nm cube (=nx=3, spacing_x=20.001=) reproduces a
  directly-meshed 60x20x20nm bar to =0.08%= in Hx (in-plane) and =0.011%= in
  Hz (out-of-plane), against the legacy acceptance bounds =1%=/=2%=.
- Thin-film analytic limits in BOTH directions at the gapped pitch
  =extent * (1 + 1e-6)=: =Nz = 0.709851 -> 0.983976 -> 0.990758= and
  =Nx = 0.054464 -> 0.007920 -> 0.004588= for =nx=ny = 1, 3, 5=, with
  monotonic approach plus BEM-finiteness/row-sum preconditions. Review
  confirmed the gap-limit is continuous from the right (=Nz -> ~0.9908= as
  gap =-> 0+=), so the =1e-6= gap is real physics, not an artefact.
- =nx=1, ny=1= reduces to plain FK demag to =1.005e-15= relative (=<
  1e-12=).
- The corrected gapped 1D image-sum sequence (=nx = 1, 3, 5, 9=): =-0.3337,
  -0.0638, -0.0245, -0.0078=, decaying toward 0, replacing the broken-path
  "plateau near -0.1007".

The exactly-touching AND overlapping (=pitch <= extent=) configurations are
numerically broken (BEM row sums =-2= at touching/=0.9*extent=, =-3= at
=0.5*extent=; non-finite on a flat slab) and are refused by =sim_with= with a
by-name =NotImplementedError= telling the user to pass a pitch strictly
larger than the mesh extent (e.g. =extent * (1 + 1e-6)=). The underlying
coincident-node BEM defect is NOT fixed -- that is a demag-algorithm change,
an explicit non-goal here -- it is pinned by
=test_pbc_coincident_tile_spacing_produces_a_non_finite_bem= and the strict
=xfail=.

*** Independent review

Verdict: APPROVE WITH FOLLOW-UPS. The touching-case diagnosis, the honesty
of the replacement witnesses, the cross-geometry witness, exactness,
mutation-resistance and scope were all independently verified.

1. Finding 1: a second touching-default witness existed
   (=test_pbc_image_sum_converges_1d=/=test_pbc_end_to_end_runs=). Acted on
   in =571df59f=.
2. Finding 2: the guard rejected only the exact-touching pitch, not
   overlapping pitches (=pitch < extent=). Acted on in =899d3906=.
3. Finding 3: the guard over-rejects a vanishingly small ~5e-10 gap band
   that would technically work. Left as benign conservatism, deliberately
   -- the error message steers users to the established =1e-6= gap rather
   than a fragile near-threshold value.

*** New acceptance-register decision

D17 in =docs/superpowers/acceptance-register.md= records this as a
deliberate divergence needing an owner decision: legacy computed a
touching/overlapping macro-geometry demag by default; this port refuses
=pitch <= extent= by name. Disposition: *pending owner decision*.

[Claude Sonnet 5]
